# Munram Tiruvandhadi

## 1. Tirukkanden

*tirukkaṇḍēn ponmēni kaṇḍēn tigaḻum arukkan aṇi nirṟamum kaṇḍēn serukkiḷarum ponnāḻi kaṇḍēn purisangam kaikkaṇḍēn ennāḻi vaṇṇan pāḷ inṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

inṟu – now (on the day when the other two mudal āḻvārs made him see); en āḻi vaṇṇan pāl – in (the divine form of) emperumān who has the complexion of ocean; tiru – periya pirāttiyār (śrī mahālakśmi); kaṇḍēn – I had the fortune to worship; pon mēni – the beautiful divine form; kaṇḍēn – I had the fortune to worship; tigaḻum – shining; arukkan – like sun; aṇi – beautiful; niṟamum – resplendent radiance \[of the divine form of emperumān\]; kaṇḍēn – I had the fortune to worship; seru – in the battlefield; kiḷarum – bursting out; pon āḻi – beautiful divine disc; kai – in the (right) hand; kaṇḍēn – I had the fortune to worship; puri sangam – (pāncajanyam) divine conch curling towards right (in the other divine hand); kaṇḍēn – I had the fortune to worship

**Перевод:**

**tirukkaṇḍēn – while everything is contained in saying “I saw emperumān”, due to excessive involvement, āḻvār says that “I saw this”, “I saw that” like people who have attained great things.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*While āḻvār had seen everything in the divine form of emperumān who has all cit (sentient) and acit (insentient) entities as his body, due to overflowing affection, he says that “I have seen this”, “I have seen that” among some of emperumān’s personal possessions and speaks about that experience in the first pāsuram. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**tirukkaṇḍēn** – I saw [pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) who is the cause for all wealth. I saw pirātti who wants those who attain her to realise their natural characteristics. Even when emperumān was running behind indra as his younger brother (during his incarnation as upēndra, also called as vāmana), since indra did not have the blessings of pirātti, all his wealth disappeared. Just as pirātti’s *puruśakāram* (recommendatory role) is required during devotion, even while seeing [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), the first to be seen is only pirātti. Is it not true of the saying “*yatōpāsanam palam*” (benefit is proportional to depth of devotion)! As per tatkratu nyāyam, devotion cannot be something and benefit, something else. Both will be identical. Just as it is mentioned in [iraṇḍām tiruvandādi 56](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/11/irandam-thiruvandhadhi-56/) “*karumālaip ponmēni kāttāmun kāttum tirumalai nangaḷ tiru*”, even to have direct darśan (viewing) of emperumān, it is pirātti who carries out *puruśakāram*! Even for jñānam (knowledge) it is only her *puruśakāram* which is the reason, as mentioned in [mudal tiruvandādi 67](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/08/mudhal-thiruvandhadhi-67/) “*tāmariyāḷ kēḷvan oruvanaiyē nōkkum uṇarvu*” (knowledge will attain only emperumān who is the consort of mahālakśmi who dwells on lotus).

**tirukkaṇḍēn**  – I saw pirātti who is the identity for establishing [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). One can see sun only after seeing its rays! īsn’t pirātti the resplendent rays for emperumān, the sun! In śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 21-16 sītā says “*ananyā rāghavēṇāham bhāskarēṇa prabhā yatā*” (just as rays do not leave sun, I do not leave śrī rāma) and in 121-15 śrī rāma says “*ananyāhi mayā sītā bhāskarēṇa prabhā yatā*” (just as rays do not leave sun, sītā does not leave me)!

**ponmēni kaṇḍēn** – I saw the complexion of emperumān, after seeing which even [pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) will be greatly involved. Hasn’t it been mentioned in manu smriti 12-122 “*rukmābham svapnadī gamyam*” (he has a golden hue complexion; he can be discerned through dream-like vision of the mind)! The greatness of his divine form is mentioned.

**tigaḻum arukkan aṇi niṟamum kaṇḍēn** – due to the union of perumāḷ and pirātti, I saw a hue like that of nascent sun. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) saying this is similar to what has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 38-15 “*śōbayan daṇḍakāraṇyam dīptēna svēna tējasā adruśyata tatō rāmō bālacandra ivōdita:*” (decorating the forests of daṇḍakāraṇyam with his radiance, śrī rāma was seen like a nascent sun which had just then arisen). The hues of perumāḷ and pirātti appear mixed together.

**tigaḻum arukkan aṇi niṟamum kaṇḍēn serukkiḷarum ponnāḻi kaṇḍēn** –  *tiruvāḻi* (divine disc), looking at the union of perumāḷ and pirātti gets fearful for their safety, where he should not get fearful and gets ready to burst out on those who see their union \[with evil eyes\].

**ponnāḻi** – the divine disc has such a beauty that people will have affection for him. I saw the divine disc on which one should sing praise similar to how [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) had sung in [tiruppallāṇḍu 2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruppallandu-2-adiyomodum/) “*vadivār* *sōdi valattuṟaiyum sudarāḻiyum pallāṇḍu* ”

**puri sangam kaikkaṇḍēn** – unlike tivāḻi āḻvān (divine disc), the divine conch will not burst out fiercely on enemies, but will keep looking all over the place for enemies so that it could pounce on them. Just as śrī guhapperumāḷ (the boatman in śrī rāmāyaṇam) cast doubts on iḷaiyaperumāḷ (lakśmaṇa) as he was the brother of bharata who had sent śrī rāma to the forest, pāncajanyam would doubt the divine disc also. I saw such conch in the divine hand of emperumān. ōr, we could also consider conch as being humble, like iḷaiyaperumāl, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 4-42 “*prājñalim prahvamāsīnam*” (one who stood humbly with joined palms).

**en āḻi vaṇṇan pāl inṟu** – towards emperumān who is like my wealth, who has a complexion like that of ocean which removes one’s fatigue, one who cannot be estimated.

**en āḻi vaṇṇan** – emperumān is just like [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/)’s ocean. āren’t [pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/), divine conch and rubies present in the ocean! In the same way, aren’t they with emperumān too! tirumangai āḻvār also has mercifully mentioned in his periya tirumoḻi 2-8-5 “*māmaṇiyum malarmēl mangaiyum sangamum tanguginṟa alaikadal pōnṟivar*” (emperumān is like the ocean which has the great gems, pirātti who resides on a flower and conch).

**inṟu** – today, when [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) decided to show himself to [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/). We can also construe this as āḻvār saying that he got to see emperumān today with the recommendatory role played by pirātti.

We shall take up the 2<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 2. Inre kalal kanden

*inṟē kaḻal kaṇḍēn ēḻ piṟappum yān aṟuttēn pon tōy varai mārbil pūnduḻāy anṟu tirukkaṇḍu koṇḍa tirumālē unnai marukkaṇḍu koṇḍu en manam*

**Пословный перевод:**

pon tōy – having golden ornaments; varai – being like a mountain; mārbil – on the divine chest; pūm tuḻāy – having divine tuḷasi garland; anṟu – during that time when the ocean was churned; tiru – periya pirāttiyār (śrī mahālakśmi); kaṇḍu kōnda – having been enjoyed; tirumālē – Oh, the consort of lakshmi!; en manam – my mind; unnai – with you; maruk kaṇḍu koṇḍu – being fully united (attained you); yān – adiyēn (the servitor, I); inṟē – today itself; kaḻal – your divine feet; kaṇḍēn – experienced, seeing; ēḻ piṟappum – all births; aṟuttēn – severed (so that they do not follow)

**Перевод:**

**inṟē kaḻal kaṇḍēn – I saw the divine feet. I severed all my births \[in future\]. Seeing the divine feet and continuing births will not be congruent. It is not that I had seen the divine feet at one point of time and severed my births at another point. The moment I saw the divine feet, I destroyed my enemy \[cycle of births\]. It has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 8-4 “druśtēnaiva hi na: śōkam apanēśyati rāghava:” (śrī rāma is one who removes our sorrow by merely looking at us) and “adya mē hi gataśōka: ” (my sorrow left me today).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that as soon as he saw emperumān, all his enemies disappeared. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**inṟē kaḻal kaṇḍēn** – I became fully involved in kainkaryam \[service to emperumān\].

**ēḻ piṟappum yān aṟuttēn** – thus I became one with all hurdles removed. I got to serve emperumān and I was also rid of samsāram (materialistic realm).

**pon tōy varai mārvil** – in the divine chest which looks like a mountain dipped in gold

**pūnduḻāy anṟu tiruk kaṇḍu koṇḍa** – he stood such that pirātti saw the garland of tuḷasi on his divine shoulder.

**anṟu tirukkaṇḍu koṇḍa** – on that day when the ocean was churned for getting nectar, śrī mahālakśmi stepped on to the divine chest of emperumān as mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam “*paśyatām sarvadēvānām yayau vakśastalam harē:*” (even as the dēvas were witnessing, (śrī mahālakśmi) stepped on the divine chest (of emperumān)). Oh tirumāl (emperumān) who has such a divine chest!

**tirumālē** – this indicates how emperumān is infatuated with pirātti.

**unnai marukkoṇḍu koṇḍen manam** – even after knowing that emperumān is being liked by pirātti, my mind, without stepping back, went ahead and was fully involved with him. Is it that I have the same extent of affection as pirātti has for him and I saw him? Wasn’t “not shunning him” all that I had! Instead of gaining a benefit from emperumān and leaving him, my mind stayed with him totally. For those who do not know the relationship between emperumān and pirātti, that ignorance is the reason for their fear. For those who know this relationship, that we are not qualified becomes the reason for not leaving him.

We will take up the 3<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 3. Manattullan

*manattuḷḷān mākadal nīruḷḷan malarāḷ tanattuḷḷān tanduḻāy mārvan sinattuc cerunarugac ceṟṟuganda tēngōdavaṇṇan varunaragam tīrkkum marundu*

**Пословный перевод:**

mā kadal nīr uḷḷān – one who is resting on the huge tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean); malarāḷ tanattu uḷḷān – one who is on the bosom of pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi); taṇ tuḻāy mārban – one who is donning the cool, tuḷasi garland on his chest; serunar – enemies; uga – to be destroyed; sinattu – through his anger; seṟṟu – destroying; uganda – one who was happy; tēngu ōdam vaṇṇan – with the complexion of a full ocean; varu naragam tīrkkum marundu – sarvēśvaran is like the medicine which will cure the disease of the unavoidable samsāram (life in the materialistic realm); uḷḷān – he is living in my heart

**Перевод:**

**manattu uḷḷān – the most desired place for emperumān is āḻvār’s mind. He made āḻvār’s mind as his mattress and entered it.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram how āḻvār’s mind gravitated had towards emperumān was mentioned. In this pāsuram, it is mentioned that emperumān remains ignorant of others, except āḻvār. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**mā kadal nīr uḷḷān** – this is the place he prepared himself in order to enter āḻvār’s mind. He reclined in tiruppāṟkadal in order to protect his followers.

**malarāḷ tanattu uḷḷān** – just as he made tiruppāṟkadal as his resting place, he also considered pirātti’s bosom as his resting place. Is he not as mentioned in [tiruppāvai 20](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/01/thiruppavai-20-muppaththu-muvar/) “*nappinnai kongai mēl vaittuk kidanda malar mārban*” (one who rested on the bosom of nappinnai (nīḷā dēvi))! It is nappinnai who helps him to go back to [periya pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/). We can also consider the verse *malarāḷ tanattuḷḷān* to indicate his wealth. From all these it is considered that what [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) said in his periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 5-2-10 is apt “*aravattamiḷiyinōdum aḻagiya pāṟkadalōdum aravindappāvaiyum tānum agampadi vandu pugundu* ” (emperumān entered āḻvār’s heart with his serpent mattress, the milky ocean and his consort).

**malarāḷ tanattuḷḷān** – the place where his lifeline is located is pirātti’s bosom.

**taṇḍuḻāy mārvan** – the place where pirātti’s lifeline is located is emperumān’s chest. What emperumān carries out in her bosom, pirātti carries out with his garland on the divine shoulder. He has such garland on his divine shoulder.

**sinattuc cerunar ugac ceṟṟu** – with his anger, annihilating his enemies; alternatively, we can add the anger to his enemies and consider this verse to mean that he destroys his enemies who are angry. Destroying his enemies like this is an activity to make pirātti happy. As mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 30-39 “*tam druśtvā śatruhantāram maharśīṇām sukhāvaham babhūva hruśtā vaidēhi bhartāram pariśasvajē* ” (after seeing śrī rāma who destroyed the enemies and made the great sages happy, sitāp pirātti realised that she existed; she was very happy and she embraced her husband well), did not sītā become happy after knowing that the enemies of great sages have been destroyed and embrace her husband!

**uganda** – emperumān was happy that he had destroyed the enemies of his followers.

**tēngōda vaṇṇan** – he has the complexion of the deep ocean. Thus, he has the form which removes his followers’ fatigue. This beauty came about due to his destroying the enemies.

**varunaragam** – Just as it is mentioned in brahmavaivartam, prakruti kāṇḍam 26-70 “*avaśyamanubhōktavyam krutam karma śubāśubham*” (one has to necessarily experience the results of one’s pāpa (sinful deeds) and puṇya (virtuous deeds)), it is the unavoidable life in this materialistic realm which is termed as *naragam* (hell).

**tīrkkum marundu** – he is the medicine for crossing over this hell which is impossible to cross (otherwise). It is emperumān’s characteristic to be the *vaidya* (physician). Just as one says that such and such person is dark complexioned \[his characteristic\]. īsn’t our mind the reason for such an entity to enter our mind! Has it not been mentioned in [śrī bhagavat gītā 6-5](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/6-5/) “*ātmaiva ātmanō bandu: ātmaivai ripurātmana:*” (mind is ātmā’s friend; mind is ātmā’s enemy)! He is inside the mind to remove the impending hell. He entered the mind to sever the connection with samsāram.

We shall move on to the 4<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

## 4. Marundum porulum

*marundum poruḷum amudamum tānē tirundiya sengaṇmāl āngē porundiyum ninṟulagam uṇḍumiḻndum nīrēṟṟu mūvadiyāl anṟulagam tāyōnadi*

**Пословный перевод:**

tirundiya – with exactness (knowing clearly that he is the supreme being); sem kaṇ – having lotus-like divine eyes; māl – with affection; āngē – in that only activity of protecting the world; ninṟu porundiya – being firmly suited (to that); ulagam – all the worlds; uṇḍu – swallowing (so that deluge will not destroy them); umiḻndu – later, spitting them out; anṟu – once, in an earlier time; nīr ēṟṟu – taking water from mahābali as a symbol of taking alms from him; mū adiyāl ulagam tāyōn – emperumān who measured all the worlds with his three steps; adi tanē – only his divine feet; marundum – are like medicine to cure the disease of samsāram (life in this materialistic realm); poruḷum – like wealth, capable of obtaining what is desired; amudamum – being sweet like nectar

**Перевод:**

**marundum poruḷum amudamum tane – the term marundu (medicine) refers to its ability to destroy enemies (since medicine cures diseases) , the term poruḷ refers to the means (wealth) for obtaining the medicine and the term amudam refers to the benefit realised. It is only emperumān who removes our enemies, gives what we need and allows himself to be enjoyed by us. He removes those which we do not want and gives what we want. Alternatively, since medicine cures the disease and gives strength, the term marundu refers to the means and the terms poruḷ and amudam refer to the two states of benefits (i.e. as wealth it is the means for obtaining other needed things and it is enjoyed as the benefit, like gold). viṣṇu darmam 72-4 says “amrutam sādanam sādyam yam paśyanti manīśiṇa:, jñēyākhyam paramātmānam viṣṇum dyāyan na sīdati ” (one never suffers any sorrow, if he meditates on viṣṇu, the supreme being, who is seen by wise people as the indestructible means and who is attained). Hasn’t brahmā too mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 117-17 “śaraṇyam śaraṇanca tvāmāhur divyā maharśaya:” (the sacred sages state that you are the means and the benefit)! In trivikrama avatāram (incarnation) since he removed enemies such as namuci, he is the medicine; since he came as a mendicant, he became the wealth too; since he kept his divine feet on everyone’s head, he became the nectar (benefit) too. Thus, divine feet of emperumān who measured all the worlds are everything. It is not samittu pādi sāvitri pādi (this is an old saying which refers to carrying out a ritual where one keeps a count of the mantra that one recites using samittu, a type of stick. One should have 1008 sticks and recite the gāyatri mantram 1008 times, without short-changing in either). emperumān is everything, unlike the above-mentioned saying.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that it is the protector of all, emperumān’s divine feet which are to be attained and which help in attaining. In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār mentioned about how he was protected. In this pāsuram, he is mentioning about protecting everyone. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**tirundiya sengaṇ māl** – he has reddish eyes which reflect that he is the supreme being. Alternatively,

**tirundiya** – he is the final goal as well as the means. He is as per his svarūpam (basic nature). ōr, if the ātmās (souls) do not change their ways, he changes his ways to align himself with them. ōr, just as ātmās do not ever correct themselves, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) will always be exact (without any error).

**sengaṇ** – he has reddish eyes to reflect his affection for his followers.

**māl** – one who is affectionate.

**āngē porundiyum ninṟu** – instead of feeling ashamed that the consort of mahālakśmi has come to ask for alms, he stands firmly in the act of taking alms.

**porundiyum** – being firm in protecting

**ninṟu** – his divine mind standing firmly in that

**ulagam uṇḍu umiḻndum** – protecting the worlds by swallowing them and letting them out in due course

**nīṟeṟṟu** – he took alms as if he is obtaining something which did not belong to him.

**nīrēṟṟum** – he obtained something which he could not have attained otherwise

**mūvadiyāl anṟu ulagam tāyōnadi** – it is the divine feet of one who measured the three worlds after taking water \[from mahābali as symbol of taking alms\] which are medicine, wealth and nectar.

We can also consider **uṇḍu** as referring to medicine, **umiḻndu** as wealth and **sengaṇmāl** as nectar.

We shall take up the 5<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 5. Adivannam tamarai

*adivaṇṇam tāmarai anṟulagam tāyōn padivanṇṇam pārkkadal nīr vaṇṇam mudivaṇṇam ōrāḻiveyyōn oḷiyum ahdanṟē ārāḻi koṇḍāṟkaḻagu*

**Пословный перевод:**

anṟu ulagam tāyōn – on that day (when mahābali poured water on vāmana’s palm), when emperumān measured all the worlds, his; adi vaṇṇam – the colour of his divine feet; tāmarai – is reddish like the lotus; padi vaṇṇam – the colour of his divine form; par kadal nīṟ vaṇṇam – is dark like the ocean, surrounding earth; mudi vaṇṇam – the colour of his crown; ōr āḻi veyyon – the colour of sun who travels on a chariot with one wheel and who has harsh rays; oḷiyum – radiance (of that emperumān); ahdanṟē – is also like that sun; Ar āḻi koṇḍāṟku aḻagu – the beauty of such emperumān holding the beautiful divine disc, is like this

**Перевод:**

**adi vaṇṇam tāmarai – simile for the divine feet is lotus.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Having mentioned about the way he measured the worlds, āḻvār loses himself to emperumān’s beauty and enjoys that beauty. āḻvār says, “Since emperumān has become medicine, aiśvaryam and nectar, let me enjoy his beauty” While describing emperumān’s beauty, recourse is taken to compare with worldly materials more out of bewilderment than any other reason. āḻvār says that once one thinks of emperumān’s beauty, it is impossible not to speak; it is also impossibleto speak. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**anṟu ulagam tāyōn padi vaṇṇam pār kadal nīr vaṇṇam** – the colour of divine form of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who measured the worlds, is that of the ocean which surrounds the world. If his complexion were white, one could say that it is like that of *pāṟkadal* (the milky ocean). But since his complexion is dark, it is mentioned as *pār kadal nīr vaṇṇam*.

**mudi vaṇṇam ōrāḻi veyyōn** – the colour of emperumān’s crown is similar to sun.

**ōrāḻi veyyōn** – this could be construed either as sun who has chariot with single wheel or as sun’s galaxy.

**oḷiyum ahdanṟē**– [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s radiance is also like that of sun.

**Ar āḻi koṇḍāṟku** – emperumān who has such a beauty that if he holds the divine disc in his hand, he doesn’t need any other ornament to be adorned with. Beauty for one who is holding the beautiful disc in his hand. In the same way, we can construe the meanings for *adivaṇṇam tāmarai* etc.

We shall take up the 6<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 6. Alaganre aliyarku

*aḻaganṟē āḻiyāṟku āḻi nīr vaṇṇam aḻaganṟē aṇḍam kadattal aḻaganṟē angai nīrēṟṟāṟku alarmēlōn kāl kaḻuva gangai nīr kānṟa kaḻal*

**Пословный перевод:**

āḻiyāṟku – for the supreme being who is holding the divine disc; āḻi nīr vaṇṇam – the complexion of ocean; aḻagu anṟē – is it not beauty personified!; aṇḍam kadattal – the activity of measuring the worlds; aḻagu anṟē – is it not beauty personified!; am kai – in the beautiful hand; nīr ēṟṟāṟku – for emperumān who accepted water \[from mahābali as symbolic of accepting alms\]; alar mēlōn – brahmā, who is atop lotus; kāl kaḻuva – when he washed the divine feet; gangai nīr – the pure water of gangā; kānṟa – one which exhibited; kaḻal – that divine foot; aḻagu anṟē – is it not beauty personified!

**Перевод:**

**aḻaganṟē āḻiyāṟku āḻi nīr vaṇṇam – For emperumān, is it only the complexion to have the colour of ocean? Is it not beauty also! For the term āḻiyāṟku we can consider the meaning as – one who is having the divine disc. Then the meaning will change as – the colour of emperumān will be contrasting to that of disc. Thus, just like it is for disc, the complexion of emperumān will also be great. For such a person, is there a need for any other ornament!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In this pāsuram, āḻvār enjoys the beauty of emperumān as mentioned in the previous pāsuram and his divine activities. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**aḻaganṟē aṇḍam kadattal** – it is not only the complexion, even the activity is beautiful. Did he carry out the difficult to achieve tasks with lot of effort?

**aḻaganṟē** – isn’t the activity of measuring all the worlds, beautiful too! Hasn’t [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) too mercifully stated in his [tiruvāimoḻi 2-2-11](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/02/thiruvaimozhi-2-2-11-eththa-ezhulagum/) “*ētta ēḻulagum koṇḍa kōlakkūttanaī*” (praising [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who measured the worlds beautifully) (*kōlakkūttan* – one who carries out an activity beautifully).

**aḻaganṟē angai nīr ēṟṟāṟku** – isn’t [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s posture even as he took the water as symbolic of alms, from mahābali in his beautiful hands, so beautiful!

**angai nīr ēṟṟāṟku** – for one who had all along been only giving to others, taking alms from someone else, as mentioned by [āṇḍāḷ](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/andal/) in nācciyār tirumoḻi 11-5 “*pollāk kuṟaḷuruvāyp poṟkaiyil nīr ēṟṟu*” (taking water in his golden hand, mischievously, as a dwarf) even the hand was described as golden (*poṟkai*).

**alarmēlōn kāl kaḻuva** – brahmā washed the divine foot of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)

**gangai nīr kānṟa kaḻal aḻaganṟē** – are the divine feet only responsible for creating an abundance of gangā water? Were they not beautiful! The divine feet are not only sacred; they are sweet too! [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) says that they are not meant for exhibiting an amazing activity or for manifesting their greatness. Are they not meant for exhibiting beauty!

We shall move on to the 7<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 7. Kalal toludum

*kaḻal toḻudum vā nenjē kārkkadal nīr vēlai poḻil aḷanda puḷḷūrdic celvan eḻil aḷandangu eṇṇaṟkariyānai epporutkum sēyānai naṇṇaṟkariyānai nām*

**Пословный перевод:**

nenjē – Oh mind!; kār kadal nīr – having black coloured sea-water; vēlai – having shore; poḻil – earth; aḷanda – one who measured \[the earth\]; puḷ ūrdi – one who has garuda as his vehicle; selvan – one who has śrīdēvi (śrī mahālakśmi), the goddess of wealth \[as his consort\]; eḻil aḷandu eṇṇarkariyānai – one whose boundary for beauty cannot even be thought of; epporutkum sēyānai – one who is far away from any object; naṇṇaṟku ariyānai – emperumān, who cannot be approached (by anyone through his/her efforts); kaḻal – divine feet; nām toḻudum – let us worship; vā – you also agree and come

**Перевод:**

**kaḻal toḻudum vā nenjē – Oh heart! Let us worship the divine feet of emperumān. You also join with me.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Losing out to the beauty of emperumān, āḻvār tells his divine mind “come, let us carry out kainkaryam (service) at his divine feet and sustain ourselves”. He tells his divine mind to enjoy the divine beauty of emperumān when it is in such unlimited way. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kār kadal nīr vēlaip poḻil aḷanda** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) had measured the world which has the shore of black coloured ocean. He had measured the world even when we had not requested him.  Hence the only work for us is to worship him; he will take care of the activities which are to be performed later.

**puḷ ūrdic celvan** – he has garuda as his vehicle and has unlimited wealth.

**eḻil aḷandu angu eṇṇaṟku ariyānai** – he is such that no one can analyse and estimate his beauty. He cannot be estimated through the mind either.

**epporutkum sēyānai** – he is far removed from (at a higher plane than) all the objects . He is greater than all, irrespective of whether they are elders or youngsters. Alternatively he is greater than all objects.”*adruśya:*” (cannot be seen). [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has said in [śrī bhagavat gītā 7-25](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/7-25/) “*nāham prakāśa: sarvasya yōgamāyāsamāvruta: mūdō’yam nābhijānāti lōkō māmajamavyayam*” (since I am hidden by the illusion of having taken a human form, I do not appear to be radiant (like paramātmā) to many people in the world. The worldly people, due to their ignorance, do not analyse and know that I am the causative factor without any birth due to *karma* (past deeds) or that I am the supreme being without any fault). He is impossible to attain for anyone through self-efforts.

**nām kaḻal toḻudum vā nenjē** – he tells his heart to come with him and worship [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine feet as emperumān has displayed himself on his own volition.

We shall take up the 8<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 8. Namam pala solli

*nāmam pala solli nārāyanā enṟu nāmangaiyāl toḻudum nannejē vā maruvi maṇṇulagam uṇḍumiḻnda vaṇḍaṟaiyum tanduḻāyk kaṇṇanaiyē kāṇga nangaṇ*

**Пословный перевод:**

nannenjē – Oh dear mind (which is trying to go ahead of me); pala nāmam solli – reciting many divine names \[of emperumān\]; nārāyaṇā enṟu – reciting the most important divine name of them all, nārāyaṇa; am kaiyāl – with beautiful hands; nām toḻudum – let us worship; maruvi vā – agree to this and come; maṇ ulagam – worlds such as earth; uṇḍu umiḻnda – one who swallowed during deluge and spat out during creation; vaṇḍu aṟaiyum taṇ tuḻāy – one who has cool tuḷasi garlands in which beetles sit and raise a humming sound; kaṇṇanaiyē – only kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa); nam kaṇ – our eyes; kāṇga – let them see and enjoy

**Перевод:**

**nāmam pala solli – one should recite only one divine name for getting a specific benefit. But if no benefit is sought, cannot all divine names be recited! Just as one picks a sugar candy that one can lay the hands on and puts it into the mouth, reciting all the divine names without expecting any benefit.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār wants all his sensory perceptions to enjoy emperumān. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nārāyaṇā enṟu** – reciting [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s unique divine name; knowing the connection between him and us; being firmly involved in it; reciting the name of the one who has *nārā* (souls) as his dwelling place and remains as a simple entity.

**nenjē** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) invites his heart to enjoy [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) through sensory perceptions such as speech etc.

**nāmam pala solli** – just as a person with unstable mind would start eating fruits at random if he enters a banana grove, reciting the divine names of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) with full voice.

**nārāyaṇā enṟu** – reciting the divine name *nārāyaṇa* which is the primary divine name

**nām angaiyāl toḻudum** – we will worship him with the hands which are given only to worship him. With the hands which have been given to carry out service to him, we will carry out activities such as *anjali* (saluting with joined palms) etc which cause delight in him.

**nal nenjē** – aren’t you \[the heart\] the root for all sensory organs! āren’t you the apt one if we have to worship [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)! Oh heart, you are ahead of me in engaging in this activity!

**vā maruvi** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) tells his heart to come and approach emperumān without taking a step back.

**maṇṇulagam uṇḍu umiḻnda** – anticipating impending deluge, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) protects the worlds in his stomach and lets them out once creation resumes.

**vaṇḍaṟaiyum taṇ tuḻāyk kaṇṇanaiyē** – only kaṇṇa (kriṣṇa) who is sweet and very easy to approach. Even if he does not have the quality of protecting all the worlds, he is so sweet that we cannot let go of him; even if he cannot carry out rare activities to protect us, he has such beauty that we cannot leave him.

**kāṇga nam kaṇ** – let our eyes see him with desire.

We shall take up the 9<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 9. Kannum kamalam

*kaṇṇum kamalam kamalamē kaittalamum maṇṇaḷanda pādamum maṟṟavaiyē eṇṇil karumāmugil vaṇṇan kārkkadal nīrvaṇṇan tirumāmaṇivaṇṇan tēsu*

**Пословный перевод:**

karumā mugil vaṇṇan – one who has the form of an expansive, black cloud; kārkkadal nīr vaṇṇan – with the complexion of black coloured water; tirumā maṇivaṇṇan – emperumān with the colour of beautiful, expensive gemstone; tēsu – beauty; eṇṇil – if one were to think of; kaṇṇum – the divine eyes; kamalam – are like lotus flower; kaittalamum – the divine hands; kamalamē – are also like lotus flower; maṟṟu – moreover; maṇ aḷanda pādamum – the divine feet which measured the world; avaiyē – are also like that lotus flower

**Перевод:**

**kaṇṇum kamalam – As mentioned in jitantē stōtram 1 “jitam tē puṇḍarīkākśa” (Oh emperumān with lotus flower-like eyes! We have been won over by you), his eyes will win us over and we will have to say the aforementioned line with trembling body. His divine eyes will first establish contact with us due to his grace.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*After āḻvār said “kāṇga nam kaṇ” in the previous pāsuram, emperumān shows his beauty to āḻvār and he enjoys that beauty. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kamalamē kaittalamum** – next, the hands will touch us. When we lose out to the divine eyes and fall at his divine feet, vouldn’t the divine hands lift us and embrace us!

**maṇ aḷanda pādamum maṟṟavaiyē** – the divine feet are the ones that we fall on to, after losing to the beauty of his divine eyes and after being touched by his divine hands.

**maṇ aḷanda pādamum maṟṟaviayē** – the lotus-like divine feet were the ones which measured the world which is full of forests and streams \[referring to the ups and downs on the surface which would have hurt [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s soft divine feet\].

**eṇṇil** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) says that we cannot delve into this. His beauty will be such that it will not allow us to delve into it. It will bewilder us. Even if we take efforts to delve….

**karumā mugil vaṇṇan** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has the form of great, dark coloured cloud.

**kārkkadal nīr vaṇṇan** – he has the complexion of the waters of dark coloured ocean.

**tirumā maṇivaṇṇan** – he has he colour of a highly radiant, very expensive gemstone. It is a very great gemstone (belonging only to him). Since there is nothing which can be considered as matching his characteristics, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) is picking up a few things here and there to explain his characteristics, similar to picking grains. In other places, the simile will be higher than the object which was meant to be explained in the first place. But in the case of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), the similes will always be insignificant compared to him.

**karumā mugil vaṇṇan** – this refers to the ability of his form to remove the fatigue of those who have the fortune to see him.

**kārkkadal nīr vaṇṇan** – this refers to the ocean which cannot be estimated by the eyes.

**tirumā maṇivaṇṇan** – refers to being totally cool and comforting.

**tēsu** – total beauty. Refers to samudāya śōbhai – beauty taken for the entire divine form instead of speaking about individual divine parts.

We shall move on to the 10<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 10. Tesum tiralum

*tēsum tiṟalum tiruvum uruvamum māsil kudippiṟappum maṟṟavaiyum pēsil valam purinda vānsangam koṇḍān pērōda nalam purindu senṟadaiyum nangu*

**Пословный перевод:**

valam purinda vān sangam koṇḍān – emperumān who is having the divine white coloured conch which is curling towards the right; pēr ōda – as his divine names are recited; pēsil – if (the advantages due to that are) spoken about; tēsum – radiance; tiṟalum – the ability to win over others; tiruvum – wealth; uruvamum – beautiful form; māsu il kudi piṟappum – birth in a clan which is free from any faults; maṟṟavaiyum – and other benefits; nalam purindu nangu senṟu adaiyum – will come on their own, with desire

**Перевод:**

**tēsum – Lot of  tējas; in other words, esteem or true nature.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*When asked “When we experience emperumān who is without any shortcoming in his beauty, we have to be appropriate for that. Hence should we not try and attain some benefits and then enjoy him?”. āḻvār says “When you enjoy emperumān falling head over heels, all the benefits will come on their own”. Is it enough if we speak only about the benefits that he has, do we not need to attain some? āḻvār says, as we start reciting his divine names, all the wealth will come to us on their own in order to retain their true characteristics. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**tiṟalum** – strength; ability to defeat others.

**tiruvum** – inseparable wealth.

**uruvamum** – beauty of the form

**māsil kudippiṟappum** – being born in a faultless clan – similar to the birth of [sītāppirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) in janaka maharāja’s great clan. Has it not been mentioned mercifully in periya tirumoḻi 1-1-9 that reciting [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine name will lead to being born in a great clan “*kulam tarum*”! Just as it has been mentioned in śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 1-9-1 “*sa: kuleena:*” (the one who has been graced by pirātti will be in a great clan) only that person who recites emperumān’s divine names will be considered to be in a great clan. Even if it is a person like guhan, who is a hunter or someone like vibhīśaṇa, who is a demon, doesn’t emperumān consider such persons as part of ikśvāku clan!

**maṟṟum** – what has not been stated

**pēsil** – if what has not been spoken of so far were to be spoken. It is not possible to state the benefits of reciting the divine names. Still, if it were to be spoken of…

**valam purinda vān sangam koṇḍān pērōda** – reciting the divine names of the entity who is holding the white coloured śrī pāncajanyam which is curved towards the right side.

**nalam purindu senṟadaiyum nangu** – it will come in some way or the  other, even if it had to crawl. All that is required for him (cētana, sentient entity) is to recite the divine names of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). All these (benefits mentioned above) will come on their own. Just as he cannot sustain himself without reciting the divine names, these cannot sustain themselves without reaching him. Benefits which are mentioned in śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 1-9-131 “*sa ślājñas sa guṇī danya: sa: kuleena: sa buddimān*” (he is apt to be praised, he has auspicious qualities, he is fortunate, he is from a high clan and is knowledgeable) and as mentioned in śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 1-9-125 “*śarīrārōjñam aiśvaryam aripakśa kśayas sukham*” (health, wealth, destruction of enemies and comfort) – all these will come on their own.

We shall move on to the 11<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 11. Nangodum

*nangōdum nālvēdattuḷḷān naṟaviriyum pongōdaruvip punalvaṇṇan sangōdap pāṟkadalān pāmbaṇaiyin mēlān payinṟuraippār nūṟkadalān nuṇṇaṟivinān*

**Пословный перевод:**

nangu ōdum nāl vēdattu uḷḷān – he is spoken of by the four vēdas (sacred texts) which are faultless; naṟavu iruyum – with sweetness which will beat honey; pongu ōdam aruvi punal – like an ocean and like the water from a stream; vaṇṇan – having divine form; sangu ōdam pāl kadalān – one who is reclining on the milky ocean with waves having conces; pāmbu aṇaiyin mēlān – one who is resting on śēśa śayanam (ādiśēśan mattress); payinṟu uraippār nūl kadalān – emperumān who is spoken of by śāstras (sacred texts) which are recited and explained by vaidikas (followers of sacred texts); nuṇ aṟivinān – not known (by those who try through their efforts to know him)

**Перевод:**

**nangōdum nāl vēdattu uḷḷān – emperumān has been established by the four vēdas (sacred texts) which have been learnt properly and sustained in the mind. He is established by vēdas which have sub-parts and which been studied by observing various religious practices such as fasting, continence etc. Just as emperumān himself has mentioned in śrī bhagavat gītā 15-15 “vēdaiśca sarvairahamēva vēdya:” (only I am known through the vēdas), he is present in all the four vēdas. emperumān is the meaning for all the words spoken in the vēdas, both direct and subtle meanings. nangōdum would also mean vēdas speaking explicitly about emperumān’s auspicious qualities.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that other than those with sharp knowledge, it is not possible to know about emperumān. When his divine names are recited, he may reveal himself, but for the others he cannot be known. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**naṟa viriyum pongōda aruvip punal vaṇṇan** – he has the complexion of ocean having lapping waves and like a stream of water. By splitting the first two words as *naṟavu iriyum* we can say that emperumān has sweetness bettering even honey. He has a divine body which, as mentioned in cāndōjña upaniśat 3-14-2 “*sarva gandas sarvarasa:*” (he has all the sweet fragrances and sweet tastes), is sweet.

**sangōdap pāṟkadalān** – he reclines in the *tiruppāṟkadal* (milky ocean) where the waves bring forth conces which keep blowing resoundingly. Alternative explanation – he is reclining in milky ocean which has conces and waves. This implies that emperumān who is known only through vēdas is now closer to samsāris (people living in materialistic realm) \[since he has descended from śrīvaikuṇṭam to tiruppāṟkadal\].

**pāmbaṇaiyin mēlān** – He reclines on the soft mattress of ādiśēśan so that his divine form will not be pricked by the ocean water. He is *anantaśāyi* (one who reclines on ādiśēśan). Alternatively, we can say that in order not to give up his followers, he came to *tiruppāṟkadal*, which is closer to this world.

**payinṟu uraippār nūṟkadalān nuṇ aṟivinān** – he is established well by learned people who are experts in sacred texts such as *smruti, itihāsam, purāṇam* etc. Alternatively, he is established by *itihāsa* and *puṟāṇas* through people who speak with total involvement in these texts.

**nuṇ aṟivinān** – he is such that only people with sharp intellect will know him. He cannot be known by those who try to know him through their own efforts.

We shall take up the 12<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 12. Arivennum tal

*aṟivennum tāḷ koḷuvi aimpulanum tammil seṟivennum tiṇ kadavam semmi maṟai enṟum nangōdi nanguṇarvār kāṇbarē nādōrum paingōda vaṇṇan padi*

**Пословный перевод:**

maṟai – vēdam (sacred text); enṟum – without a break; nangu ōdi – practicing well; aṟivu ennum – knowledge (of ātmā, soul and paramātmā, supreme being); tāḷ koḷuvi – latching; aimpulanum – the five sensory perceptions; tammil seṟivu ennum – keeping them under control in their respective matter; tiṇ kadavam semmi – closing them with a strong door; nangu uṇarvār – those who meditate well; paingōda vaṇṇan padi – the characteristics of emperumān who has the complexion of green ocean; nāḷ dōṟum – every day; kāṇbar – will be able to see

**Перевод:**

**aṟivu ennum tāḷ koḷuvi – ensuring that the mind does not stray into matters other than emperumān by bolting firmly with the latch of knowledge. Leading it (mind) instead towards firm knowledge on emperumān.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*When āḻvār was asked “Does that mean that no one can know about emperumān and see him?”, he responds saying “he can be seen through the method that I am going to say”. He says in this pāsuram that it is very difficult to know him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**aimpulanaum tammil seṟivu ennum tiṇ kadavam semmi** – keeping the sensory perceptions from straying into worldly matters and ensuring that they stay in their respective position by locking them up within the strong door. Ensuring that they do not go out by locking the door – the firm door in which mind and sensory perceptions are linked together. This refers to how the sensory organs are controlled.

**maṟai enṟum nangōdi** – constantly reciting the vēdas which, by nature, do only good to others, and sustaining this way. Just as it is mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-6-2 “*yōgāt* *svādyāya māmanēt*” (through meditation, attaining learning), if one were able to practice meditation, the union of pramāṇam (authentic source of information, here it refers to vēdas) and pramēyam (one who is indicated by the vēdas, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) should be experienced.

**nangu uṇarvār kāṇbarē** – those who follow this way will be able to see \[emperumān\] always. Those who know well, follow these steps: *śravaṇam* (hearing), *mananam* (thinking), *nididyāsanam* (meditating), *upāsanam* (carrying out upāsanā or devotion), *druvānusmruti* (meditating continuously), *darśanasamānakāratā* (appearing like visualising directly) and *pratyakśāpatti* (seeing with the eyes) \[in matter relating to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\].

Who do they see?

**paingōda vaṇṇan padi** – characteristics of one who has the complexion of green coloured ocean; one who has the divine form which removes fatigue. Such people (who know, following the steps mentioned above) will see every day. Just as it is mentioned of nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam) in rig aśtakam 1-2-7 “*sadā paśyanti sūraya:*” (nityasūris see [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) constantly), these people will also be able to see all the time.

Alternative explanation:

**nangōdi nangu uṇarvār** – Just as it is mentioned in harivamśam baviśya 89-9 “*ōmityēva sadā viprā: patatadyāda kēśavam*” (Oh brāhmaṇas! Always recite *Om*. meditate on kēśava), reciting constantly. **nangu uṇarvār** – those who recite in the proper way.

We shall take up the 13<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 13. Padivattat tamarai

*padivattat tāmarai paṇḍulagam nīr ēṟṟu adivattattāl aḷappa nīṇḍa mudivattam āgayamūdaṟuttu aṇḍam pōy nīṇḍadē māgāyamāy ninṟa māṟku*

**Пословный перевод:**

paṇḍu – in an earlier time; ulagam nīrēṟṟu – obtaining earth as gift (from mahābali); tāmarai padi vattam – earth which is like a blossomed lotus, round in shape; adi vattattāl aḷappa – to measure with the divine feet which are like involuted lotus; mā kāyamāy ninṟa māṟku – for emperumān who stood with a huge form (as trivikrama); nīṇḍa mudivattam – the long crown; āgāyam ūdaṟuttu – piercing the sky (and the outer worlds); aṇḍam pōy nīṇḍadē – did they not grow until the walls of the universe! How amazing is this!

**Перевод:**

**padi vattat tāmarai – earth looks like the circular shaped, blossomed lotus. śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 2-12-37 says “padmākāra samudbhūtā” (earth which came in the form of a lotus). āḻvār mercifully describes his experience of seeing the divine activity of emperumān …**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous two pāsurams, it was mentioned that it is very difficult to know emperumān. In this pāsuram, āḻvār says that one can see emperumān only if he decides to show himself to his followers. Another interpretation – āḻvār mercifully mentions about how he is enjoying the activities of emperumān who is a very difficult entity to know. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**padi vattat tāmarai** – the greatness which resulted from emperumān keeping his divine feet.

**paṇḍu ulagam nīr ēṟṟu** – once upon a time, obtaining the world which was in the form of circular lotus.

**adi vattattāl aḷappa** – when [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) measured the worlds  with his divine feet which were like circular lotus

**nīṇḍa mudivattam** – the crown which had grown long. *mudivattam* refers to the radiance of his divine crown.

**āgāyam ūdaṟuttu** – piercing the sky

**aṇḍam pōy nīṇḍadē** – it grew until it reached the walls of the universe. For whom did this grow?

**mā kāyamāy ninṟa māṟku** – for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who had a great \[huge\] divine form, with a longing to manifest his affection for the world. His longing to grow was not limited to this. He wanted to grow even huger. But since there vasn’t enough space to grow, it appeared that he had to restrict himself to this form.

We shall take up the 14<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 14. Marpal manam

*māṟpāl manam suḻippa mangaiyar tōḷ kai vittu nūṟpāl manam vaikka noyvidām nāṟpāla vēdattān vēngadattān viṇṇōr mudi tōyum pādattān pādam paṇindu*

**Пословный перевод:**

nāṟpāla vēdattān – being mentioned by the four vēdas (rig, yajur, sāma and atarvaṇa); vēngadattān – standing in tirumalai as simplicity personified; viṇṇōr mudi tōyum pādattān – emperumān who has divine feet on which nityasūrs’ crowns would unite; pādam – divine feet; paṇindu – vorśipping; māl pāl – towards that emperumān; manam suḻippa – for the heart to be engaged; mangaiyar tōḷ kaivittu – getting rid of the desire to embrace the shoulders of women; nūl pāl – in śāstras (such as vēdam etc); manam vaikka – to engage the mind; noyvidu Am – is very easy

**Перевод:**

**māṟpāl manam suḻippa – the reason for getting involved with worldly pursuits is because they are seen. In the same way, since emperumān manifests himself, being unable to forgo him, one should get involved with him and let go of the involvement in other matters. It is impossible to get involved with emperumān after leaving desire in the shoulders of women. Hence, once desire develops towards emperumān, it will be better to let go of the desire in the shoulders of women.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*As mentioned in the 12th pāsuram “aṟivennum tāḷ koḷuvi”, being victorious over sensory perceptions is a very difficult task indeed. Unless \[emperumān is\] seen in person, it is not possible to defeat sensory perceptions. But this is not possible \[seeing him\]. Thus, for those who are asking “don’t we have a way to enjoy emperumān?”, āḻvār mentions about a way which can be followed even by people without strength. emperumān has taken residence in tirumalai to overcome the shortcoming of being difficult to be seen. āḻvār says that one can attain his divine feet and see him. Has it not been mercifully mentioned by tirumangai āḻvār in tirukkuṟundāṇḍagam 19 “kaṇḍiyūr arangam meyyam kacci pēr mallai enṟu maṇḍinār uyyalallāl maṟṟaiyārkku uyyalāme” (one cannot sustain oneself without attaining arcāvatāra emperumān (emperumān in deity form in various divine abodes)) and in tirukkuṟundāṇḍagam 18 “vidiyil kāṇbār meymmaiyē kāṇgirpārē” (could those who try to see him on their own efforts really see him?)! āḻvār says that divine abodes are there to control sensory perceptions and to sever us from various worldly pursuits. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**māṟpāl manam suḻippa** – if the word *māl* is taken to mean one who is great, we can consider this as our heart getting engaged with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). ōr, if *māl* is taken to mean one who is affectionate, we can consider this as our heart engaging with one who is affectionate towards us.

**māṟpāl manam suḻippa** – if *māl* is taken to mean one who is totally infatuated, this will mean that we should be fully infatuated with one who is infatuated with us.

**mangaiyar tōḷ kai vittu** – getting rid of the connection with all other matters \[worldly pursuits\]. Getting rid of the desire with the shoulders of women, which can land one in *naragam* (hell).

**nūṟpāl manam vaikka noyvidām** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) says that keeping just a tiny bit of interest in emperumān is sufficient to make it easy to attain emperumān. It will be easy to keep the mind towards *nūṟpāl* (authentic texts). ōr, it will be easy to keep the mind towards [tirumantram](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2015/12/rahasya-thrayam.html) (reciting the name of nārāyaṇa in a specific way) which is the essence of all vēdas (sacred texts). It can also be considered that one can get engaged with emperumān after ridding of the desire in worldly pursuits.

**nāṟpāla vēdattān** – one who is described vividly by the four vēdas.

If he is to be known only through vēdas, will it not be difficult to see him for those who are seeing only worldly pursuits?

**vēngadattān** – he is standing in tiruvēngada malai (the divine hills of tirumalai) so that he can be enjoyed through eyes \[for everyone\]. In that case, is he also one among the worldly pursuits?

**viṇṇōr mudi tōyum pādattān** – one who is standing like this is desired by nityasūris too. His divine feet are such that even nityasūris, who have absolutely no connection with materialistic realm, will desire and attain him here. Even though [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) lowers himself for the sake of *samsāris* and takes residence in tirumalai, his divine feet retain their greatness such that nityasūris will come here and bow down to him.

**pādam paṇindu** – holding on to his divine feet.

We will take up the 15<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 15. Panindu uyarnda

*paṇinduyarnda pauvap padutiraigaḷ mōda paṇinda paṇamaṇigaḷālē aṇinda angu anantan aṇaik kidakkum ammān adiyēn manam tan aṇaikkidakkum vandu*

**Пословный перевод:**

paṇindu uyarnda – agitating by rising and falling; pauvam padutiraigaḷ – waves which form in the ocean; mōda – striking on all sides; paṇinda – (ensuring that the droplets from those waves do not fall on the divine form, like an umbrella) bowed down; paṇam – formed by the hoods; maṇigaḷālē – by the carbuncles; aṇinda – decorated; anantan – tiruvantāḻvān (ādiśēśan); aṇai – on the divine mattress; ammān – supreme being; angu – there; vandu – reaching \[from there\]; adiyēn than – in my; manam aṇai – the mattress of my mind; kidakkum – reclined

**Перевод:**

**paṇindu uyarnda pauvap padu tiraigaḷ mōda – being struck by the agitating waves which are formed in the ocean and which rise and fall \[keeping with high and low tides\]. Even if they are only droplets, to āḻvār they appear like mountains falling on the soft divine form of emperumān and hence he says mōda (striking).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*When asked “How did you get to see such a great supreme being and enjoy him?”, āḻvār responds saying “emperumān entered my heart without any reason and reclined; thus I saw him”. He says that he, who was selected by emperumān, has sorrow \[suffered by others in samsāram\]. emperumān himself came to āḻvār by practicing yōga. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings.*

**paṇinda paṇa maṇigaḷālē** – to ensure that such droplets do not fall on emperumān’s divine form, ādiśēśan has lowered his hoods \[and holding them like an umbrella\].

**aṇinda angu anantan aṇai** – reclining on such mattress of anantāḻvān whose hoods are decorated with rubies.

**kidakkum ammān** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is resting

**adiyēn manam than aṇaik kidakkum vandu** – leaving that mattress, emperumān has reached my mind and is reclining on that as his mattress. emperumān left the mattress of ādiśēśan who has lot of affection for him and rested in my mind which does not have affection for him nor is it like a mattress for him. Leaving aside those who are affectionate towards him, emperumān will come on his own into the hearts of those who, even if they do not have affection, realise that he is the lord and we are the servitors. Has it not been divinely mentioned in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 5-4-8 “*anantan pālum garudan pālum aidu noydāga vaittu en manandanuḷḷē vandu vaigi vāḻacceydāy empirān*” (Oh great benefactor! Keeping only a tiny bit of affection for tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan) and garudāḻvān (garuda), you entered my heart, fitting well, and blessed me to live happily) and in 5-4-9 “*panikkadalil paḷḷigōḷaip paḻagavittu ōdivandu en manakkadalalil vāḻavalla māya maṇāḷa nambi*” (Oh one with amazing powers, who is the consort of śrī mahālakśmi and one who is complete with auspicious qualities! Forgetting your habit of taking residence in tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean), you left that place and took residence in my heart which became an ocean for you)! He will rest on ādiśēśan’s lap, considering that to be his apt place. But he will also rest happily in my mind itself. Where does he reside \[normally\] and where has he come to reside \[now\]! [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is seeking out my mind like a person who has no bed to sleep on and is coming in search of a bed! Just as when the people of ayōdyā surrounded the place where śrī rāma was staying \[citrakūtam) to take him back to ayōdyā, śrī rāma went into the forest, asking riśis for a place, as said in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 5-34 “*āvāsam tvahamiccāmi*” (I am looking for a place to say), emperumān made my mind as his dwelling place.

We shall take up the 16<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 16. Vandudaitta

*vandu udaitta veṇṭhiraigaḷ sembavaḷa veṇmuttam andi viḷakkum aṇiviḷakkām endai oruvallitt tāmaraiyāḷ onṟiya sīr mārvan tiruvallikkēṇiyān senṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

oru alli tāmaraiyāḷ – periya pirāttiyār (śrī mahālakśmi) who has the incomparable lotus flower which has beautiful petals as her dwelling place; senṟu onṟiya sīr mārvan – one who has beautiful divine heart in which pirātti fitted nicely; veṇṭhiraigaḷ vandu udaitta sem pavaḷam oṇ muttam – red corals and white pearls which were brought out in an agitated way by the white complexioned waves; andi viḷakkum aṇi viḷakkām – manifesting the evening time through auspicious lamps; tiruvallikkēṇiyān – emperumān who is dwelling in the divine abode of tiruvallikkēṇi; endai – my lord

**Перевод:**

**vandudaitta veṇ tiraigaḷ – being pushed by the white coloured waves which keep kicking in**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār mercifully mentions about the way in between when emperumān left tiruppāṟkadal and entered his divine mind. In the previous pāsuram āḻvār had said manandanaik kidakkum vandu – he would take rest in my mind). He says now that the reason for doing like this is pirātti’s connection with emperumān (it is only because of pirātti’s puruśakāram (recommendatory role) that emperumān entered my mind). Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**sembavaḷa veṇmuttam** – reddish corals and cool pearls

**andi viḷakkum aṇi viḷakkām** – having bright lamps during the period of dusk

**endai** – my leader

**oru allit tāmaraiyāḷ** – periya pirāttiyār (śrī mahālakśmi) who is having unlimited sweetness. Just as it is mentioned in periya tirumadal “*ōr inniḷa vanjikkodi*” (incomparable, sweet, tender creeper), pirātti, being like a creeper, cannot sustain herself unless she is with a supporting pole. Hence, she attained [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is like her supporting pole. Just as emperumān is the *uttaman* (best) among men, [pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) is the *uttamai* (best) among women.

**tāmaraiyāḷ onṟiya sīr mārvan** – emperumān became superior to everyone else only because pirātti liked him. śrī viṣṇu purāṇam says “*paśyatām sarvadēvānām yayau vakśastalam hare:* “ (even as all the celestial entities were watching, tirumagaḷ (śrī mahālakśmi) stepped on to the divine chest of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)). Here, the usage *paśyatām dēvānām* refers to 6<sup>th</sup> case in samskruta grammar. Just as it is said of 6<sup>th</sup> case “*anādarē śaśti*” (6<sup>th</sup> case is used in places where there is a sense of indifference), [pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) climbed on to the divine chest of emperumān even as celestial entities were watching, not bothering about them. She climbed on to the divine chest of emperumān just as a mother climbs on to the mattress of her husband even as the children are seeing. śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-9-106 says “*dayā’valōkitā dēvā*:*….parām nirvrutimāgatā*:*.*” (the celestial entities who were graced by her attained supreme bliss). After she climbed on to emperumān’s divine chest, realising what emperumān thought of in his divine mind “look at their troubles”, pirātti graced the celestial entities. Why did she not grace them in the first place? This is because she had to first realise her *svarūpam* (basic nature) and only then could she grace others. She could grace others only after knowing the divine mind of emperumān! Just as it is mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 131-80 “*pradēhi subhakē hāram yasya tuśtāsi bhāminī*” (Oh auspicious person! Give this necklace to whoever you feel happy with) sītā gave the necklace to hanuman after knowing śrī rāma’s thoughts and words! Since [pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) had got separated from śrī rāma, first she had to get his grace and only after that could hanuman get her grace. vibhīśaṇa got her grace first before getting śrī rāma’s grace. śrī guhapperumāḷ (boatman guha) got both [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s and pirātti’s grace at the same time. Just as it is mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 9-2-1 “*nin tiruvaruḷum pangayattāḷ tiruvaruḷum koṇḍu*” (having both your and pirātti’s divine grace).

**tiruvallikkēṇiyān endai** – my lord who has taken residence in tiruvallikkēṇi.

**endai** – he considered being my lord as his fortune, a fortune which he could not have got easily!

We will take up the 17<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 17. Senra nal

*senṟa nāḷ sellāda sengaṇmāl engaḷmāl enṟanāḷ ennāḷum nāḷāgum enṟum iṟavāda endai iṇaiyadikkē āḷāy maṟavādu vāḻttuga en vāy*

**Пословный перевод:**

sengaṇmāl engaḷmāl enṟa nāḷ – if there is a day when it is said “the lotus eyed kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) is very affectionate towards us”; senṟa nāḷ – the days which have passed \[before that day\]; sellāda (nāḷ) – days which will come hereafter; ennāḷum – at all times; nāḷ āgum – will be very good days only; enṟum iṟavāda endai – emperumān who does not have an end ever; iṇai adikkē – to the two divine feet; āḷ āy – to be subservient; maṟavādu – without forgetting; en vāy vāḻttuga – my mouth should praise

**Перевод:**

**sengaṇmāl – since the lotus eyed emperumān is together with pirātti there is no hurdle to getting the benefit. āḻvār says that his tongue should henceforth be ready to enjoy him continuously. We can also construe this to mean that āḻvār is telling us to be thankful to emperumān whose quality is like this \[to uplift the followers\].**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that once a person realises that such emperumān is very affectionate towards us, the days before that realisation as well as days which come after such realisation would all be very beneficial, without any difference. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**sengaṇmāl** – supreme kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) who is lotus eyed. The reason for being lotus eyed is his being the consort of pirātti.

**enṟa nāḷ** – if we get the knowledge one day that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is affectionate towards us

(then)

**senṟa nāḷ sellāda nāḷ ennāḷum nāḷāgum** – the days which have passed and the days which are going to come, will all be good days only. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) says in the same manner by which akrūra said in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-17-3, after seeing kriṣṇa “*adyamē sapalam janma suprabhātā ca mē niśā yadunnidrābhjapatrākśam viṣṇōr drākśāmyaham mukam*” (Today itself I achieved the purpose of my birth. Today itself my night dawned into a good day. Did I not get to see the divine face of śri viṣṇu who has eyes similar to the petals of blossomed lotus!). How can the days which were spent wastefully (without knowing about emperumān) be good days? Since the earlier days were spent appropriately, in knowing about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) now, they are good days only. Weren’t the past days spent in such a way as to say “*sengaṇmāl engaḷ māl*” today! Hence they are also good days only. The days to come will also be spent in experiencing emperumān and hence they too will be good. The days to come will be spent in recalling the thoughts that we are having about emperumān now!

**ennāḷum** – the days being spent now are also good days only since these are spent with thoughts about emperumān. There is no shortcoming in this.

**enṟum maṟvādē** – only activity to be carried out now is to ensure that no fault accrues to this henceforth.In other words, no hurdle comes to this experience.

ōr, if the text is

**enṟum iṟavāda endai** – the one who is forever, without any end, at the divine feet of that father. In śrīviṣṇusahasranāmādyāyam, it is said “*bhūtānām ya: avyaya: pitā*” (one who is indestructible father to jīvātmās, the sentient souls).

**endai** – one who is the leader. He is appropriate for us.

**iṇaiyadikkē** – at the two divine feet which are equivalent to each other

**āḷāy** – as a servitor

**maṟavādu** – without forgetting

**vāḻttuga en vāy** – my mouth should keep praising.

**maṟavādu vāḻttuga** – the following pramāṇams (authentic texts) show what will happen if we forget [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and if we think of him without forgetting: a) taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli 6 “*asannēva sa bhavati asat brahmēti vēda cēt asti brahmēti cēt vēda santamēnam tatō vidu:*” (if he does not have knowledge about brahmam (emperumān) he does not exist; if he has knowledge of brahmam, he is considered to exist by people only because of that reason. b) gāruda purāṇam 222-22 “*yanmuhūrtam kśaṇam vāpi vāsudēvō na cintyatē sā hāni tanmahaccidram sā bhrāntis sā ca vikriya*” (if in a muhūrttam (a time frame of 48 minutes) or in a moment, vāsudēva is not thought of, that is destruction; that is great sorrow; that is bewilderment; that is disfigurement). c) taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli 6,7 “*udaramantaram kurutē abhayam pratistām vindatē atasō bhayangatō bhavati*” (when he attains emperumān with devotion to be without fear for samsāram, he gets rid of fear of samsāram). Has it not been said in periya tirumoḻi 6-2-2 “*maṟandēn unnai munnam maṟanda madiyin manattāl iṟandēn*” (I forgot you earlier and as a consequence I died)

We will take up the 18<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 18. Vay molindu

*vāy moḻindu vāmananāy māvalipāl mūvadimaṇ nīyaḷandu koṇḍa nedumālē tāviya nin enjā iṇaiyadikkē ēḻ piṟappum āḷāgi anjādirukka aruḷ*

**Пословный перевод:**

vāmanan āy – in the form of vāmana \[one of the incarnations of emperumān\]; māvali pāl – with māhabali (going to him); vāy moḻindu – speaking a few words (like a child); mūvadimaṇ – land with three steps (taking as alms); aḷandu koṇḍa – measuring that; nedumālē – Oh supreme being!; enjā – without any shortage (in beauty); tāviya – the act of measuring the world; nin iṇai adikkē – for your two divine feet; ēḻ piṟappum – in all births; āḷ āgi – being a servitor; anjādu irukka – to be without any fear; nī aruḷ – you should grace

**Перевод:**

**vāmananāy vāy moḻindu – mercifully asking for land mass of three steps, as vāmana. emperumān went to the court of king mahābali and in his gibberish language \[as a child\], making even mahābali to be compassionate and not to refuse, played out an act.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār tells emperumān that carrying out service to emperumān’s divine feet alone should be the life for him who till now enjoyed emperumān’s experience at all times, without losing out on anything. He prays to emperumān that he should not get the fear of thinking about worldly life which is the exact opposite of the former \[carrying out service to emperumān’s divine feet\]. He tells emperumān to grace him by accepting him as his possession since emperumān does not let go of any of his possessions. Is there any benefit for thinking of emperumān forever without forgetting? āḻvār tells emperumān that he should tell him “Do not fear!” and grace him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vāy moḻindu** – emperumān spoke a few words which were not consistent, such as mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 3-8-9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/10/thiruvaimozhi-3-8-9-kolvan-nan/) “*koḷvan nān māvali mūvadi tā*” (Oh mahābali! I will accept; give me land of three steps) \[it is mentioned here as inconsistent since vāmana was a poor brāhmana and mahābali was a great king and instead of requesting humbly, he was ordering the king to give him what he was asking for\]. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is mentioned of as not speaking to anybody and is indifferent, as in cāndōjña upaniśat 3-14-2 “*avākyanādara:*” speaks such words to mahābali!

**vāmananāy** – even if his characteristics have to change, should the form also change? \[vāmana means being a dwarf\]

**māvalipāl mūvadimaṇ aḷandu koṇḍa nedumālē** – Oh [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)! You measured three steps of land after accepting as alms from mahābali. Did you measure something which did not belong to you? (were  they not your own worlds which you had asked for as alms from mahābali and measured!)

**tāviya** – measuring effortlessly

**nin enjā iṇai adikkē** – at your auspicious divine feet which measured the divine worlds

**nin enjā iṇai adi** – beautiful divine feet without any creases. ōr, very broad divine feet.

**ēḻ piṟappum āḷāgi** – carrying out service at all times

**anjādirukka aruḷ** – you should mercifully shower your grace on me such that I am able to carry out such service without any hurdle and without any fear. You should grace me saying “Will I let go of my possession? Once I have redeemed you, my possession, I will not let go of you. Do not fear”. You should say “I will not leave your protection with you; I will not leave your protection with someone else too”. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) tells [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) to mercifully shower his grace on him just as he carried out the task which indra wanted. Is it possible to remain without any fear? Does not one, who has the antidote for poison, put out his hand into the mouth of a snake without fear! In the same manner once a person gets the grace of emperumān, there is no hurdle for him to remain in samsāram .

We will take up the 19<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 19. Aruladu oliyume

*aruḷādu oḻiyumē ālilai mēl anṟu teruḷāda piḷḷaiyāic cērndān iruḷāda sindaiyarāyc cēvadikkē semmalar tūyk kaitoḻudu mudaiyarāy niṟpārkku mun*

**Пословный перевод:**

iruḷāda sindaiyarāy – having a heart which has not been touched by the darkness of ignorance; sē adikkē – for the reddish divine feet; sem malar tūy – submitting fresh flowers; kai toḻudu – joining the palms together as anjali; mundaiyarāy niṟpārkku – devotees who stand at the forefront (in emperumān’s matter); anṟu – at an earlier point of time; teruḷāda piḷḷai āy – as an innocent child; Al ilai mēl sērndān – emperumān who took rest on a tender banyan leaf; mun aruḷādu oḻiyumē – will he not shower grace ahead?

**Перевод:**

**aruḷādu oḻiyumē – Will he remain without showering grace on us? Will he remain without gracing us despite our being qualified? tirumaḻisai āḻvār also  mercifully mentions in tiruccanda viruttam 115 “ettināl idark kadal kidatti ēḻai nenjamē” (Oh foolish heart! Why are you immersed in the sea of sorrows?). Was it not for us that emperumān had said in śrī bhagavat gītā 18-66 “aham tvā sarva pāpēbhyō mōkśayiśyāmi ” (I will liberate you from all sins)!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār had requested emperumān to grace him to be without fear. His divine mind told him “If we compel him like this to grace us, would it mean that we are qualified to ask him for his mercy? ōr, is he capable of showering his grace on us?” and backtracks. āḻvār then consoles his mind saying “There is nothing in this, you see. Before showering his grace on people who are qualified, he will grace even unqualified people like us due to his ability to achieve impossible tasks. Hence do not fear”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ālilai mēl anṟu teruḷāda piḷḷaiyāyc cērndān** – the innocent child who reclined on a tender banyan leaf. For one who carried out the impossible task of keeping all the worlds in his stomach as a child, it is not that he cannot shower his mercy. When the worlds were under deluge, even without anyone asking him to protect, he protected all by keeping them in his stomach. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) is asking whether he would not shower his mercy on us to remain without fear when he is showering his mercy on those who do not even know to ask him to shower his mercy on them. He says that people would have hoped that he would protect them from deluge of samsāram just as he protected the worlds from \[actual\] deluge.  He says that he carried out an impossible task when he reclined on a tender banyan leaf which had just sprouted when the worlds were about to be destroyed.

**iruḷāda sindaiyarāy** – those with hearts which do not have any ignorance with regard to matter concerning [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

**sēvadikkē semmalar tūyk kaitoḻudu** – offering fresh flowers to his reddish, divine feet. Worśipping him with hands, offering him flowers.

**mundaiyarāy niṟpārrku mun** – you would see \[[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) tells his heart\] that he will grace us ahead of those who are standing in the forefront. He will delay showering his grace on those who are trying to attain him on their own efforts. For those like us who are holding on to him, he will shower his grace ahead \[of others\].

**teruḷāda piḷḷaiyāy** – during deluge, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) did not have the knowledge to make a distinction between those who prayed to him to protect them and those who did not pray to him for protection. īsn’t that the reason that he protected everyone in his stomach!

We will take up the 20<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 20. Mun ulagam

*munnulagam uṇḍumiḻndāykku avvulagam īradiyāl pinnaḷandu kōdal peridonṟē ennē tirumālē sengaṇediyānē engaḷ perumānē nī idanaip pēsu*

**Пословный перевод:**

mun – at an earlier point of time; ulagam – all the worlds; uṇḍu – swallowing them; umiḻndāykku – later spitting them out; avvulagam – those worlds; pin – at a later point of time; īr adiyāl – with two divine feet; aḷandu kōdal – measuring \[them\]; peridu onṟē – is it a huge task? \[No\]; tirumālē – Oh consort of mahālakśmi!; sem kaṇ nediyānē – Oh supreme being, having reddish divine eyes!; engaḷ perumānē – our svāmy (lord); nī idanai pēsu – you have to tell (for me to know); ennē – how amazing is this!

**Перевод:**

**mun ulagam uṇḍu umiḻndāykku – āḻvār says “for you who had carried out activities such as swallowing the worlds and spitting them out”, it is not a great act (measuring the worlds).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*For those who prayed to him, is he carrying out tasks without transforming himself? āḻvār says that he carries out their tasks, transforming himself. For those who have got rid of their fear of worldly life, isn’t talking about such activities of emperumān their pastime? He is asking emperumān to state such activities himself. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**avvulagam īradiyāl pin aḷandu kōdal peridonṟē** – this world was ready to be moulded as per your wish. This world had suffered just like cotton does when it undergoes twelve transformations from beginning to end such as (1) removing the seed (2) beating (3) curling (4) forming thread (5) making warp and weft (6) weaving (7) folding (8) selling (9) washing (10) drying (11) wearing (12) tearing.  Did you think that it was a great act when you measured this world with your two divine feet after receiving it as alms when you asked for three steps of land? Did you not carry out that activity out of love for the world!

**ennē** – How amazing is this! [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) says this with the same involvement as was exhibited by [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) when he said as in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-3-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruvaimozhi-1-3-1-paththudai-adiyavar/) “*ettiṟam*” (how amazing!). [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s simplicity is causing distress to āḻvār. Is there any congruence between your greatness and this lowly activity of asking for alms?

**tirumālē sengaṇ nediyānē** – did you carry out this activity realising who you are? ḍidn’t you, the consort of mahālakśmi who is the presiding deity for all wealth in this world, ask for alms! ḍidn’t you, having reddish eyes due to the happiness of being her consort, ask for alms!

**sengaṇ** – Just as cādōjña upaniśat says “*kapyāsam puṇḍarīkamēvamakśiṇī*” (having eyes like lotus just blossomed by sun), you, who are having lotus like eyes, asked for alms.

**nediyānē** – one who has immeasurable greatness! [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) is asking him whether he asked for alms because of any fault in his greatness. He is asking whether he carried out such great activities because of his greatness.

**engaḷ perumānē nī idanaip pēsu** – don’t you, who carried out such tasks for indra et al who desire other benefits, have to carry out such tasks for people like us too who do not desire any benefit other than you? Hence you have to say this.

**nī idahnaip pēsu** – are you only carrying out these activities? Could you also not speak about these? Even as [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) is asking him these questions, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is quite happy listening to him. āḻvār wants to spend his time, listening to emperumān.

We will take up the 21<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.
