## 21. Pesuvar evvalavu

*pēsuvār evvaḷavu pēsuvar avvaḷavē vāsa malarttuḻāy mālaiyān tēsudaiya cakkarattān sanginān sārngattān pongarava vakkaranaik konṟān vadivu*

**Пословный перевод:**

vāsam malar tuḻāy mālaiyān – one who is adorning the tuḷasi garland which is with fragrant flowers; tēsu udaiya cakkarattān – one who is having the radiant divine disc; sanginān – one who is having śrī pāncajanniyam (divine conch); sārngattān – having the divine bow sārngam; pongu aravam vakkaranaik konṟān – emperumān who killed dantavakkiran who came with lot of tumult; vadivu – characteristics \[of emperumān\]; pēsuvār evvaḷavu pēsuvar avvaḷavē – to the extent to which those who speak about them, speak

**Перевод:**

**pēsuvār evvaḷavu pēsuvar avvaḷavē – those who have the ability speak, spoke to the extent that they could. He is this much. It is not possible to know about all the characteristics of emperumān and speak about them. Has it not been mentioned “matikśayāt nivartatē na gōvinda guṇakśayāt” (people stop talking about gōvinda because of shortage of their knowledge and not because of shortage of his auspicious qualities)! Alternative explanation – he limits himself to the extent that his followers think about him and does not have anything for himself.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says “is it not impossible to speak about emperumān estimating his capabilities?”. In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār mentioned about vāmanāvatāram and in this pāsuram, he is speaking about kriṣṇavatāram in which emperumān, like vāmana, moved with everyone without any distinction. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vāsam malart tuḻāy mālaiyān** – Reasons are given for why he cannot be spoken of, estimating him completely.

**vāsam malart tuḻāy mālaiyān** – the word *vāsam* refers to coolness and fragrance. emperumān is decorated with divine tuḷasi garland which has coolness and fragrance, and is symbolic of the fact that he is the lord of all wealth.

**tēsudaiya cakkarattān sanginān sārngattān** – he has divine disc, divine conch and divine bow which have unlimited radiance. It is implied here that his divine weapons add further radiance to his already radiant divine form.

**pongu arava vakkaranaik konṟān vadivu** – one who killed the demon dantavakkiran who came making lot of noise. Alternatively, the divine form of one who killed dantavakkiran who came tumultuously and uproariously \[this refers to his killing dantavakkiran who came to help śiśupālan when kriṣṇa abducted rukmiṇi\].

We will take up the 22<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 22. Vadivar mudikotti

*vadivār mudikōtti vānavargaḷ nāḷum kadiyār malar tūvik kāṇum padiyānai semmaiyāl uḷḷurugic cevvanē nenjamē meymmiayē kāṇa virumbu*

**Пословный перевод:**

nenjamē – Oh heart!; vānavargaḷ – nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam); vadivu Ar – being beautiful; mudi – crowns; kōtti – making them worship; kadi Ar malar – having fragrant flowers; nāḷum – every day; tūvi – offering; kāṇum padiyānai – emperumān who is seen (always) with divine form; semmaiyāl – in a proper way; uḷ urugi – with mind melting; sevvanē – in a direct way; meymmaiyē – as it is; kāṇa – to worship; virumbu – have desire

**Перевод:**

**vadivār mudi kōtti – nityasūris lower their beautiful crowns, bow down and worship emperumān. āḻvār says that it should be like this only \[everyone should worship emperumān\].**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār said that it is impossible to speak about emperumān making an estimate of him. He then looks at his heart and asks it to involve itself in efforts to see him directly. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**mudi kōtti** – nityasūris bow down their crowns. Just as [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) mercifully mentioned in his tiruvāimoḻi 8-4-5 “*paṇiyā amarar*” nityasūris, like rāvaṇa who did not bow down to emperumān, will not bow down to anyone other than [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

**vānavargaḷ** – this term could refer to either nityasūris or to celestial entities such as brahmā et al.

**nāḷum** – everyday. Just as we eat every day, nityasūris will worship [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) every day. Has it not been mentioned in rig aśtakam 1-2-7 “*sadā paśyanti sūraya:* ” (nityasūris constantly see emperumān)!

**kadiyār malar tūvi** – offering fragrant flowers at the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), without expecting any benefit

**kāṇum** – as the benefit of offering such flowers, they will be able to see emperumān. They will engage all their sensory perceptions with emperumān.

**padiyānai** – having divine form. The word *padi* would refer to either his divine form or his divine characteristics.

**semmiayāl uḷ urugi** – melting with the heart, realising in a proper way that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is the lord and we are servitors.

**sevvanē nenjamē** – Oh heart! In the way to attain him. This implies having emperumān as the means for attaining him. Even if you engage in *bhakti* (devotion) instead of trying to see him through your efforts, seeing him when he manifests himself on his own.

**meymmaiyē kāṇa virumbu** – instead of trying to see only one quality of his and withdrawing, make an effort to see him fully! Have desire in this instead of looking for other benefits. In other words, implying that he is the end goal (benefit). [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) is already enjoying the darśan of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). What is meant by asking his heart to desire to see him? Since it has seen emperumān once, it should not think that it is enough; also, since he is seeing emperumān in this world only and since he cannot wait patiently to attain paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) he is saying this way.

We will take up the 23<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 23. Virumbi vin

*virumbi viṇ maṇṇaḷanda anjiṟaiya vaṇḍār surumbu tuḷaiyil senṟu ūda arumbum punam tuḻāyān ponnam kaḻaṟkē manam tuḻāy mālāy varum*

**Пословный перевод:**

am siṟaiya vaṇḍu – female beetles with beautiful wings; Ar surumbu – the male beetles which will never separate from them; tuḷaiyil senṟu ūda – blowing in the nerves (of flowers); arumbum – blossoming; punam tuḻāy mālaiyān – emperumān who is donning tuḷasi garland, where the tuḷasi is always fresh as if it were on its own field; virumbi viṇ maṇ aḷanda pon am kaḻaṟkē – in the matter of the beautiful divine feet which measured the outer worlds as well as earth, desirously; manam – my heart; tuḻāy – fully immersed; māl āy varum – is lying bewildered

**Перевод:**

**virumbi viṇ maṇ aḷanda – āḻvār says that emperumān measured the skies and earth as if he had obtained something which was not his to start with. Since it is stated that he measured it vilfully, he did not measure the worlds which had forests and streams \[uneven surfaces with ups and downs\] because he had to, but because he wanted to.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār told his heart to desire to see emperumān fully. His instruction immediately fructified. His heart immediately started desiring as he had asked. ṣouldn’t āḻvār’s heart be like this! Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**anjiṟaiya vaṇḍu** – beetle with beautiful wings

**Ar surumbu** – beetle which is together with divine tuḷasi. periyavāccān piḷḷai, one of he commentators for this prabandam, makes a distinction here – while *vaṇḍu* would refer to beetle, since [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) has used the term *surumbu* which means male beetle, the first term *vaṇḍu* would refer to female beetle and the two are together always.

**tuḷaiyil senṟu ūda** – the beetles know where honey gets generated and hence they go to such nerve roots and blow with their mouths.

**arumbum punam tuḻāy mālaiyān** – due to such act \[of the beetles\] the tuḷasi just blossoms. Since tuḷasi is constantly touching the divine chest of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), the garland believes that it is very much on its own habitat and hence is always fresh.

Such emperumān’s …..

**ponnam kaḻaṟkē** – at the beautiful divine feet

**manam tuḻāy mālāy varum** – the mind will come vilfully, with total infatuation, to delve \[into such divine feet\]. The mind will get bewildered at the tuḷasi garland of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and stay that way. Due to infatuation, mind will get bewildered and come searching. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/)’s mind will suffer just as the beetles suffered at the hands of divine tuḷasi.

We will move on to the 24<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

## 24. Varungal irunilanum

*varungāl iru nilanum māl visumbum kāṟṟum nerungu tī nīr uruvum ānān porundum sudarāḻi onṟudaiyān sūḻ kaḻalē nāḷum todar āḻi nenjē toḻudu*

**Пословный перевод:**

āḻi nenjē – Oh my heart which is like the deep sea!; iru nilanum – expansive earth; māl visumbum – boundless sky; kāṟṟum – wind; nerungu tī – fire which cannot be split; nīr uruvum ānān – the fifth element water. emperumān became the antaryāmi (in-dwelling soul) for the five elements; porundum sudar āḻi onṟu udaiyān – emperumān who is holding the well suited (to him) radiant divine disc; sūḻ kaḻalē – the divine feet which subordinate the followers; varungāl nāḷum – in the days to come; toḻudu – vorśipping; todar – keep following

**Перевод:**

**varungāl – let the days bygone remain as having been spent without purpose. In the days to come, try to do like this \[āḻvār tells his heart to do the things which he will spell out shortly\]**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār tells his heart to fall head over heels on emperumān and attain him, instead of remaining passively, thinking that we have carried out all that was required to be done with total involvement. He tells his heart to enjoy emperumān without any break, now that the desire has been created. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**irunilanum mālvisumbum kāṟṟum nerungutī nīr uruvum ānān** – he is the sustaining force for the world made of five elements (since he is the indvelling soul for the expansive earth, the boundless skies, wind, fire which cannot be separated and water, it is implied that he is the lord of all leelā vibhūti). Reference to the five elements implies mentioning the entire leelā vibhūti.

**porundum sudarāḻi onṟudaiyān** – since it says that he has the unique, radiant divine disc, it implies that he is with nitya vibhūti. Reference to divine disc implies everyone in nitya vibhūti. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has the divine disc which makes everyone to worship him.

Thus, the one who has both vibhūtis, such [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s ….

**sūḻ kaḻalē** – the divine feet which subordinate the followers. The divine feet which will not allow anyone to go out but keep them with themselves. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) asks his heart “After knowing that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is coming towards us with the intention of capturing us, should you not follow him?”.

**āḻi nenjē** – Oh heart who is limited \[to the depth of the ocean\]! Is there a need for me to instruct you? Henceforth, keep following him, vorśipping him. It is enough that you have lost out on him earlier. In the times to come, keep following him without any break and attain him.

Let us move on to the 25<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 25. Toludal paludunde

*toḻudāl paḻuduṇḍē tūnīr ulagam muḻuduṇḍa moykuḻalāḷ āycci viḻuduṇḍa vāyānai mālvidaiyēḻ seṟṟānai vānavarkkum sēyānai nenjē siṟandu*

**Пословный перевод:**

tū niṟ ulagam – world surrounded by oceans with pure water; muḻudu – totally; uṇḍu – one who kept in his divine stomach; moy kuḻalāḷ āycci – yaśōdā, who has dense locks; viḻudu – butter; uṇḍa vāyānai – one who has the mouth which ate; māl vidai ēḻ seṟṟānai – one who killed the seven plump bulls; vānavarkkum sēyānai – one who is out of reach of celestial entities such as brahmā et al, such emperumān; nenjē – Oh heart!; siṟandu – in a great way; toḻudāl – if you worship; paḻudu uṇḍē – will there be any fault?

**Перевод:**

**toḻudāl paḻudu uṇḍē – if you attain emperumān will any fault come your way? If you do not attain him, what calamity will befall you? After telling his heart about the greatness of vorśipping emperumān, āḻvār is asking his heart whether there is any harm in it. He tells his heart to tell him if there is anything wrong in what he is saying. āḻvār is asking his heart “Just as emperumān’s stomach became the sustenance for the world and butter became the sustenance for emperumān, isn’t servitorśip the sustenance for you?”.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Despite āḻvār instructing his divine mind, it appeared to āḻvār that it kept thinking of the past, with haughtiness. āḻvār tells his mind “if you approach emperumān and attain him, no shortcoming will come your way”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**tū nīr ulagam muḻudu uṇḍu** – Am I asking you to drink the bitter juice of neem fruit? Am I not asking you to attain the sweet [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)?

**tū nīr ulagam muḻudu uṇḍu** – there is none who is considered as unwanted by emperumān. Even after keeping the worlds in his stomach during deluge, he was not satisfied.

**tū nīr ulagam** – world having clear water. In other words, when emperumān kept the world in his stomach, he ensured that the waters surrounding the earth did not get muddied. Since he was not satisfied even after swallowing the world, he ate the butter which had the fingerprints of his followers. He filled up the void created after swallowing the world.

**moy kuḻalāḷ āycci viḻuduṇḍa vāyānai** – only after eating the butter kept by yaśōdāp pirātti was his stomach filled up. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who controls the entire worlds, was subservient to yaśōdā. It appeared to emperumān that when he did not eat butter, deluge had set in. He cannot sustain himself if he does not eat his followers’ possessions (emperumān has the divine mouth which ate butter kept by yaśōdā who has beautiful locks).

**māl vidai ēḻ seṟṟānai** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who killed the seven fearsome bulls. One who killed the seven bulls which were in exult, and which were a hurdle for him to be with nappinnaip pirātti \[incarnation of nīḷā dēvi\]. This implies that he removes the hurdles of followers who are special to him.

**vānavarkkum sēyānai** – one who is far away from entities such as brahmā et al. This implies that he is far removed from those who try to attain him through their own efforts.

**siṟandu toḻudāl paḻuduṇḍē** – is there any fault in vorśipping one who is simple to this followers?

**siṟandu** – if you \[[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/)’s heart\] make yourself qualified and worship him, is there any harm? After knowing the characteristics of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), you are wondering whether you are qualified to worship him; you are not above entities such as brahmā et al! āḻvār says that since emperumān is far removed from entities such as brahmā et al, there is no alternative to falling at his feet.

We will take up the 26<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 26. Siranda en sindaiyum

*siṟanda en sindaiyum sengaṇ aravum niṟainda sīr nīḷ kacciyuḷḷum uṟaindaduvum vēngadamum vehkāvum vēḷukkaippādiyumē tām kadavār taṇ tuḻāiyār*

**Пословный перевод:**

taṇ tuḻāyār tām – emperumān who is adorning the cool, tuḷasi garland; kadavār – not leaving for even one day; uṟaindaduvum – the places where he took permanent residence; siṟanda en sindaiyum – my heart which is the greatest (amongst all); sem kaṇ aravum – tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan) who has reddish eyes; niṟainda sīr – having abundant wealth; nīḷ – expansive; kacciyuḷḷum – the divine town of kacci (present day kāncīpuram); vēngadamum – the divine abode of tirumalai; vehkāvum – the divine abode of tiruvhkā; vēḷukkaip pādiyumē – the divine abode of tiruvēḷukkai

**Перевод:**

**siṟanda en sindaiyum sengaṇ aravum – both these are equal as far as emperumān’s desire goes. In other words, āḻvār says that emperumān considers āḻvār’s heart to be the exact match for tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan) who is his most desired mattress.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār mercifully states how, after he attained emperumān, he desired āḻvār’s divine mind more than his divine abodes. The reason for emperumān taking residence in temples is only to enter the hearts of followers who accept him. Alternatively, we can say that āḻvār is saying that the benefit that we obtained out of emperumān eating yaśōdāp pirātti’s butter is the simplicity of emperumān, more than his simplicity in eating butter, by taking residence in divine abodes. Had he not asked “Is there any harm in vorśipping?”. After āḻvār saying this, will emperumān keep quiet? (he took residence in āḻvār’s divine heart). Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**sengaṇ aravum** – anantāḻvān’s eyes are reddish, like the eyes of those who take intoxicating materials such as honey, because of his connection with emperumān.

**uṟaindaduvum** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has taken residence in āḻvār’s divine heart, considering it as his own,  just as he has taken residence atop tiruvanantāḻvān who is his most desired mattress.

**niṟainda sīr nīḷ kacciyuḷḷum** – in divine kacci which is full of divine abodes of emperumān and unlimited sweetness. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) will reside continuously at tirukkacci, tirumalai, tiruvehkā and tiruvēḷukkai, without ever letting go of them. By considering the verse sequence to be *niṟainda sīr nīḷ kacciyuḷḷum uṟaindaduvum – vēngadamum vehkāvum vēḷukkaip pādiyumē tām kadavār* we can say that emperumān cannot let go of each of the divine abodes of vēngadam, vehkā and vēḷukkai and he will reside continuously in these divine abodes and that he resides with lot of desire in the great divine mind of [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/), tiruvanantāḻvān and the town of tirukkacci which has many divine abodes. Since the word *uṟaindaduvum* (resides) has been used, we can consider it this way. Alternatively, by taking the sequence  *siṟanda en sindaiyum sengaṇ aravum tām kadavār* we can say that emperumān will not go past āḻvār’s great divine mind and tiruvanantāḻvān. *niṟainda sīr nīḷ kacciyuḷḷum vēngadamum vehkāvum vēḷukkaippādiyumē uṟaindaduvum* will give the meaning that emperumān merely resided in these places.

Who does like this?

**tanduḻāyār** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who wears the divine tuḷasi garland, taking a vow that he will protect the followers.

We will take up the 27<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 27. Are tuyarulandar

*ārē tuyaruḻandār tunbuṟṟār āṇḍaiyār kārē malinda karum kadalai nērē kadaindānaik kāraṇanai nīraṇaimēl paḷḷi adaindānai nāḷum adaindu*

**Пословный перевод:**

kārē malinda karum kadalai – the dark ocean which is full of clouds; nērē kadandānai – one who stood in the forefront and churned; kāraṇanai – one who is the cause for all the worlds; nīr aṇai mēl paḷḷi adaindānai – one who is reclining on tiruppāṟkadal (on ādiśēśan); adaindu – after attaining; nāḷum tuyar uḻandār Ar – who suffered even for one day?; tunbu uṟṟār āṇḍaiyār – where are those who experienced sorrow (like that)?

**Перевод:**

**ārē tuyar uḻandār tumbuṟṟār – who, before this, has suffered the consequences of carrying out sinful deeds which are the reason for sorrow? Are there people who have experienced the result of carrying out lowly deeds? After attaining emperumān, suffering the results of sins is equivalent to “agnina sincēt ” (wetting with fire). Who has experienced the consequences of carrying out sins in the past, after attaining emperumān? Hasn’t emperumān himself told arjuna in śrī bhagavat gītā 9-31 “kauntēya pratijānīhi na mē bhakti: praṇaśyati” (my devotee does not get destroyed. Oh arjuna! You take this oath!). When the king of ocean did not pay heed initially to śrī rāma’s request to yield way for going to lankā (to wage war against rāvaṇa), śrī rāma prepared to shoot an arrow on him. But when the king of ocean surrendered to him, did not  śrī rāma not only not shoot the arrow on him but shoot it against the enemies of the king of ocean! Once a person attains emperumān, all that remains is experiencing the benefit of that.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār is asking whether we have ever heard of anyone suffering due to the sins committed, after attaining emperumān. If this is the characteristic of emperumān, is there anything such as someone is experiencing the results of sins, asks āḻvār. Did not yama (deity of dharma, righteousness) ask a person who was experiencing the results of sinful deeds in hell, “You will go the terrible way! Have you not attempted to attain emperumān even for one day?”. Did yama not tell a person, as mentioned in śrī viṣṇu darmam “kim tvayā nārcitō dēva: kēśava: klēśa nāśana:” (did you not worship kēśava, who removes sufferings?). Had he not said “You will go the terrible way! You did not go the right way! You spoiled yourself!”. ḍidn’t sītāp pirātti tell rāvaṇa in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 21-19 “mitramaupayikam kartum rāma: stānam parīpsatā, vadam ca aniccatā ghōram tvayā asau puruśarśabha: ” (śrī rāma  who is the best among all puruśas (souls) is the apt person to make friends for you who would like to remain in this world and who would not like to die a horrible death )! Did she not say that he had not attempted to carry out atonement for all the sins that he had committed! Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**āṇḍaiyār** – in which time are those, who did not experience that benefit?

**kārē malinda karum kadalai** – is emperumān such powerless that his followers have to suffer the consequences of sinful deeds? Is there any suffering for those who attain the omnipotent? īsn’t he the one who churned the ocean which was surrounded by clouds with *mantara* mountain as the staff!

**nērē kadaindānai** – he churned the ocean such that only nectar came out, without disturbing the clouds which had gathered over the ocean.

**kāraṇanai** – the causative entity for all the worlds. Am I leaving him because I want to disprove the relationship with him? There is no place to go other than he, who is the cause for the worlds.

**nīraṇai mēl paḷḷi adaindānai** – are we suffering because he is not making any effort to protect us? He is reclining in the milky ocean so that he could come near us and carry out our activities since he is the causative factor.

**nāḷum adaindu** – have those, who attained him, suffered because they had carried out deeds which would give rise to sorrow? Since attaining him itself is sweet, attaining him every day will give rise to sweetness only. Alternatively, we can consider the meaning for *āṇḍaiyār* as any point of time and *nāḷum* as every day and consider the meaning for the verse as those who would not suffer at any point of time or any day. The reason for [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) saying this is the deep belief that he has on [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).  An incident which happened in the life of [parāśara bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/) (son of [kūrattāḻvān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kuraththazhwan/)) is quoted here. One day, during the utsavam at the śrīrangam temple, parāsara bhattar was recounting the greatness of [periya perumāḷ (śrī ranganātan)](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), at a place called tiruvūṟṟangarai. At that time, a disciple of siṟiyāttān came there and told bhattar, “jīyā! (a name by which bhattar was known, which means a knowledgeable/venerable person) All the brāhmaṇas have congregated here. This is the time for *sandyāvandanam* (a daily ritual for people from the first three varṇas). Come, let us get up and carry out sandyāvandanam”.  bhattar told him “What have you done? When periya perumāḷ  and the festivities are going on in a grand scale, just because I have not carried out a small task,  will yama write it down in his book?”. bhattar then went on to mercifully recite the pāsuram “*sollāy tirumārbā*” from periya tirumoḻi 6-3-9 and gave the following explanation:  “*unakkāgat toṇḍu patta nallēnai  vinaigaḷ naliyāmai nambu nambi* ” – a king was listening to the names of people who had committed crimes in his kingdom. If the person who is reading out the names, mentions the name of a person who is very close to the king and the king says “let it be so. Go ahead and read the other names”, can that person’s name be read out again? In the same way, if emperumān has accepted a person, yama et al cannot delve into his misdeeds ever. Thus [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) says that due to any shortcoming in the power of emperumān or if the relationship between us and emperumān becomes false, we have to experience the result of our karmas (deeds). In other words, since there is no shortage in [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s powers to remove our sins and since there is nothing false in our relationship between us and emperumān, there will be no need to experience the result of our sins.

We will take up the 28<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 28. Adaindadu aravanai mel

*adaindadu aravaṇai mēl aivarkkāy anṟu midaindadu bārada vem pōr udaindaduvum āyccipāl mattukkē ammanē vāḷeyiṟṟup pēycci pāl uṇḍa pirān*

**Пословный перевод:**

vāḷ eyiṟu – having teeth which are like sword; pēycci – pūtanā’s; pāl – bosom milk; uṇḍa – one who drank and killed her; pirān – the benefactor; adaindadu – reached out to take rest; aravu aṇai mēl – on top of ādiśēśan as mattress; anṟu – at one point of time; aivarkku āy – for the five pāṇḍavas; midaindadu – conducted vehemently; bārada vempōr – the rigorous bhārata battle \[ held at kurukśētra\]; udaindaduvum – trembling with fear; āyccipāl – towards the covherd person yaśōdā’s; mattukkē – staff; ammanē – how amazing!

**Перевод:**

**adaindadu aravaṇai mēl – emperumān cannot ignore tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan) who cannot sustain himself without emperumān. Hence he reclines on him.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that those who attained emperumān will experience the result of their misdeeds on the day when the pāṇḍavas kill their adversaries themselves \[without seeking kriṣṇa’s help\] (in other words, this will not happen at all). In continuation of the earlier pāsuram when āḻvār had mentioned about divine abodes “vēngadamum vehkāvum vēḷukkaippādiyumē”, he mentions about how emperumān is subservient to his followers. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**aivarkkāy anṟu midaindadu bārada vem pōr** – for the sake of his followers, emperumān even transformed himself. For the sake of pāṇḍavas, he conducted the rigorous mahābhārata war. By these two lines, it is opined here that emperumān will give his own divine form as he gave to ādiśēśan and transform his divine form, as he did in the case of pāṇḍavas. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) says that emperumān, who has the softness of divine form to recline on ādiśēśan, conducted the rigorous battle also.

**midaindadu** – āḻvār says that it was [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who conducted the mahābhārata battle. The pāṇḍavas were not even the mere reason for it.

**udaindaduvum āyccipāl mattukkē** – emperumān did not fear bhīśma et al; he did not fear their weapons such as brahmāstra, spear etc. But he was very much afraid of the staff \[used for churning curd to get butter\] at the hands of yaśōdā. The one who did not lose to great warriors lost out to yaśōdā, without any remedy! Fearful of yaśōdā, he suffered at her hands just as his opponents suffered at his. It appears that if his enemies had dropped their weapons and instead took a staff in their hands, they might have won! When rāvaṇa lost his bow, didn’t śrī rāma spare him without killing him, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 59-130 “*cacāla cāpanca mumōca vīra:*” (rāvaṇa walked unsteadily; he let go of his bow)!

**ammanē** – how amazing is this act of his when he stood trembling seeing the staff at the hands of yaśōdā! [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) says “What simplicity! Haven’t I been won over by this simplicity?”

**vāḷ eyiṟṟup pēycci pāl uṇḍa pirān** – he is the benefactor who destroyed pūtanā who came with teeth which were like swords when he was just an infant. Afraid of yaśōdā who was very much favourable to him, he did not sever himself from the string with which she had tied him. Is it not amazing that even at that tender age, he had the capability to distinguish between his mother and the demon who came in the form of his mother! What would have happened had you been afraid of pūtanā who came in the form of yaśōdā? Is it not our fortune that you did not fear her thinking that she is your mother! For the phrase *vāḷ eyiṟu* we can consider the meaning as pūtana with radiant teeth. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) expresses his amazement with the word *ammanē*.

We will take up the 29<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 29. Peyccipalunda

*pēyccipāluṇḍa perumānaip pērndeduttu āycci mulai koduttāḷ anjādē vāytta iruḷār tirumēni in pavaḷac cevvāy teruḷā moḻiyānaic cērndu*

**Пословный перевод:**

pēycci – pūtanā’s; pāl – bosom milk; uṇḍa – one who drank; perumānai – being the supreme being; vāytta iruḷār tirumēni – having a divine form which is dark, and befitting him; in pavaḷac cevvāy – having a reddish mouth similar to sweet coral; teruḷā moḻiyānai – kaṇṇapirān (kriṣṇa) who was speaking gibberish; sērndu – going near him; āycci – yaśōdā; pērndu eduttu – embracing him with affection; anjādē – without any fear; mulai koduttāḷ – fed him her bosom

**Перевод:**

**pēycci pāḷ uṇḍa perumānai – killing pūtanā after drinking milk from her poisoned bosom. Due to this, he protected himself and gave himself to the world. Has it not been mentioned in harivamsam “stanyam tat viśasammiśram rasyamāsīt jagatgurō:” (her breast-milk which was poisoned, was very sweet for him)! There is none in the world to equal him when it comes to escaping death.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Once he mentioned about yaśōdāp pirātti, āḻvār remembers of her affection for kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) and her qualities and says that she is unique. In the previous pāsuram had he not said “udaindaduvum āyccippāl mattukku”? Just as there is none to equal him in being afraid of yaśōdā, he says that there is none to equal yaśōdā in showering affection.  In the previous pāsuram he had said “midaindadu bārada vempōr”. Just as emperumān transformed himself for the same of his followers, he says in this pāsuram yaśōdā transformed herself for his sake. For a woman, being afraid of is a natural characteristic. For yaśōdā, being without fear is transforming herself and it is this which she did, he says. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**perumānai** – he re-established his quality of śēśi (being the Lord). When events such as pralayam (deluge) take place, we can remain unperturbed since he is there to take care of. Did not danger come to him (due to pūtanā)?

**pērndeduttu āycci mulai koduttāḷ anjādē** – vouldn’t the mother become fearful once she comes to know that kriṣṇa has been captured by pūtanā! But, leaving aside the quality of fear which is natural for women, yaśōdā rushed to him and tried to give him antidote. Instead of swooning on hearing that kriṣṇa has been taken away by pūtanā, yaśōdā came walking to the place where kriṣṇa was, lifted him up and started nursing him. Swooning is an act of affection only. However, śouldn’t one take the initiatives to get back kriṣṇa! Did not [āḻvārs](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/), who were blessed with knowledge sans any ignorance, engage in the activity of *madalūrdal* (an act in which the person sits on a horse made of palmyra leaves and requests the townspeople to unite him/her with his/her beloved) knowing full well their as well as [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s nature! This (act of yaśōdā) is similar to that.

**vāytta iruḷār tirumēni** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) describes the radiance in the divine form of kriṣṇa as he sat on pūtanā’s lap to drink her milk. Wasn’t this beauty the reason for yaśōdā to shun looking after herself and lift kriṣṇa, falling head over heels!

**in pavaḷac cevvāy** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has divine lips similar to coral. He smiled when he was suckling from pūtanā just as he smiled when suckling from yaśōdā.

**vāytta iruḷār tirumēni** – he has a divine form which will remove fatigue.

**teruḷā moḻiyān** – when suckling from pūtanā, he will talk something gibberish. Looking at her face, in that gibberish language he said “*ācci, ācci* ”(mother)

**mulai koduttāḷ**  – for such kriṣṇa, yaśōdā transformed herself and suckled him.

**sērndu** – approaching him.

We will take up 30<sup>th</sup> pāsuram, next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 30. Sernda tirumal

*sērnda tirumāḷ kadal kudandai vēngadam nērnda en sindai niṟai visumbum vāynda maṟai pādagam anantan vaṇ tuḻāykkaṇṇi iṟai pādiyāya ivai*

**Пословный перевод:**

kadal – tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean); kudandai – tirukkudandai (present day kumbakōṇam); vēngadam – tiruvēngadam; nērnda en sindai – my suitable heart; niṟai visumbum – the completely fulfilled śrīvaikuṇṭam; vāynda maṟai – fitting vēdam (sacred text); pādagam – tiruppādagam (divine abode in present day kāncīpuram); anantan – ādiśēśan; āya ivai – all these; vaṇ tuḻāyk kaṇṇi – one who is wearing the beautiful tuḷasi garland; tirumāḷ sērnda – where śrīman nārāyaṇa gives divine darśan appropriately; iṟai pādi – capitals (places where he has taken residence)

**Перевод:**

**sērnda tirumāḷ – the places where emperumān who is śrīya: pati (consort of śrī mahālakśmi) has reached**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*yaśōdā showered affection on kriṣṇa since he was near her. When we say the he is not near us now, āḻvār gives the names of divine abodes where he has taken residence. He mercifully states that emperumān, who was simplicity personified for one person (yaśōdā) at one point of time, is now simplicity personified for all people at all times by taking residence in many divine abodes. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kadal kudandai vēngadam** – tiruppāṟkadal, tirukkudandai and tiruvēngadam

**nērnda en sindai** – my heart which is not that great \[as the divine abodes mentioned above\]. Since āḻvār says **nērnda en sindai** the reason for taking residence in all these divine abodes is only to enter his heart.

**niṟai visumbu** – śrīvaikuṇṭam which is a completely fulfilled divine abode. It is called as *tripādvibhūti*  (three times as expansive as leelā vibhūti, the materialistic realm)

**vāynda maṟai** – the vēdas (sacred texts) which describe him as he is. Thus, to āḻvār, emperumān taking residence in divine abodes explicitly and standing firmly in vēdas appear with equal radiance, without any difference.

**pādagam** – the divine abode of tiruppādagam

**anantan** – ādiśēśan

**vaṇ tuḻāyk kaṇṇi** – the divine tuḷasi garland

**pādiyāya** – are capitals (towns)

**ivai** – āḻvār says that these are the places where emperumān has taken residence. *vaṇ tuḻāyk kaṇṇi*  could also be considered as a place where he has taken residence.  ōr it can taken as an adjective for emperumān and consider the meaning as: emperumān who has adorned the beautiful tuḷasi garland  has taken residence in these places.

We will take up the 31<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 31. Ivai avan koyil

*ivai avan kōyil iraṇiyadu āgam avai seydu ari uruvum ānān sevi teriyā nāgattān nāl vēdattuḷḷān naṟavēṟṟān pāgattān pāṟkadaluḷān*

**Пословный перевод:**

iraṇiyanadu – the demon hiraṇya kaśyap’s; āgam – chest; avai seydu – to break it into many pieces; ari uruvam ānān – one who incarnated as narasimha; sevi teriyā nāgattān – having as his mattress tiruvanantāḻvān who does not have separate ears as his eyes serve the purpose of both eyes and ears; nāl vēdattu uḷḷān – one who resides inside the four vēdas (sacred texts); naṟavu ēṟṟān pāgattān – one who has in one part of divine form, rudra, who has honey-like gangā in his body (alternatively, one who has in one part of divine form, rudra, who has liquor in his hand); pāṟkadaluḷān – one who is reclining on tiruppāṟkadal; avan – that emperumān’s; kōyil ivai – divine abodes are these which were mentioned in the previous pāsuram

**Перевод:**

**ivai avan kōyil– the divine abodes mentioned in the previous pāsuram are his places of dwelling.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that emperumān, who has taken residence in the divine abodes mentioned in the previous pāsuram, removes the enemies of followers and is very simple. The divine abodes mentioned in the previous pāsuram still remain in his heart and continue. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**iraṇiyadu āgam** – the body of the ill-tempered hiraṇya kaśyap

**avai seydu ari uruvum ānān** – Just as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 94-82 “*cinnam bhinnam*” (cut and split), [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) took the form of narasimha to split iraṇiyan’s heart into many parts.

**āgam avai seydu** – splitting the chest. He split one into two. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) says that the purpose of emperumān taking residence in many different abodes is to destroy enemies \[of his followers\].

**sevi teriyā nāgattān** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has his mattress tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan) who carries out the activities of his ears through his eyes. āren’t snakes referred to as “*cakśu: śrava:*” (in tamiḻ, *katsevi*, having eyes as ears)! Has it not been mercifully mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 9-9-9 “*tūdu sey kaṇgaḷ koṇḍonṟu pēsi*” (speak with the help of eyes and carry out a message) by which the function of one sensory organ has been appropriated by another!

**nāl vēdattuḷḷān** – emperumān has been identified by the four vēdas.

**naṟavēṟṟān pāgattān** – *naṟavu* refers to the lowly arrack. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has the characteristic of giving a portion of his divine form to accommodate Sivan who carries lowly arrack in his hand. This refers to his quality of śeelam (simplicity). *naṟavu* could also be considered as referring to sweetness \[like honey\]. Thus this will refer to Sivan taking gangā on his head, and emperumān giving place to such Sivan on his divine form. *ēṟṟān* would refer to one who has *ēṟu* (bull) as his vehicle and combined with *naṟavu* this will refer to Sivan who has bull as his vehicle.

Who has such a person as a part of his divine form?

**pāṟkadaluḷān** – one who is reclining in tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean).

We will take up the 32<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 32. Parkadalum vengadamum

*pāṟkadalaum vēngadamum pāmbum panivisumbum nūṟkadalum nuṇṇūlatāmari mēl pāṟpattu irundār manamum idamāgak koṇḍān kurundu ositta gōpālagan*

**Пословный перевод:**

kurundu – the kurundam tree (a variety of tree growing along the bank of river yamunā); ositta – one who snapped it and destroyed it; gōpālagan – kaṇṇapirān (kriṣṇa) who tends to cows; pāṟkadalum – tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean); vēngadamum – tiruvēngadam hills; pāmbum – tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan); panivisumbum – paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) which is very cool (without the heat from samsāram casting its shadow); nūṟkadalum – śāstras which are like the expansive ocean; nuṇ nūla tāmarai mēlpāḷ pattirundār manamum – the hearts of yōgis (those who carry out penance) who focus their sensory perceptions on the lotus-like heart which is mentioned in those subtle śāstras; idam āgak koṇḍān – has taken these as his places of dwelling

**Перевод:**

**pāṟkadalum – tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that there is no limit to the divine abodes where emperumān, who is this simple, has taken residence. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vēngadamum** – tiruvēngadam hills

**pāmbum** – tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan)

**pani visumbum** – the sweet śrīvaikuṇṭam which is cool, without a trace of the heat which is emanating from samsāram (materialistic realm)

**nūṟkadalaum** – the sea of knowledge. The sea of itihāsa (great epics such as śrī rāmāyaṇa and mahābhārata) and purāṇas (ancient literature describing [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)).

**nuṇ nūla tāmarai mēlpāl pattirundār manamum** – Just as it is mentioned in taittirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli “*padmakōśapratīkāśam hrudayncāpyadōmukham*” (heart which is like the inverted lotus) the hearts of those who know that emperumān resides in the lotus-like hearts of people, as known only from śāstras. The hearts of yōgis who focus their sensory perceptions on the place revealed by śāstras as lotus-like heart.

**idamāgak koṇḍān** – one who has these places as his dwelling abode.

Who is this?

**kurundu ositta gōpālagan** – kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) who has the characteristic of destroying enemies. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) is amazed that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who has such greatness, appears as such a simple entity to him.

We will move on to the 33<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 33. Palaganay alilai mel

*pālaganāy ālilai mēl paiya ulagellām mēloru nāḷ uṇḍavanē meymmaiyē mālavanē mandarattāl mānīrkkadal kadaindu vānamudam andarattārkkīndāy nī anṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

mēl oru nāḷ – once upon a time; pālaganāy – in the form of an infant; Al ilai mēl – on top of a tender banyan leaf; ulagu ellām – all the worlds; paiya – slowly; meymmaiyē – truly; uṇḍavanē – Oh one who ate and reclined!; mālavanē – Oh the great one!; nī – you, who are like these; anṟu – on that day; mandarattāl – with the mantara hill \[a celestial hill\]; mā nīr kadal kadaindu – churning the ocean which has huge quantity of water; vān amudam – the great nectar; andarattārkku – to dēvas (celestial entities); īndāy – you offered

**Перевод:**

**pālaganāy – taking the form of an infant so that some people will have sympathy on him**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that emperumān, who is like this, gives whatever is asked of to those who ask for them. He says that emperumān’s possessions are all only for others. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings.*

**ālilai mēl** – atop a tender banyan leaf which had just then sprouted

**mēl orun nāḷ uṇḍavanē** – when deluge showed itself, you kept all the worlds inside your stomach and protected so that the worlds would not face hurdles from the deluge

**ulagellām paiya uṇḍu** – keeping the worlds in the divine stomach slowly so that they would not hit against each other

**meymmaiyē** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) says that truly there was no fault in this activity. Unlike clairvoyants who carry out some magical acts \[with the sleight of their hands\], did not [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) truly carry out this deed \[of keeping the worlds in his divine stomach\]!

**mālavanē** – *māl* refers to greatness. Oh supreme being! No one can estimate the extent of activities carried out by you

**mandarattāḷ mā nīr kadal kadaindu** – churning the huge ocean with the \[celestial\] mountain *mantara* as the staff.

**vān amudam** – the potent nectar; in other words, the nectar which has the power to provide permanency to the physical form; the nectar which would give life to dēvas (celestial entities) so that they would not face old age, death etc.

**andarattārkku īndāy nī anṟu** – you gave nectar to dēvas who dwell in *suvargam (*heaven). dēvas are referred to as *andarattār* since they are neither in samsāram which is down below nor in śrivaikuṇṭam which is far above, but somewhere in between the two, like a loft for samsāram fastening themselves to samsāram (remaining in samsāram by taking nectar, without having to face death).

**meymmaiyē** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) is amazed that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) truly swallowed the worlds during the time of deluge, protecting them, and churned the oceans to offer nectar to dēvas, without any trace of magic.

We will take up the 34<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 34. Anrivvulagam

*anṟivvulagam aḷanda asaivē kol ninṟirundu vēḷukkai nīṇagar vāy anṟu kidandānaik kēdil sīrānai mun kanjaik kadandānai nenjamē kāṇ*

**Пословный перевод:**

nenjamē – Oh heart!; anṟu – once upon a time; ninṟu – standing; ivvulagam – all these worlds; aḷanda – of measuring; asavē kol – is it due to exhaustion; vēḷukkai – at tiruvēḷukkai (a divine abode in kāncīpuram); irundu – in sitting posture; nīḷ nagar vāy – in the great divine abode (of tiruvehkā, another divine abode in kāncīpuram); anṟu – once upon a time; kidandānai – one who took reclining posture; kēdu il sīrānai – one who has auspicious qualities which will never get destroyed; mun – once upon a time; kanjan – kamsan; kadandānai – emperumān who killed; kāṇ – keep meditating

**Перевод:**

**anṟu ivvulagam aḷanda asaivē kol – is it the fatigue of leaping onto the skies and measuring the worlds, in standing posture, when the worlds did not even think of \[asking for the help\]!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār, remembering emperumān’s act of measuring the worlds, thinks that he is standing, sitting, lying in various divine abodes in kāncīpuram, probably due to the fatigue that he had while carrying out that activity and gets fearful. Since the purpose of his dwelling \[in various divine abodes\] is only for the sake of others, he tells his heart to desire to see him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**irundu vēḷukkai** – dwelling in sitting posture in tiruvēḷukkai

**nīṇagar vāy anṟu kidandānai** – one who took a reclining posture in the huge town of [tiruvehkā](http://thiruvekka.koyil.org/). He stood up to measure the worlds. Since the divine feet were hurting, he sat (in the abode tiruvēḷukkai). Since even sitting was hurting, he reclined (in tiruvehkā).

**kēdil sīrānai** – one who has auspicious qualities forever.

**mun kanjaik kadandānai** – one who removed the enmity which came from kamsa, once upon a time. One who caused harm to kamsa and killed him when kamsa thought of harming and killing him.

**nenjamē kāṇ** – Oh heart! Will you not think \[of these activities\]? Why are you thinking of other activities instead of meditating on these? Is it proper not to meditate on [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is transforming himself for our sake and carrying out such activities? [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) tells his heart to think of emperumān who is sitting at tiruvēḷukkai and reclining at tiruvehKa, probably due to the tiredness of measuring all the worlds.

We will move on to the 35<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 35. Kan kan ena

*kāṇ kāṇ ena virumbum kaṇgaḷ kadirilagu pūṇḍāragalattān ponmēni pāṇkaṇ toḻilpādi vaṇḍaṟaiyum tongalān sempoṟ kaḻalpādi yām toḻudum kai*

**Пословный перевод:**

kaṇgaḷ – my eyes; kadir ilagu – being radiant; pūṇ – divine ornaments; tār – garlands; agalattān – emperumān who is having \[the aforementioned\] on his divine chest; pon mēni – golden hued divine form; kāṇ kāṇ ena virumbum – will keep desiring repeatedly, to see; vaṇḍu – beetles; aṟaiyum – humming; tongalān – emperumān who is donning those garlands; toḻil – activities; pān kaṇ pādi – singing to a nice tune; sem pon kaḻal – reddish divine feet; yām – we; kai – with hands; pādi toḻudum – will worship, singing

**Перевод:**

**kadir ilagu pūṇ tār agalattāṇ pon mēni kāṇ kāṇ ena virumbum kangaḷ – look at the beautiful form of emperumān. Is this form such that I have to instruct you to see it? (Should you not have the desire to see it!)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār had instructed his heart to see emperumān. The other sensory perceptions started urging āḻvār. The other sensory perceptions will function only with the help of mind. Now, it has so happened that the other sensory perceptions have started urging him directly \[in all divyaprabandams of āḻvārs, the words heart and mind are used interchangeably\]. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kadir ilagu pūṇ** – ornaments which are radiant. Ornaments which are shining.

**tār agalattān** – *tār* means garland. One who has a broad chest donning garland

**pon mēni** – beautiful divine form. It is implied that it is not the fault of the sensory organs to have the desire to see him. During famine, just as children keep asking for food repeatedly, the eyes keep throbbing for seeing the divine form of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

**pāṇ kaṇ toḻil pādi** – singing his activities through songs. Alternatively, singing in the style of pāṇars (pāṇars are a class of people who engage in singing, constantly). ōr, singing in such a way that this itself becomes the profession.

**vaṇḍaṟaiyum tongalān** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who has garland in which beetles come humming \[for extracting honey\]. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) implies that his sensory organs will remain without engaging with emperumān only if beetles can remain without engaging \[with his garlands\].

**sempoṟkaḻal pādi yām toḻudum kai** – it is impossible not to get involved with such beauty. Hence, since it is rare for us to take residence under such divine form, we shall praise his divine feet fully with our mouth and worship with our hands, says [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/).

We will take up the 36<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 36. Kaiya kanalali

*kaiya kanalāḻi kārkkadalvāy veṇsangam veyyagadai sārngam venjudar vāḷ seyya padai paravai pāḻi pani nīr ulagam adiyaḷanda māyar avarkku*

**Пословный перевод:**

pani nīr ulagam – world surrounded by cool waters; adi – with his divine feet; aḷanda – one who measured; māyar avarkku – for that emperumān who has amazing activities; kaiya – in the hand; kanal āḻi – the divine disc which is radiant; kār kadal vāy veṇ sangam – the white coloured divine conch which appeared in the dark ocean; veyya – valorous; gadai – mace; (veyya) – valorous; sārngam – the bow sārngam; vem sudar vāḷ – the sword nāndagam which has radiant glow; seyya padai – are the divine weapons; paravai – the ocean; pāḻi – is the mattress

**Перевод:**

**kaiya kanal āḻi – the cakrāyudam (divine disc) which spits fire on enemies.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār said yām toḻudum (we will worship with our hands). Just as he desired, he enjoys emperumān in this pāsuram. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kārkkadal vāy veṇ sangam** – the conch *pāncajanyam* which has the roar of clouds, has the ocean as its place of birth and has white complexion. ōr, the conch which makes furious sound similar to the cloud and ocean. ōr, the divine conch which has as its place of birth, the dark coloured ocean.

**veyya gadai sārngam** – the valorous mace and the valorous bow.

**venjudarvāḷ** – the divine sword which glows with radiance.

All  these are

**seyya padai** – great weapons. The term *seyya padai* refers to the weapons which are appropriate for eliminating the enemies of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s followers. If these weapons are considered as ornaments, the word *seyya* will refer to their radiance. Since these weapons are present, there is no need to praise emperumān to live long. It is enough to praise the weapons as said in tiruvāimoḻi 7-4-1 “*tisai vāḻi eḻa*” (let the sounds of praise rise in all directions). [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) too, in his [tiruppallāṇḍu](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruppallandu/) mercifully said “*sudarāḻiyum pallāṇḍu; pāncasanniyamum pallāṇḍu* ” (praise to the divine radiant disc; praise to the divine conch), praising the weapons.

**kaiya** – all these are in his hands

**paravai pāḻi** – the milky ocean is his mattress

**pani nīr ulagam adi aḷanda māyar avarkku** – for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who measured the world which is surrounded by cool waters with his divine feet, disc, conch, mace, bow and sword  are the beautiful divine weapons.

We will move on to the 37<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 37. Avarkkadimai patten

*avarkkadimai pattēn agattān puṟattān uvarkkum karungadal nīruḷḷān tuvarkkum pavaḷavāyp pūmagaḷum panmaṇippūṇāram tigaḻum tirumārvan tān*

**Пословный перевод:**

avarkku – to that emperumān \[as described in the previous pāsurams\]; adimai pattēn – I became a servitor; uvarkkum karungadal nīr uḷḷān – he reclines on the dark ocean with salty water; tuvarkkum pavaḷa vāy pū magaḷum – pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi) who has reddish coral-like mouth; pal maṇi pūṇ – many ornaments with different types of gemstones; āram – necklaces; tigaḻum – being adorned with; tirumārvan tān – emperumān with such divine chest; agattān puṟattān – is present both within and outside (of me)

**Перевод:**

**avarkku adimai pattēn – I became a servitor to emperumān. I told him in a superficial way, without feeling wholeheartedly, that I have become his servitor. I said only this much.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that he, in a way, became a servitor at emperumān’s divine feet. He says that even though he did not consider emperumān as a desirable entity, emperumān considers him as a desirable person. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**agattān puṟattān** – What did emperumān do when I said this? The minute I became his servitor, emperumān pervaded me, both from within and outside equally, just like the sage saubhari, and started enjoying me. He became powerless to move away from me.

**uvarkkum karungadal nīr uḷḷān** – It is said that apart from tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean) [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) reclines in the oceans which surround the earth. It is said that he reclines in all oceans which are great. When he churned tiruppāṟkadal, haven’t \[knowledgeable\] people mercifully stated that he had churned other oceans too, such as in [tiruppāvai 30](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/01/thiruppavai-30-vangak-kadal-kadaindha-and-vazhi-thirunamam-etc/) “*vangakkadal kadainda*”! We can also construe this to mean that he has taken tiruppāṟkadal as his dwelling place. (he is reclining in the dark, salty oceans surrounding the earth. Has it not been mentioned in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 2-6-6 “*neelak kadalil nedungālam kaṇvaḷarndān*” (he reclined in the bluish ocean for a very long time)).

**tuvarkkum pavaḷa vāy pū magaḷum** – periya pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi) who has lips which are like reddish coral. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) says that the reason for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) pervading him is only to get the approval of periya pirātti, who is happy due to this \[act of entering me\], through her smile.

**panmaṇip pūṇ āram tigaḻum tirumārvan tān** – emperumān has divine chest which is decorated with ornaments made of the nine gemstones and necklaces. Alternatively, *panmaṇippūṇāram* could be considered as one word and the meaning would be that he has necklace made of different types of gems.

**agattān puṟattān** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who has a divine chest which is sweet and appropriate for periya pirātti, remains within and outside of [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) and is overjoyed that he has got something which he couldn’t ever get. Is it not such emperumān who has such ornaments, greatness and the affection of [pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/), who has come with desire, inside and outside of āḻvār! āḻvār is very joyous that emperumān has such affection for him.

## 38. Tane tanakkuvaman

*tānē tanakkuvaman tannuruvē evvuruvum tānē tava uruvum tāragaiyum tānē erisudarum mālvaraiyum eṇḍhisaiyum aṇḍattu irusudarumāya iṟai*

**Пословный перевод:**

evvuruvum – all the entities \[both sentient and insentient\]; than uruvē – will be part of his physical form; tavam uruvum – entities such as brahmā et al, who carry out penance for a long time and obtain exalted physical forms; tāragaiyum – the stars; tānē – will be part of his physical form; eri sudarum – the glowing agni (fire); māl varaiyum – the huge mountains \[which sustain the earth\]; eṇ disaiyum – all the directions; aṇḍattu – belonging to this universe; irusudarum – sun and moon; tānē āya iṟai – emperumān, who has all these as his physical body; tanakku tānē uvaman – is a simile for himself (since nothing else can be compared to him)

**Перевод:**

**tānē tanakku uvaman – he can be compared only to himself. There is none other than himself for comparison. Another entity can be compared to him only if there is another īśvaran! Hence, only he can be compared to himself.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār when asked whether the entity who had entered him vilfully, both inside and outside, is a simple person, says that he is the supreme being! Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**than uruvē evvuruvum** – bruhadāraṇyaka upaniśat 5-7 says “*yasyātmā śarīram yasya prutivī śarīram*”; śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-22-86 says “*tāni sarvāṇi tadvapu:*”’ śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-22-38 says “*tat sarvam vai harēs tanu:* “; śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 20-26 says “*jagatsarvam śarīram tē*”. As mentioned by all these authentic sources, he has the entire universes as his physical form. The reason for having none to be compared with him is because he is the substrate for all other entities and he does not have a substrate for himself. In other words, all other entities are his physical body.

**tānē tava uruvum** – all the higher level puruśas (souls) such as brahmā et al are his attributes, since they attained their exalted physical forms after carrying out penance \[directed at him\] for a long time. mahābhāratam sabhā parvam 14-8 says “*yugakōti sahasrāṇi viṣṇumārādya padmabhū: punas trailōkya dātrutvam prāptavāniti śuśruma:*” (we hear that brahmā carried out penance for thousands of yugams (ages) towards viṣṇu and vorśipped him and attained the post of brahmā, the head of the three worlds). The same is true of rudra too, as mentioned in mahābhāratam śānti parvam 20-12 “*mahādēva: sarvamēdē mahātmā hutvātmānam dēvadēvō babhūva*” (Sivan, the mahādēva (greatest among celestial entities) offered himself in the sarvarmēda yāgam (a sacrificial ritual) and became the head of celestial entities). Thus, all these entities obtained exalted physical forms after carrying out penance over a long period of time towards [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and became part of his physical form.

**tāragaiyum tānē** – stars are also his attributes. By mentioning brahmā and Siva in the previous verse and stars in this verse, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) says that both sentient entities and insentient entities are his physical form, without any distinction.

**tānē erisudarum** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is the glowing fire

**māl varaiyum** – he is the great mountains \[which sustain the world\]

**eṇ disaiyum** – he is all the eight directions

**aṇḍattu** – all the universes which are made of the five elements

**iru sudarum āya ivai** – light emitting objects such as sun and moon in each of these universes are his attributes and are under his control.

**iṟai** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is all these entities, is his own equivalent \[and there is none to compare with him\].

We will move on to the 39<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 39. Iraiyay nilanagi

*iṟaiyāy nilanāgi eṇḍhisaiyum tānāy maṟaiyāy maṟaipporuḷāy vānāy piṟai vāynda veḷḷattaruvi viḷangoli nīr vēngadattān uḷḷanttinuḷḷe uḷan*

**Пословный перевод:**

iṟaiyāy – being the lord of all; nilan āgi – being the indvelling soul for earth; eṇ disaiyum tān āy – pervading all the entities in the eight directions; maṟai āy – being established by vēdams (sacred texts); maṟaipporul āy – being the meanings of vēdams; vān āy – being the controller of śrīvaikuṇṭam; piṟay vāynda – rising till the lunar region; veḷḷam aruvi viḷangu – being manifested by streams which have copious water; oli nīr – having resounding streams; vēngadattān – emperumān who is residing at tiruvēngadam; uḷḷattinuḷḷē uḷan – is residing inside my heart

**Перевод:**

**iṟaiyāy – being the lord of both nityavibhūti (śrīvaikuṇṭam) and leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that emperumān, who has the entire world as his divine form, has taken residence in tiruvēngadamalai (tirumalai hills), to be close, and is staying without leaving my heart. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nilanāgi** – pervading throughout the earth

**eṇḍhisaiyum tānāy** – being the controller of the eight directions and the entities which reside in these directions

**maṟaiyāy** – being the vēdams which establish him truly.

**maṟaipporuḷāy** – being the meaning of such vēdams. Being the secrets of the sacred texts and being established by them.

**vānāy** – he has paramapadam, also referred to as *paramākāśam* (śrīvaikuṇṭam) as his attribute. He has everything as his attribute. Thus it is implied that he is the indestructible lord.

**piṟai vāynda** – tiruvēngadamalai which rises up to the lunar region.

**veḷḷattu aruvi** – streams with copious water.

**viḷangoli nīr vēngadattān** – vēngadamalai which is manifested by such streams which make lot of noise (due to the rush of water flowing). He has as his residence the tiruvēngadamalai which has such resources.

**uḷḷattin uḷḷē uḷan** – Residing at tirumalai, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) was waiting for an apt moment to enter my heart and at the opportune time, entered it. It appears that emperumān residing at tiruvēngadam was only to wait for the opportune time to enter [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/)’s heart.

**uḷḷattin uḷḷē uḷan** – Just as it is mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 1-10 “*divyajñāna upapannās tē rāmam druśtvā maharśaya:*” (the great sages, with divine knowledge, saw śrī rāma) [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) says that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who cannot easily be seen, became visible to him after reaching tiruvēngadam.

We will take up the 40<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 40. Ulan kanday

*uḷan kaṇḍāy nannenjē uttaman enṟum uḷan kaṇḍāy uḷḷuvār uḷḷattu uḷan kaṇḍāy viṇṇodungak kōduyarum vīngaruvi vēngadattān maṇṇodungat tānaḷanda man*

**Пословный перевод:**

nannenjē – Oh my good heart, which made him \[emperumān\] also to exist \[within you\]; viṇ odunga – making the worlds above to appear to be in a corner; kōdu – peaks; uyarum – having them to be tall; vīngu aruvi – having lots of streams; vēngadattān – one who is residing in tiruvēngadam; maṇ – the entire surface of earth; odunga – to make it appear to be in a corner (of his divine foot); tān aḷanda – one who measured it; man – the king; uḷan kaṇḍāy – you can see that he exists, since he is protecting us; uttaman – that emperumān who is puruśōttaman (best among all souls); enṟum uḷan kaṇḍāy – you can see that he exists at all times (with a vow to protect us); uḷḷuvār uḷḷattu – in the minds of those who think of him; uḷan kaṇḍāy – you can see that he resides permanently

**Перевод:**

**uḷan kaṇḍāy – āḻvār says that emperumān did not exist before and he started existing now.  Before, when emperumān had not attained him, he did not exist, as mentioned in taittirīya upaniśat “asannēva sa bhavati”. Now, after attaining us, he exists, says āḻvār. We do not have to fear that we are not for ourselves. We can be bold that he \[emperumān\] is for us. Just as we exist to create destruction for ourselves, he exists to protect us. Our existence is the reason for his existence. Just as it is mentioned in śrīvaikuṇṭastavam 36 “iccāta Eva tava viśvapadārta sattā” (all entities get their existence due to your desire), he makes us exist and makes himself to exist.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār tells his heart that there is no deficiency in him after emperumān, who is superior to everyone else, entered his heart. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nal nenjē** – Oh my heart, only after becoming non-malevolent towards you \[in other words, friendly towards you\], did he also become existing. Both non-malevolence and existence happened simultaneously. Only now you are in a state as mentioned in taittirīya upaniśat 6 “*asti brahmēti cēt vēda santamēnam tatō vidu:*” (only if a person gets knowledge about brahmam (supreme being) will he be considered as existing).

**uttaman** – he is considering our protection as his benefit. Just as for all the persons other than [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), destroying themselves is a characteristic, for emperumān making us subservient to him is a characteristic.

**enṟum uḷan kaṇḍāy** – even during times when we do not need him, he desires us. Even when we do not think that he is the reason for our existence, he thinks of us.

**uḷḷuvār uḷḷattu uḷan kaṇḍān** – All that we have to do is just this – *think of him*. He exists in the hearts of those who do not shun him when he is thinking of entering their minds.  Alternatively, we can consider this to mean that it is enough if we know \[that he exists\].

**viṇ odungak kōdu uyarum** – the hills of tiruvēngadamalai are such that it will appear that the sky has shrunk. They will be so tall that other worlds and certain limits do not exist and that they are not needed; they will rise beyond all limits making even sky etc to get shrunk. Alternatively, if we consider *viṇ* to mean paramapadam, these peaks will rise to that extent that even paramapadam would appear to shrink in size.

**vīngaruvi vēngadattān** – he has as his residence tiruvēngadam which has streams with copious flow of water.

**maṇṇodungat tān aḷanda man** – “**maṇṇodunga”** making the earth to shrink under the divine feet. When he measured the worlds, he spread out his feet such that only his feet were visible and the earth was not.

**man** *–* he avoided twin-rulers by making it clear that he is the only ruler and there is no one else. He is the owner of all these possessions. It appears that since he reclaimed the kingdom that indra had lost and since he seized that from mahābali, he is the owner of that kingdom. Existence of [jīvātmā (soul)](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) will create a fear within jīvātmā whereas existence of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) will remove that fear for jīvātmā. draupadi, when she was dragged by her hair, complained to kriṣṇa in mahābhāratam uttara parvam 83 “*sāham kēśagraham prāptā tavyi jīvatyapi prabhō*” (Oh lord, even when you existed, I was dragged by my hair by my enemies. There is none who is my protector, other than you. You also did not protect me now. Oh emperumān! Can you connect your existence and the insult which happened to me? Would I have to weep like this if you are also like me and my husbands (as a burden for the earth))? Just as it is mentioned in jitantē ślōkam 5 “*na tē rūpam na cākāra: …… bhaktānām tvam prakāśasē*” (your divine form is not for yourself…. you have them all for the sake of your followers), it will be no wonder if one expresses displeasure when he does not help.

We will proceed to the 41<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 42. Kovalanay aniraigal

*kōvalanāy āniraigaḷ mēyttuk kuḻal ūdi māvalanāyk kīṇḍa maṇivaṇṇan mēvi ariyuruvamāgi iraṇiyadu āgam teriyugirāl kīṇḍān sinam*

**Пословный перевод:**

kōvalan āy – being born as a covherd; āniraigal – herds of cows; mēyttu – grazing them (by taking them to places which have grass and water); kuḻal ūdi – playing the flute (to bring the cows together); mā – the demon kēśi who came in the form of a horse; valan āy kīṇḍa – one who tore it capably; maṇi vaṇṇan – one who has the form of a blue coloured gem; mēvi ari uruvam āgi – appropriately taking the form of narasimha (combination of lion face and human body); iraṇiyadu āgam – the chest of demon iraṇiyan (hiraṇya kaśyap); ugirāl – with his finger nails; kīṇḍa – emperumān who tore; sinam – anger; teri – (Oh heart) know it

**Перевод:**

**kōvalanāy āniraigaḷ mēyttuk kuḻal ūdi – born in the clan of covherds in order to make himself appropriate for herding cows, he grazed the cows by playing on the flute. Just as a brāhmaṇa recites vēdam (sacred text), he grazed the cows, which is appropriate for one born in the clan of covherds and played on the flute. He carried out acts which are apt for the clan.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the same breath as when he spoke of emperumān keeping all the worlds under his divine feet, āḻvār speaks about the removal of enmities which happened to one town (tiruvāyppādi or gōkulam) and protection of covherds. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**mā valanāyk kīṇḍa** – even when he had not attained the apt age, he tore the mouth of a demon kēśi who had come in the form of a horse. Instead of stepping back on seeing the demon who came in the form of a horse, he stood face to face, with dexterity, and tore his mouth.

**mā valanāyk kīṇḍa** – he disproved the thinking of kēśi who thought after looking at the softness of kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) that he \[kaṇṇan\] was incapable of killing him \[kēśi\].

**maṇivaṇṇan** – because he destroyed the enemy, his form became such that it removed the fatigue of the beholder. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) celebrates the beautiful form of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) which he had when he decimated kēśi by referring to him as *maṇivaṇṇan*.

**mēvi ari uruvamāgi** – fitting well with the form of narasimha. In other words, while the combination of lion and human would be something which cannot gel well and be incongruent, that form was so homogeneous that the two forms merged well. Just as adding candy to well boiled milk would enhance sweetness, the form of narasimha was also very appropriate. Alternatively *iraṇiyadu āgam mēvi* could be considered as approaching iraṇiyan.

**iraṇiyadu āgam ugirāḷ kīṇḍān sinam** – he tore the tough physical form of iraṇiyan with his finger nails.

**sinam teri** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) tells his heart to think of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s anger and determine that what we need is not his grace but the anger that he has on the enemies of his followers and think of that anger. Alternatively, *teri ugir* could be construed as broad nail.

We will take up the 43<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 43. Sinama madakalirrin

*sinamā madakaḷiṟṟin tiṇ maruppaic cāyttu punamēya bhūmi adanai danamāgap pēragalattuḷḷodukkum pērāra mārvanār ōragalatuḷ uḷḷadu ulagu*

**Пословный перевод:**

sinam mā mada – being angry and having huge exultation; kaḷiṟṟin – the elephant (kuvalayāpīdam); tiṇ maruppai – its strong tusks; sāyttu – one who broke it; punam mēya – being together with arable land; bhūmi adanai – the earth; danam āga – considering it as a gift; pēr agalattuḷ odukkum – one who protected by keeping in his expansive stomach; pēr āram mārvanār – emperumān, who has donned huge chains on his chest, his; ōr agalattu – in a part of his knowledge, which is in sankalparūpam (in the form of his will or solemn vow); ulagu uḷḷadu – all the worlds are existing

**Перевод:**

**sina mā mada kaḷiṟṟin tiṇ maruppaic cāyttu – breaking the tusks of the elephant kuvalayāpīdam which was angry, huge, in exultation and not knowing what is going on around it; snatching it effortlessly.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār wonders whether emperumān protected only one prahlāda. He says that when the danger crosses limits, doesn’t he protect everyone by keeping everyone inside him! āren’t the worlds existing merely by his sankalpam (will)! Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**punamēya bhūmi adanai** – the earth which has arable land, which could be enjoyed by the sentient entities \[by cultivating\]

**danamāga** – considering it as a wealth

**bhūmiyadanait tanamāga** – did he not protect earth which has forests and streams \[implying ups and downs or in other words, rough terrain\] as a great wealth!

**pēragalattu uḷ odukkum** – one who has the ability to protect it inside his stomach which \[stomach\] is so huge that even after keeping these worlds inside his stomach, it could be said that there was still a lot of space.

**pēr Ara mārvanār** – even when he swallows all the worlds, he does it so capably that his decorations do not get disturbed. His qualities are also equally beautiful. It is implied here that even as he protected the worlds which were his possessions, it appeared that he had decked himself up with ornaments out of that happiness.

**ōr agalattuḷ uḷḷadu ulagu** – the worlds are in his incomparable, immeasurable *sankalpa* (will / thought). We can also construe that the worlds are in the memory of his knowledge which is in the form of his will. While he can protect the worlds through his mere thoughts, the fact that he physically carried out the act by keeping them in his stomach indicates the affection that he has for his followers.

We will take up the 44<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 44. Ulagum ulagiranda

*ulagamum ulagaiṟanda ūḻiyum oṇkēḻ alar kadirum sendīyumāvān palakadirgaḷ pāritta paimpon mudiyān adi iṇaikkē pūritten nenjē puri*

**Пословный перевод:**

en nenjē – Oh my heart!; ulagamum – the worlds; ūḻiyum – deluge; āḻiyum – the oceans; oṇ kēḻ alar kadirum – moon and sun who have beautiful colour and radiant rays; sem tīyum – fire with reddish colour; āvān – one who has all the above as his attributes; pala kadirgaḷ pāritta – one who lets go of many rays; paimpon mudiyān – emperumān who has golden hued locks; adi iṇaikkē – for his divine feet; pūrittu puri – desire wholeheartedly

**Перевод:**

**ulagamum – all the worlds**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār implores his heart to attain emperumān who is the lord of both vibhūtis. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ūḻiyum** – the remaining period after all the entities have been destroyed

**āḻiyum** – oceans

**oṇ kēḻ alar kadirum** – moon and sun who have beautiful coloured radiant rays. We can also construe the meaning as beautifully blooming sun and moon.

**sendīyum āvān** – emperumān, apart from all the above mentioned entities,  is also fire which is reddish. He has all these as his attributes. Thus, it is opined here that all the worlds are part of emperumān’s form.

**pala kadirgaḷ pāritta paimpon mudiyān** – this describes his status in paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam). Just as it is mentioned in [śrī bhagavat gītā 11-12](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/11-12/) “*divi sūryasya bhavēt yugapaduttitā yadi bhā: sadruśī sā syāt bhāsas tasya mahātmana:*” (if a thousand suns rise in the sky together, the combined brightness of all these may probably match the radiance of paramātmā (emperumān)), one who has a beautiful crown which emits the radiance of hundreds of thousands of suns.

**paimpon mudi** – the locks which are brighter than *divi sūrya* mentioned in the previous paragraph. There is no equivalent for this.

**adi iṇaikkē** – his two divine feet which match each other totally.

**pūrittu en nenjē puri** – āḻvār tells his heart to attain completely with happiness, just like people who are riding on top of elephant will be.

**pūrittu** – āḻvār implores his heart to attain emperumān to get rid of the sorrows of having forgotten him for a very long time.

**paimpon mudi** – even as we sight his divine crown, we would feel like falling at his divine feet. Thus, his divine crown lets the beholder to fall at the divine feet. āḻvar tells his heart to know that emperumān is the only entity who is apt for carrying out our tasks and tells it to fall at emperumān’s divine feet, entrusting him with all the responsibility and be like one who has completed the assigned task.

We will take up the 45<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.
