## 45. Purindu mada velam

*purindu mada vēḻam māppidiyōdūdit tirindu sinattāl porudu virinda sīr veṇkōttu muttu udirkkum vēngadamē mēlorunāl maṇkōttuk koṇḍān malai*

**Пословный перевод:**

mada vēḻam – male elephant which is in exultation; mā pidiyōdu – with its great female elephant; purindu – engaging in union; ūdi – (after that) engaging in love-quarrel; tirindu – (due to that separating from its female and) wandering; sinattāl porudu – hitting (against gem rocks) in anger; virinda sīr – having the wealth of valour; veṇ kōdu – from its white tusks; muttu – pearls; udirkkum – will shed; vēngadamē – tirumalai hills; mēl oru nāḷ – at an earlier point of time; maṇ – earth; kōdu – on its tusks; koṇḍān – one who had it; malai – divine hills

**Перевод:**

**mada vēḻam – an exulting elephant**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*After saying in the previous pāsuram “pala kadirgaḷ pāritta paimpon mudiyān” that he is the lord of paramapadam, āḻvār wants to attain him; if he were to attain him, he has to see him! But then, will emperumān become so very simple that we could see him? āḻvār says that only to remove that shortcoming, he has taken residence, close to us, at tirumalai, as a simple entity, so that we could see him with our eyes. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**māppidiyōdu ūdi** – engaging in love-quarrel with its female elephant, knowing well that the female is capable of making it (the male elephant) carry out tasks that it thinks.

**purindu** – after the love quarrel, engaging in union

**tirundu** – the male elephant will wander about as a result of its pride over that union

**ūdi māppidiyōdu** – the greatness of the female was the cause for the male to engage in love-quarrel.

**tirindu** – wandering in orchards

**sinattāl porudu** – looking at its own shadow on the rocks, mistaking the shadow for another elephant, it will become angry and hit against the rocks. It will remain in that posture to manifest its valour fully.

There is another way to interpret the meaning:

**madavēḻam māppidiyōdu purindu ūdi** – the male elephant will engage in love-quarrel after its union with the female.

**tirindu sinattāl porudu** – because of that quarrel, the male elephant will wander here and there and hit against the gem rocks in the hills. Just as it has been mentioned in [śrī bhagavat gītā 2-62](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/2-62/) “*kāmāt krōdōbhijāyatē*” (infatuation gives birth to anger), hitting against the rocks due to that anger.

**virinda sīr** – the valour which the elephant had because of hitting \[against the rocks\] like this. Just as sītāppirātti happily embraced śrī rāma after seeing his valour when he killed demons such as kara , dūśa et al, the female elephant would put an end to the love quarrel and embrace the male elephant.

**virinda sīr veṇkōttu muttu udirkkum** – [nanjīyar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nanjiyar/), the ācārya of [nampiḷḷai](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nampillai/) \[who has written this commentary\] would mercifully say: it is that tirumalai where the elephant which has wealth of valour would shed pearls formed in its tusks \[from hitting against the gem rocks\].

**mēl oru nāḷ maṇ kōttuk koṇḍān** – isn’t tirumalai, which has such benefits, the land which is the dwelling place of one who once upon a time dug out the earth and kept in on his tusks! It is the hill belonging to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) when he took the form of śrī varāha \[wild boar\] and held bhūmip pirātti (earth) on his tusks. The elephants in tirumalai complement the divine form of śrī varāha. The act of elephant in exultation, hitting against the gem rock with the pride of valour and shedding pearls from its tusks reminds one of the act of śrī varāha in digging out earth and sustaining her on his tusks. [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) too has mercifully mentioned in periya tirumoḻi 3-4-3 “*varāhamonṟāy maṇṇellām idandeduttu madangaḷ seydu*” (digging out earth as the unique varāha and engaging in exultation with pride).

**malai** – just as one says that he has this mountain, this itself is his wealth.

We will take up the 46<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 46. Malai mugadu

*malai mugadu mēl vaittu vāsugiyaic cuṟṟi talai mugadu tānoru kai paṟṟi alai mugattu aṇḍam pōy nīr teṟippa anṟu kadal kadaindān piṇḍamāy ninṟa pirān*

**Пословный перевод:**

piṇḍam āy ninṟa pirān – emperumān who is the material cause \[for the creation of the worlds\]; anṟu – once upon a time; mugadu malai – the mountain mantaram with peaks; mēl vaittu – keeping it atop (himself in the form of tortoise); vāsugaiyaic cuṟṟi – coiling the snake vāsugi (around that mountain as rope for churning); talai mugadu – the tallest peak \[of mantaram\]; tān oru kai paṟṟi – holding it with one of his hands; alai mugattu nīr – the droplets of water on top of the waves; aṇḍam pōy teṟippa – to hit against the walls of the universe; kadal kadaindān – he churned the ocean

**Перевод:**

**malai mugadu mēl vaittu – keeping the mountain mantaram with its tall peak on top of himself (in the form of kūrma, tortoise)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that emperumān who has taken residence at tirumalai, close to us, will also fulfil the desires of those who desire other benefits. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vāsugiyaic cuṟṟi** – coiling the snake vāsugi around the middle of *mantaram* as a rope for churning \[the ocean\].

**talai mugadu tān oru kai paṟṟi** – just as he took the form of a tortoise and sat under the mountain so that it would not sink during the churning process, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) pressed his hand on top of the peak to support it so that the mountain will not get toppled over during the churning process.

**alai mugattu nīr teṟippa anṟu kadal kadaindān** – he churned the ocean such vigor that the water droplets on top of the waves went all the way to the walls of the universe and banged against them.

Who is the entity who churned the ocean such that the water from the waves hit against the walls of the universe?

**piṇḍamāy ninṟa pirān** – the one who is the cause (for all these – the world, ocean, mountain etc). Just as clay, which is the previous state for pot, lid etc, is the cause for these products, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is the cause for the worlds. mahōpaniśat states “*ēkō ha vai nārāyaṇa āsīt*” (only nārāyaṇa was present before creation).

**pirān** – one who is the benefactor. When the worlds, which are the resultant factors, were destroyed and he alone remained, he is the benefactor who created these again so that they will reap the benefits. He is the one who is affectionate towards the worlds which he created.

**piṇḍamāy ninṟa pirān** – should not the father, who sired the children, provide for their sustenance? He churned the oceans only for that and gave nectar \[to celestial entities\].

**pirān** – while he could have carried out the task of protecting the worlds through his mere *sankalpa* (vow/will/thought), did he not churn the ocean, hurting his divine form in the bargain! [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) engages with this characteristic of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) saying “How is this simplicity!”

We will take up the 47<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 47. Ninra perumane

*ninṟa perumānē nīrēṟṟu ulagellām senṟa perumānē sengaṇṇā anṟu turagavāy kīṇḍa tuḻāy mudiyāy nangaḷ naragavāy kīṇḍāyum nī*

**Пословный перевод:**

nīr ēṟṟu ninṟa perumānē – Oh emperumān, who stood near mahābali to take the water as symbolic of taking alms!; ulagu ellām senṟa perumānē – Oh emperumān who measured all the worlds!; sem kaṇṇā – Oh one who has lotus like eyes!; anṟu – at an earlier point of time; turagam – the demon kēsi who took the form of a horse; vāy – his mouth; kīṇḍa – one who tore it and killed the demon; tuḻāy mudiyāy – Oh one who wears the tuḷasi garland on top of your crown!; nangaḷ – our; naragam vāy – way to samsāram which is like hell for us; kīṇḍāyum nī – was it not you alone who destroyed this also!

**Перевод:**

**ninṟa perumānē nīrēṟṟu – emperumān, who is avāptasamasta kāman (one who has all his desires already fulfilled), went to mahābali and stretched his hand towards him for taking water, which is symbolic of accepting alms from him.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that it is emperumān, who fulfilled the desires of celestial entities, who also removes the enemies of lowly entities such as ourselves and makes us his servitors. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ninṟa perumānē** – one who stood as trivikrama, manifesting his supremacy, after receiving alms.

**ulagellām senṟa perumānē** – As soon as mahābali poured water on his hand, he grew to make it appear that he himself is all the worlds, and measured the worlds. He stood such that he is the possessor of all the worlds!

**sengaṇṇā** – his eyes became red due to the happiness of having removed the shortcoming of indra!

**anṟu turaga vāy kīṇḍa tuḻāy mudiyāy** – when the demon kēśi came in the form of a horse, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) killed him without his decorations getting disturbed. It is implied that the ring of tuḷasi which was adorning his divine crown did not change position even a little bit when he killed kēśi.

**nangaḷ naraga vāy kīṇḍāyum nī** – For the one who tore apart the mouth of hell for us (samsāram), is it a wonder that you tore the mouth of kēśi! For those with superior knowledge (of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) life in samsāram is the true hell. Did not sītāppirātti tell śrī rāma in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 30-18 “*nirayō yastvayā vinā*” (being separated from you is hell)!

We will move on to the 48<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 48. Niyanre nirerru

*nīyanṟē nīrēṟṟu ulagam adi aḷandāy nīyanṟē ninṟu nirai mēyttāy niyanṟē māvāyuram piḷandu māmarudinūdu pōyt tēvāsuram porudāy seṟṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

nīr ēṟṟu – accepting water as symbolic of alms (from mahābali); ulagam – all the worlds; adi aḷandāy nī anṟē – was it not you who measured with your divine feet?; ninṟu – being focussed on protection; nirai – herds of cows; mēyttāy nī anṟē – was it not you who grazed them?; mā – the demon kēśi who came in the form of a horse; vāy uram – the strength of his mouth; piḷandu – destroying it; mā marudin ūdu pōy – going between the twin arjuna trees and uprooting them; dēvāsuram – in the war between celestial and demonic entities; seṟṟu – destroying (the demons); porudāy nī anṟē – was it not you who waged the war!

**Перевод:**

**nīyanṟē nīr ērru ulagam adi aḷandāy – was it not you who went as a mendicant to mahābali to take as alms the worlds which are your possessions, stretched out your hand to accept water that he poured, and measured the worlds with your divine feet! Did I carry out this task? ōr did indra carry out this?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*emperumān tells āḻvār “Oh āḻvīr! I am not aware of all these matters which you are saying” to which āḻvār responds saying “Oh emperumān! Then, who else carried out all these activities!”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nīyanṟē ninṟu nirai mēyttāy** – was it not you who incarnated as kriṣṇa \[in the clan of covherds\] and followed the cows with a stick in hand in order to graze them! Did I carry out this activity or did someone else do this?

**nīyanṟē māvāy uram piḷandu** – was it not you who tore the mouth of kēśi, the demon, who came in the form of a horse! (*mā vāy uram piḷandu* – he tore the mouth of kēśi such that the strength of the mouth was demolished) That he carried out that task effortlessly when he went to graze the cows is mentioned here.

**māmarudin ūdu pōy** – when two demons, yamaḷa and arjuna, stood in the form of two huge arjuna trees, you crawled in between the two trees and uprooted them, thus escaping from that danger too.

**dēvāsuram porudāy seṟṟu** – was it only the insentient entities that you destroyed? Did you not destroy the sentient enemies too? When a war broke out between the celestial and demonic entities, was it not you who engaged in the battle, destroying the demons? But then, is it not mentioned that it was only Siva who burnt the three towns? It does not appear that way to [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/). Just as it is mentioned in mahābhāratam karṇa parvam 29 “*viṣṇurātmā bhagavata: bhavasyāmita tasmāt tanurjyāsamsarkkam sa viśēhē mahēśvara:*” ((during the time of burning the three towns) viṣṇu remained as *antarātmā* (the indvelling soul) of Siva, who has unlimited radiance and is venerable. It is because of this that Siva had the capability to draw the bow), since [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is the [antaryāmi](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/) (indvelling soul) of all entities, it was emperumān who carried out this task too, says [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/).

We will take up the 49<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 49. Serraduvum sera

*seṟṟaduvum sērā iraṇiyanai senrēṟṟup peṟṟaduvum mānilam pinnaikkāy muṟṟal muriyēṟṟin munninṟu moymboḻittāy mūric curiyēṟu sangināy sūḻndu*

**Пословный перевод:**

pinnaikkāy – for the sake of nappinnai (incarnation of emperumān’s consort neeḷā dēvi); muṟṟal – being strong; muri – running (here and there); ēṟṟin – the seven bulls’; mun ninṟu – standing in front (without fear); sūḻndu – analysing (the way of killing them); moymbu oḻittāy – Oh one who annihilated their strength!; mūri – being large; suri ēṟu – being curved inside; sangināy – Oh one who is holding the conch in the divine hand!; sērā iraṇiyanai – hiraṇya kaśyap who could not live with you; seṟṟaduvum (nī anṟē) – was it not you who killed him!; senṟu – going (to mahābali); mā nilam – the expansive earth; ēṟṟu – taking as alms; peṟṟaduvum (nī anṟē) – was it not you who obtained that!

**Перевод:**

**seṟṟaduvum sērā iraṇiyanai – the entity who emperumān killed was hiraṇya kaśyap who was incongruent with him. ōr, it can be construed that hiraṇya could not fit with emperumān’s followers. ōr, we can construe it as emperumān killing hiraṇya kaśyap, being unable to tolerate the way that hiraṇya kaśyap was following in destroying himself by engaging in cruel activities and accumulating sins.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār asks whether these alone are the tasks that he had undertaken for his followers and replies that there are many others too. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**senṟu ēṟṟup peṟṟaduvum mānilam** – going to mahābali, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) sought as alms his own possession of the worlds and felt very joyous on obtaining it, as if he had got something that he could never have got otherwise.

**pinnaikkāy muṟṟal muri ēṟṟin munninṟu moymboḻittāy sūḻndu** – in order to marry nappinnai, you stood in front of the seven raging, strong bulls and destroyed their strength.

**sūḻndu** – waiting, for an opportune moment. ōr we can construe it as *sūḻndu aḻittāy* which would indicate his strength in destroying them.

**muri** – raging bulls, moving here and there

**mūric curiyēṟu sangināy** – was it not [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who had the expansive, curling conch in his divine hand who surrounded the bulls and killed them! He killed them with his own hands, in an appropriate way.

**pinnaikkāy** – since he was infatuated with the beauty of nappinnai, unmindful of the danger that was coming his way, he stood in front of the raging bulls.

We will move on to the 50<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 50. Sulnda tulayalangal

*sūḻnda tuḻāyalangal sōdi maṇi mudi māl tāḻnda aruvit tadavarai vāy āḻnda maṇi nīrccunai vaḷarnda māmudalai konṟān aṇi neelā vaṇṇattavan*

**Пословный перевод:**

sūḻnda – decorating all over his divine form; tuḻāy alangal – garland of fragrant tuḷasi; sōdi – radiant; maṇi mudi – crown with gems; aṇi neela vaṇṇattavan – one who has the complexion of beautiful blue colour; māl – sarvēśvaran (supreme entity); tāḻnda aruvi tadavarai vāy – in the middle of a huge mountain which has many streams flowing towards earth; āḻnda maṇi nīr sunai – in the reservoir which is deep and having clear water; vaḷarnda – growing (without any fear); mā mudalai – a huge crocodile; konṟān – he killed

**Перевод:**

**sūḻnda tuḻāy alangal sōdi maṇi mudi māl – emperumān who has tuḷasi garlands and crown which indicate that he is the lord of all the worlds**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār said that there is no limit to the tasks that he undertakes for the sake of his followers. Where did we see such a characteristic? He says he showed his anger, which he did not show on people such as bhīśma et al, on a worm in the water \[reference here is to a crocodile\] for the sake of an elephant and manifested his partiality towards his followers. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**sūḻnda tuḻāy alangal** – tuḷasi garland which symbolises his primacy.

**sōdi maṇi mudi** – crown with resplendent gems

**tāḻnda aruvit tadavarai vāy** –  on the slopes of the mountain having many streams which flow towards the earth

**āḻnda maṇi nīrccunai vaḷarnda** – growing in the waters of the reservoir having deep and clear water

**mā mudalai konṟān** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) killed a huge crocodile, not considering it just as a worm in the water. Did he not let go of the divine disc on the crocodile, which he had not done even when he was angry with people such as bhīśma et al! The affection that he has towards his followers is the reason for such an act.

**aṇi leela vaṇṇattavan** – he got the colour on his divine form only after killing his follower’s enemy. Since he became very happy that he had got rid of his follower’s enemy, he acquired the complexion of blue colour which removes the fatigue of those who see him.

**aṇi neela vaṇṇattavan** – by displaying such a complexion, he fulfilled the desire of gajēndra (the elephant) who wanted to see him.

**konṟān** – by killing the enemy of his follower, he fulfilled his own longing. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who has the complexion of an ornament, donning the tuḷasi garland and resplendent crown, killed the huge crocodile.

We will take up the 51<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 51. Avane aruvaraiyal

*avanē aruvaraiyāl āniraigaḷ kāttān avanē aṇimarudam sāyttān avanē kalangāp perunagaram kāttuvān kaṇḍīr ilangāpuram erittān eydu*

**Пословный перевод:**

aruvaraiyāl – with the help of gōvardana hill which nobody could move; āniraigaḷ – herds of cows; kāttān – protected them mercifully; avanē kaṇḍīr – see, it was only emperumān; aṇi marudam – the two arjuna trees which were very close to each other; sāyttān – felled it; avanē kaṇḍīr – see, it was only emperumān; eydu – shooting arrows; ilangāpuram – the town of lankā; erittān – one who burnt it; avanē – he himself; kalangāp perunagaram – paramapadam which is without any bewilderment; kāttuvān kaṇḍīr – will show the way, see that

**Перевод:**

**avanē aruvaraiyāl āniraigaḷ kāttān – holding as an umbrella, the hill gōvardana, which could not be shaken by anybody. āḻvār says that it was only emperumān who protected the cows and the covherds so that no trouble came their way. Did either the cows or the covherds try to protect themselves when indra caused torrential rains? Neither did the cows nor the covherds go to kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) and appeal to him to save them. The only thing that they did was when kaṇṇan lifted the gōvardana hill as an umbrella, they did not say that they will not stand under it. There is nothing that either the covherds or the cows could do. He also does not expect them to do anything. All that is required is that they should not change their stance that they are the protected and kaṇṇan is the protector.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that emperumān who removed the danger of gajēndran is the one who is our means \[for attaining him\]. Is it that he removed the danger from one crocodile alone? āḻvār says that it is emperumān who ensures that no danger comes our way and leads us to paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam). Even though emperumān could have killed the crocodile through his sankalpa (vow/thought), he came running to the shore of the pond in order to manifest his affection for followers and killed the crocodile. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**avanē aṇi marudam sāyttān** – it was he who felled the two demons yamaḷa and arjuna who came in the form of the intertwined *maruda* (arjuna) trees. At that time, is it not true that people like yaśōdā et al did not help him! āḻvār says that emperumān protects himself without having to take the support of yaśōdā. The emphasis in the word *avanē* denotes that he carries out these tasks without support from anyone.

**avanē kalangāp perunagaram kāttuvān kaṇḍīr** – when his support was required for covherds in places which are well known to them do we have to mention that his support is required for us to reach paramapadam which we have not seen at all! īsn’t he the means for us, without the need for anyone to support him, to attain our goal \[paramapadam\]! He does not expect anything good in us for him to grant paramapadam. He will show us paramapadam without expecting anything from us.

**kalangāp perunagaram** – paramapadam is not in the grip of poverty like our samsāram where the clothes worn by one person will have to be shared by seven.

**kāttuvān kaṇḍīr** – just as śrī rāma showed ayōdyā to sītāppirātti as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 123-55 “”*ēśā sā druśyatē*” (ayōdyā town is visible here), emperumān will show us paramapadam and all that we have to do is to see it. It cannot be seen by those who try to see it with their efforts.

**ilangāpuram erittān eydu** – vasn’t it he who burnt the town of lankā by shooting the arrows and making them fly around, when the weapons of people like brahmā et al could not even enter the town! Did sītāppirātti let go of one arrow to protect herself? There is no difference between the ignorant cows and the knowledgeable sītāppirātti when it comes to the realisation that they cannot protect themselves. The purpose of sītāppirātti having knowledge was to say, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 39-40 “*mām nayēt yadi kākutsta: tat tasya sadruśam bhavēt*” (it will be fitting if śrī rāma could come on his own and take me). The ignorance of the cows was the reason for their not shunning the help when emperumān came to help them from danger.

We will take up the 52<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 52. Eydan maramaram

*eydān marāmaram ēḻumirāmanāy eydān ammān maṟīyai ēndiḻaikkāy eydaduvum tennilagaik kōn vīḻac cenṟu kuṟaḷ uruvāy munnilam kaikkoṇḍān muyanṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

irāmanāy – incarnating as śrī rāma; marāmaram ēḻum – the seven ebony trees; eydān – he pierced with his arrow; ēndu iḻaikkāy – for the sake of sītāppirātti who had decorated with ornaments; am mān maṟiyai – aiming at the fawn (actually the demon mārīca who had come in the form of a fawn); eydān – he shot it with his arrow effortlessly; eydaduvum – shooting his arrows well; then ilangai kōn vīḻa – to kill rāvaṇa, the king of southern lankā; mun – at an earlier point of time; kuṟaḷ uruvāy – in the form of vāmana (dwarf); senṟu – going to mahābali; muyanṟu – making efforts (such as manifesting his divine form, speaking gibberish, measuring the world etc); nilam kaik koṇḍān – he captured earth

**Перевод:**

**eydān marāmaram ēḻum irāmanāy – in order to convince his follower sugrīva who had asked  him to pierce one ebony tree to prove that he has the capability to kill vāli (sugrīva’s elder brother), śrī rāma pierced seven ebony trees with a single arrow. It is implied that when he creates desire in those who are ill-disposed towards him, he will do so just as he does in the case of those who are well disposed towards him.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār again speaks about the activities of śrī rāma. In the previous pāsuram, had he not mentioned “kalangāp perunagaram kāttuvān”! When asked whether emperumān gives paramapadam only to those who desire it, he says that it is emperumān himself who creates that desire also. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**eydān ammān maṟiya ēndiḻaikkāy** – not willing to go against the word of sītāpiirātti, he shot and killed that deer such that the demon mārīca who had come in the form of deer fell, rolling on the ground \[in agony\]. If the text is taken as **maṟiyai** which means a fawn, he killed it without considering it that it is a fawn, since sītāppirātti had desired it.

**ēndiḻaikkāy** – sītāppirātti’s beauty bewildered śrī rāma too, who has said in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 64-78 “*purēva mē cāru tadīmanintitām*”(sītā who is the princess of mitilā, who has beautiful row of teeth and who is faultless). It is implied here that by going after the deer, he nurtured the desire of sītā.

**eydaduvum then ilangai kōn vīḻa** – he killed rāvaṇa, the king of lankā which is in the southern direction, by shooting arrow. By displaying his valour, he made people, who were not engaged with him on seeing his physical beauty, to be engaged with him.

**senṟu kuṟaḷ uruvāy mun nilam kaik koṇḍān** – at an earlier point of time, in the form of vāmana (dwarf) he took efforts to get back earth, by accepting it as alms.

**mun nilam kaik koṇḍān muyanṟu** – he captured all the worlds by taking efforts.

**muyanṟu** – this describes the incongruent words that he had spoken as vāmana when he told mahābali “*koḷvannān māvali mūvadi tā*” (I will accept, mahābali; give me three steps) which are disconnected like a child speaking gibberish. This implies that he attracted mahābali’s attention and received alms.

We will take up the 53<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 53. Muyanru tolu

*muyanṟu toḻu nenjē mūri nīr vēlai iyanṟa maratālilaiyin mēlāl payinṟangōr maṇṇalangoḷ veḷḷattu māyak kuḻaviyāyt taṇṇalangal mālaiyān tāḷ*

**Пословный перевод:**

mūri nīr vēlai – in the ocean having water with waves; iyanṟa – fitting; ālamarattu ilaiyin mēlāl – on top of a banyan leaf; payinṟu – reclining for a long time; angu – there; maṇ – earth’s; nalam – benefit; koḷ – seized; veḷḷattu – in the deluge; ōr māyak kuḻavi āy – as an amazing child; taṇ alangal mālaiyān tāḷ – emperumān who has a cool, swaying tuḷasi garland, such emperumān’s; tāḷ – divine feet; nenjē – Oh heart!; muyanṟu toḻu – make an effort and worship

**Перевод:**

**muyanṟu toḻu nenjē – Oh heart, try to attain emperumān with zeal instead of having the attitude of “if it comes, let it; I vouldn’t go after it”! Make an effort and attain him. Keep falling \[in front of emperumān\] till your hands fracture.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār enjoys the experience of emperumān reclining on banyan leaf, just as he enjoyed the deed of vāmana in the previous pāsuram. He tells his heart that they should fall head over heels and experience such deeds when emperumān’s characteristics are like these. He says that emperumān is capable of uniting things which are incompatible. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**mūri nīr vēlai** – in the ocean which has water throwing up waves

**iyanṟa marattu Al ilaiyin mēlāl** – on top of a tender banyan leaf which had just sprouted on that ocean

**payinṟu** – lying on top of the banyan leaf for a very long time such that people such as mārkaṇḍēya become fearful \[as to what trouble will befall [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\]

**angōr maṇ nalam koḷ veḷḷattu** – in the deluge which tries to destroy the characteristics of earth. Earth is in the effect mode.

**maṇ nalam koḷ veḷḷam** – the virtues of earth are: it is an implement for the enjoyment of [cētanas (sentient entities)](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/), it is the place for enjoying and it is the object of enjoyment. Such earth is destroyed during deluge.

**māyak kuḻaviyāy** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is an infant with amazing activities.

**taṇ alangal mālaiyān tāḷ** – the divine feet of emperumān who has the swaying garland which removes fatigue.

**muyanṟu toḻu nenjē** – when he kept all these objects \[the worlds, oceans etc\] inside his stomach, did he not carry out that task in such a way that his garland and other decorative ornaments were not disturbed even a little bit! The beauty with which he was lying on the banyan leaf with his garland! Was yaśōdā present there to deck him up? īsn’t everything amazing!

We will move on to the 54<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 54. Talal sagadam

*tāḷāl sagadam udaittup pagadundi kīḷā marudidai pōyk kēḻalāy mīḷādu maṇṇagalam kīṇḍu angōr māduganda mārvaṟkup peṇṇagalam kādal peridu*

**Пословный перевод:**

sagadam – sagadāsuran \[a demon who had entered a wheel to kill kriṣṇa\]; tāḷāl – with divine feet; udaittu – kicking; pagadu – elephant \[called as kuvalayāpīdam\]; undi – pushing it aside; kīḷā – without a split; marudu idai pōy – crawling between the arjuna trees; kēḻal āy – in the form of varāha (wild boar); mīḷādu – without hesitating and returning; agalam maṇ kīṇḍu – digging out the expansive earth from the wall of the universe; ōr mādu uganda mārvaṟku – emperumān who has the divine chest much desired by periya pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi); peṇ agalam – on the divine form of bhūmippirātti (śrī bhūdēvi); kādal – affection; peridu – will be flooding

**Перевод:**

**tāḷāl sagadam udaittu – killing the demon sagadāsuran with his divine feet. Kicking aside a wheel which came close to his divine feet.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār enjoys the deeds of kaṇṇapirān (kriṣṇa) which could be compared with the deed of emperumān on the banyan lead. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**pagadundi** – *pagadu* – elephant. Killing the elephant kuvalayāpīdam set on him by kamsa, by pushing it. He pushed hard at the elephant which came close to his hand. He kicked the wheel which came in his way, with his divine feet and pushed aside the elephant, as mentioned by [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) in periya tirumoḻi 6-5-6 “*puguvāy ninṟa pōdagam*” (elephant which stood in his way of going towards kamsa) with his divine hands.

**kīḷā marudidai pōy** – finding a path between two arjuna trees which had intertwined closely without any gap. We can also construe it as kriṣṇa going between the trees by splitting them.

**kēḻalāy mīḷādu** – When he, who is greater than everyone else, takes a very lowly form of boar, should he not look like a boar? Thus, he felt like a boar. If he had thought of himself as the consort of *tirumagaḷ* (śrī mahālakśmi) could he have carried out the activities of a boar?

**mīḷādu maṇṇagalam kīṇḍu** – we can construe the meaning as – digging out the earth such that it cannot be snatched away.

**angōr maṇṇagalam kīṇḍu** – he came out of deluge there, holding the expansive earth.

**mādu uganda mārvaṟku** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who has the divine chest much desired by [periya pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/).

**peṇṇagalam kādal peridu** – his affection towards śrī bhūmippirātti (śrī bhūdēvi) was over-flooded. *peṇṇagalam* refers to the divine form of śrī bhūmippirātti.  Has bhūmippirātti’s form not been described in [siṟīya tirumadal](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/03/siriya-thirumadal/) “*kārār varaikkongai kaṇṇar kadal udukkai*” (bhumippirātti has tiruvēngadam and tirumālirunjōlai on which clouds take rest, as her bosom and the expansive ocean as her dress)!

**kādal peridu** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s infatuation with bhūmippirātti is described here. emperumān’s desire for those whom he likes is much larger than the desire which those who like him have for him. Did not emperumān who has all his desires fulfilled, take out earth from the walls of the universe by taking the form of a boar!

**peṇṇagalam kādal peridu** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) says “Is it not because of the affection that he has for bhūmippirātti that he dug out the earth  which she respects a lot (or earth which has bhūmippirātti as the caretaking deity)?”.

We will take up the 55<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 55. Periya varai marvil

*periya varai mārvil pērāram pūṇḍu kariya mugil idai min pōla tiriyungāl pāṇodunga vaṇḍaṟaiyum pangayamē maṟṟavan tan nīṇedungaṇ kāttum niṟam*

**Пословный перевод:**

periya varai mārvil – in the divine chest \[of emperumān\] which is like a huge mountain; pērāram pūṇḍu – donning a huge necklace; tiriyungāl – if he roams around; kariya mugil idai min pōla – it will appear like a flash of lightning across rain-laden clouds; maṟṟu – more over; avan than – that emperumān’s; nīḷ negum kaṇ – long expansive eyes’; niṟam – colour; pāṇ odunga vaṇḍu aṟaiyum pangayamē kāttum – lotus flower, in which beetles remain and sing so sweetly that all the other songs which had earlier been sung and called as songs would be obliterated, would show out

**Перевод:**

**periya varai mārvil – emperumān’s divine form would appear like mēru mountain (a celestial mountain which is very huge). With garlands decorating his divine chest, the divine chest would appear like a mountain which cannot be estimated \[for its expansiveness\].**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār enjoys the beauty of emperumān who has affection like this for bhūmippirātti. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**pērāram pūṇḍu** – the greatness of the garland is such that it has to be folded twice over on that divine chest.

**kariya mugil idai min poḷa** – an example is given to describe how the garland appeared on his black divine chest. Just as a flash of lightning would appear across dark clouds, the golden garland appeared across the black divine form of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

**tiriyungāl** – when emperumān was roaming here and there, donning the huge garland on his divine chest. ōr, it can be construed as: when thinking of this beauty.

**pāṇ odunga vaṇḍu aṟaiyum pangayamē** – the beetles will sing so sweetly that all the others who can sing would hang out their heads in shame. Lotus flowers, on which these beetles would be foraging, would show. What will they show?

**maṟṟu avan than nīḷ nedungaṇ kāttum niṟam** – They will show the colour of the long, expansive divine eyes of emperumān. The lotus flower will show the colour of his eyes whose beauty is immeasurable. Did not [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) too describe emperumān’s divine eyes in his tiruviruttam 45 “*perum kēḻalār tam perungaṇ malarppuṇḍarīkam*” by saying that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine eyes are like lotus flowers!

We will take up the 56<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 56. Niram velidu

*niṟam veḷidu seydu pasidu karidenṟu iṟai uruvam yām aṟiyōm eṇṇil niṟaivudaiya nāmangai tānum nalam puriya vallaḷē pūmangai kēḷvan polivu*

**Пословный перевод:**

iṟai – sarvēśvaran’s (supreme being’s); uruvam – the divine auspicious form; niṟam – by colour; veḷidu – will it be white? (ōr); seydu – will it be red? (ōr); pasidu – will it be green? (ōr); karidu – will it be black?; enṟu – of what colour will it be; eṇṇil – if we analyse; yām aṟiyōm – we will not know; niṟaivu udaiya – one who is complete in knowledge and power; nāmangai tānum – sarasvati dēvi too; pū mangai kēḷvan – emperumān, the consort of śrī mahālakśmi who dwells on a lotus flower, his; polivu – the completeness; nalam pugaḻa vallaḷē – does she have the power to praise? (No, she does not)

**Перевод:**

**niṟam veḷidu seydu pasidu karidu enṟu iṟai uruvam yām aṟiyōm eṇṇil – if we try to analyse whether emperumān’s complexion is whitish or reddish or greenish or blackish, we cannot know that, even a little bit.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that one can enjoy this beauty of emperumān but cannot estimate the beauty and say that it is like this. One can engage in enjoying the benefit of experiencing emperumān’s beauty but it is not possible to describe that beauty. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**yām aṟiyōm eṇṇil** – we can only speak with devotion but cannot think with our mind. It is only this much. That we cannot think of, is not due to any shortage of our knowledge but because of his greatness.

**niṟaivudaiya nāmangai tānum** – even sarasvati, who is apt to be attained by people of the world who try to speak about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and one who has the greatness herself to speak about emperumān; sarasvati dēvi who has the quality to speak without any confusion and one who can speak in an unbiased way.

**nalam pugaḻa vallaḷē** – is she capable of speaking completely about the auspicious qualities of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)? Is she capable of speaking appropriate to emperumān’s greatness ?

What is the reason for her being unable to praise emperumān?

**pūmangai kēḷvanpolivu** – is it possible for sarasvati to speak fully about the greatness of emperumān who has as his servitor [*tirumagaḷ*](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) [(śrī mahālakśmi)](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) who in turn has sarasvati as her servitor! Is it possible for some people to speak about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s greatness? Is it possible to speak in a place where emperumān and pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi) are together? Has it not been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 37-18 “*apramēyam hi tat tēja: yasya sā janakātmajā*” (is it possible to estimate the radiance of śrī rāma with whom sītā, the daughter of janaka, is together)! That it is not possible to estimate the greatness of emperumān has been mercifully mentioned by [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) too in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-1-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/03/thiruvaimozhi-1-1-1-uyarvara-uyarnalam/) “*uyarvaṟa uyarnalam udaiyavan*” (one who has great auspicious qualities which cannot be surpassed by anyone).

**iṟai uruvam yāM aṟiyōm eṇṇil** – this the greatness of the divine form of emperumān (implies that it is impossible to speak about his auspicious qualities).

We will take up the 57<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 57. Polindirunda kar vanil

*polindiruṇḍa kārvānil minnē pōl tōnṟi malindu tiruvirunda mārvan polinda garudan mēl kaṇḍa kariyān kaḻalē teruḷ tanmēl kaṇḍāy teḷi*

**Пословный перевод:**

tiru – pirātti; polindu iruṇḍa kār – being very dark during rainy season; vānil – among the cloud; minnē pōl tōnṟi – dazzling like lightning; malindu irunda mārvan – one who has the divine chest in which she resides with pride; polinda garudan mēl koṇḍa – conducting garuda; kariyān – emperumān who is of dark complexion; kaḻalē – only the divine feet; teruḷ than mēl – is the matter for devotion, which is superior to knowledge; teḷi kaṇḍāy – (Oh heart!) be clear on this

**Перевод:**

**polindu iruṇḍa kār vānil minnē pōl tōnṟi –  golden hued periya pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi) has taken residence in the dark complexioned divine form of emperumān just like a flash of lightning dazzles across the dark clouds during monsoon when the sky is dark.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that it will be great to meditate on emperumān who has such greatness. He tells his heart to attain emperumān, who has garuda as his flag and, who is the consort of śrī mahālakśmi. He says that instead of being aloof saying that it is not possible, if we attain him, that itself will give eminence to everyone. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**malindu tiru irunda mārvan** – emperumān has his divine chest on which periya pirātti has taken residence with her greatness showing clearly. Alternatively, *malindu tiru irunda* can be construed as periya pirātti greatly desiring the divine chest of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and taking residence in it.

**polinda garudan mēl koṇḍa kariyān kaḻalē** – it appears that for emperumān being atop garudan gives him supremacy over being the consort of [periya pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/). It appears that the beauty when he is atop garuda is more than that when he is with periya pirātti. The dark coloured divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is riding garuda…

**polinda garudan** – we can consider this as garuda who is fully complete. emperumān sitting on garuda is like a dark coloured peak sitting over the golden colured great mēru mountain.

**kariyān** – it is implied by this that the two of them ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and garuda) ae having contrasting hues.

**teruḷ than** – *terul* means knowledge; being meditated upon. We can consider this as referring to bhakti (devotion) which is the matured state of *jñāna* (knowledge).

**mēl kaṇḍāy** – think higher than these. In other words, think that all the rest are on a lower level and *bhakti* is at a higher level. īsn’t this the greatness which is apt for the servitor’s nature!

**teḷi** – think of this as being truly stated, inside the heart, instead of stating something superficially.

**garudan Mel koṇḍa kariyān tiruvirunda mārvan** – [pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) is like lightning which manifests both [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and garuda.

We will take up the 58<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 58. Telinda siladalattin

*teḷinda silādalattin mēl irunda mandi aḷinda kaduvanaiyē nōkki viḷangiya veṇ madiyam tāvennum vēngadamē mēlorunāḷ maṇ madiyil koṇḍugandān vāḻvu*

**Пословный перевод:**

teḷinda – being clear; silādalattin mēl irunda mandi – the female monkey sitting on the crystalline rock; aḷinda karudanaiyē nōkki – looking at the affectionate male monkey; viḷangiya veṇ madiyam tā ennum vēngadam – tiruvēngadam (tirumalai) which appears such that the female monkey will ask the male monkey to catch hold of and give the bright moon; mēl oru nāḷ – at an earlier point of time; maṇ – earth; madiyin – through cleverness; koṇḍu – obtaining (from mahābali); ugandān – emperumān who was happy in his divine mind; vāḻvu – the place of residence

**Перевод:**

**teḷinda silādalattin mēl irunda mandi – female monkey sitting on a black rock which appeared pure like a crystal. These monkeys are much like śrī rāma and sītāppirātti who were residing in the divine citrakūtam during their time of dwelling in forest.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*When told that we should attain emperumān who has such greatness, we told āḻvār “we can hold on to him only if we see him”. āḻvār responds saying that he has taken residence in tirumalai so that we can see him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**silādalattin mēl irunda** – this implies that the female monkey was sitting on the black rock already.

**irunda** – the female monkey was sitting on the rock, manifesting its greatness. This monkey is sitting on the rock just like sītāppirātti was sitting on a rock on the banks of river gōdāvari. Just as śrī rāma told lakśmaṇa as in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 63-12 “*asminmayā sārdamutāraśeelā śilātalē pūrvamupōpaviśtā, kāntasmitā lakśmaṇa jātahāsā tvāmāha sītā bahuvākyajātam*” (Oh lakśmaṇa! sītā, who has a deep nature and a beautiful smile, was sitting close to me on this rock earlier and said many words to you with a banter), this monkey is also doing the same.

**aḷinda kaduvanaiyē nōkki** – looking at the male monkey, which was very affectionate towards the female monkey and expecting the next command from the female monkey.

**aḷinda** – just like servitors who look for signs from knitting of the brow. This implies an expectation from the male monkey as to when the female monkey is going to order it to carry out a specific task.

**aḷinda kaduvan** – don’t love quarrels take place due to excessive affection!

**kaduvanaiyē nōkki** – when there is a love quarrel, without the need for a mediator, the female monkey directly talks to the male. When there was a love quarrel between śrī rāma and sītā, lakśmaṇa was present to be a mediator but here there is none like that.

**nōkki** – by its mere look, the female monkey gives out everything that it wanted to covey.

**viḷangiya veṇ madiyam tā ennum** – the female monkey wants the male to bring the brightly shining moon. Just as asking for a mirror to see its face, it is asking for the moon. Since tirumalai hills are taller than the lunar region, the other side of moon is visible to the female monkey. Since there is no fault on the other side of moon, it is asking the male monkey to give the faultless moon.

**tā ennum** – since the female monkey is sitting on top of the rock and the male monkey below, it is stretching its hand to offer it the moon.

**vēngadamē** – tiruvēngadam hills like this

**mēl oru nāḷ maṇ madiyil koṇḍu ugandān vāḻvu** – this is the place which is considered as his wealth by [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who by his cleverness, lowered himself in the presence of mahābali and obtained the earth by asking for alms from him, and became very delighted.

**maṇ madiyil koṇḍu ugnadān** – by having the knowledge that (mahābali) will give if asked for as alms, emperumān asked for as alms.

**ugandān** – he was delighted as if he had got something which was not his.

**vāḻvu** – the place of residence. It can also be considered as his wealth.

**maṇ madiyil koṇḍu** – it appears that when the monkey asked for moon, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) was reminded of vāmana asking for three steps of land!

We will consider the 59<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 59. Valum vagai

*vāḻum vagai aṟindēn mai pōl nedu varai vāy tāḻum aruvi pōl tār kidappa sūḻum tirumā maṇi vaṇṇan sengaṇmāl engaḷ perumān adi sērap peṟṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

mai pōl nedu varai vāy – in the huge mountain which is very dark like a black pigment; tāzum – flowing down, on both sides; aruvi pōl – like stream; tār kidappa – with the garland lying; sūḻum – circling it without leaving it even for a moment; tiru – one who has śrī mahālakśmi; mā maṇi vaṇṇan – one who has the complexion of blue coloured gem; sengaṇ māl – the great one with reddish eyes; engaḷ perumān – emperumān’s; adi – divine feet; sērappeṟṟu – since (I) approached; vāḻum vagai aṟindēn – I learnt the way to sustain myself

**Перевод:**

**vāḻum vagai aṟindēn – I learnt the way to sustain myself – by attaining emperumān and holding on to him. I learnt that this is the wealth which is meant for ātmā (souls). In earlier times, as mentioned in śrī bhagavat gītā 2-62, and 2.63 “dyāyatō viśayān pumsa … buddināśāt praṇaśyati” (by thinking of worldly matters, the mind was having attachment towards them…. he is lost since he loses his intelligence), I had learnt the way to get lost by involving with worldly matters and getting destroyed. nammāḻvār too has mercifully stated in tiruvāimoḻi 6-9-9 “pala nī kāttip paduppāyō” (are you going to destroy me by showing me all the worldly matters?). Only knowledge about emperumān and lack of that knowledge are the reasons for attaining salvation (from samsāram) and for getting destroyed, respectively. Since sītāppirātti had instructed rāvaṇa, as in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 21-20 “yadi jīvitumiccasi tēna maitri bhavatu tē” (if you desire to live, make friends with śrī rāma), if one wishes to sustain oneself, he/she should attain emperumān.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the 57th pāsuram, āḻvār had told his divine mind “kariyān kaḻalē teruḷ” (being devoted to the divine feet of emperumān). In this pāsuram, he approaches those divine feet and experiences them. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**mai pōl nedu varai vāyt tāḻum aruvipōl tār kidappac cūḻum** – even if [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) does not grant us salvation but destroys us, he has such beauty that we cannot shun him.  Just as streams roll down the sides of a huge black mountain, garlands from his shoulders are surrounding his divine chest. Just like two streams falling rapidly from mēru mountain (a celestial mountain) towards earth, the garlands come down from the shoulders towards the divine feet. Since the mountain is not sufficient to describe the divine chest of emperumān, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) is adding a simile to it by saying mountain which is like a black pigment.

**sūḻum tirumāmaṇivaṇṇan** – we can combine the last word of the previous verse (*sūḻum*) with the first word of the next verse (*tirumāmaṇivaṇṇan*) and say that [pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) will surround [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), unable to let go of this beautiful divine form of emperumān, as mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 6-10-10 “*agalagillēn iṟaiyum enṟu*” (not wishing to leave him even for a moment) and dwell permanently on that divine chest.

**tirumāmaṇivaṇṇan** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has the complexion of a lustrous, priceless gem. Alternatively, this could mean that he has on his divine chest *tiru* ([pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/)) and kaustuba garland (blue coloured pendant on a chain, resting on his divine chest, representing all the jīvātmās). He has periya pirātti and a divine form that removes the fatigue of those who see him.

**sengaṇmāl** – emperumān with lotus eyes. These eyes will give out the fact that he is the supreme being. We can construe the reddish colour of the eyes to have come from the fact that he is the consort of śrī mahālakśmi. This implies that the cause for happiness is also an ordained one.

**engaḷ perumān** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and [pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) together – the pair – is a very sweet matter for [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/). emperumān who is with periya pirātti is *asmat svāmy* (my lord).

**adi sērappeṟṟu** – I learnt the way to approach and be with such emperumān. I learnt the way to attain the divine feet of emperumān, who is sweet, great and ordained for me, and to sustain myself.

We will take up the 60<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 60. Perram pinaimarudam

*peṟṟam piṇai marudam pēymulai māccagadam muṟṟakkāttūdupōy uṇḍudaittu kaṟṟukkuṇilai viḷanganikkuk koṇḍeṟindān veṟṟip paṇilam vāy vaittugandān paṇḍu*

**Пословный перевод:**

peṟṟam – cows; muṟṟakkāttu – protecting without leaving out even one cow; piṇai marudam ūdu pōy – going between the (two) intertwined pair of arjuna trees; pēy mulai uṇḍu – suckling the demon pūtanā; mā sagadam udaittu – kicking the huge wheel; kaṟṟu kuṇilaik koṇḍu – using the calf as a stick to throw against; viḷanganikku eṟindān – emperumān who threw it against the wood apple; paṇḍu – once upon a time; veṟṟi paṇilam – conch which has the habit of winning; vāy vaittu – blowing it from his mouth; ugandān – was happy

**Перевод:**

**peṟṟam piṇai marudam …. – this pāsuram comes under the category of niṟai aṇi in tamiḻ grammar. In other words, each of the words in the first line should be connected with the corresponding word in the second line. Thus, the sequence will be: peṟṟam – muṟṟak kāttu; piṇai marudam – ūdu pōy; pēy mulai – uṇḍu; māc cagadam – udaittu.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*When āḻvār was asked “If you wish to have attachment to life, you can live only if your enemies are removed. What happened to your enemies?”. āḻvār responds saying “getting rid of enemies has becomes emperumān’s responsibility” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**peṟṟam** – cows.

**piṇai marudam** – two arjuna trees which had intertwined without any gap between them.

**pēy mulai** – the bosom of demonic woman, pūtanā.

**māccagadam** – the fearsome wheel.

**muṟṟak kāttu** – protecting all the cows without leaving out even one

**ūdu pōy** – going between the arjuna trees, in which the demons yamaḷa and arjuna had pervaded, in such a way that they were felled

**uṇḍu** – suckling pūtanā such that she was killed

**udaittu** – kicking the demon who had come in the form of a wheel

then

**kaṟṟuk kuṇilai** – using a calf as a throwing stick

**viḷanganikkuk koṇḍu eṟindān** – he threw (the calf) at a wood apple fruit. By using one (calf) \[demon\] which came to kill him, he killed the other (wood apple).

**veṟṟippaṇilam vāy vaittu ugandān** – who did all these? It is he who blew the victorious conch pāncajanyam keeping in his mouth and felt happy, after killing the gang of demons, that he had annihilated the enemies of his followers. Since he had taken a vow that he will not don any weapon, he killed his followers’ enemies by the mere trumpeting sound of conch. Instead of killing each enemy separately with the bows of arjuna, he killed all the enemies simultaneously with the sound of conch and carried out benefit for his followers.

**paṇḍu** – before he reached the stage of adulthood, he killed the inimical demons with his hands. After becoming an adult, he killed the enemies with the sound of śrī pāncajanya (divine conch).

We will take up the 61<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 61. Pandellam vengadam

*paṇḍellām vēngadam pāṟkadal vaikundam koṇḍangu uṟaivāṟkuk kōyilpōl vaṇḍu vaḷangiḷarum neeḷsōlai vaṇpūngadigai iḷangumaran tan viṇṇagar*

**Пословный перевод:**

vaikundam – paramapadam; koṇḍu – keeping it as his residence; angu – in that place; uṟaivāṟku – for emperumān who resides there permanently; pāṟkadal – tiruppāṟkadal, the milky ocean; vēngadam – tirumalai; vaṇḍu vaḷam kiḷarum neeḷ sōlai – having expansive gardens where swarms of beetles gather; vaṇ – beautiful; pū – sweet; kadigai – the divine hills of kadigai (also known as cōḷaśimhapuram or śōḷingapuram); iḷam kumaran than viṇṇagar – tiruviṇṇagar which the youthful emperumān considers as his own; paṇḍu – before emperumān subjected āḻvār as his servitor; kōyil pōl – looks like these were his temples (the implied meaning here is that nowadays, emperumān considers āḻvār’s heart as his temple)

**Перевод:**

**vaikundam koṇḍu angu uṟaivāṟku – to emperumān who considers śrīvaikuṇṭam as his dwelling place and resides there permanently with lot of involvement**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that it is not that emperumān merely removes the enemies of his followers. He says that the reason for emperumān, who is having paramapadam as his residence, to dwell in various divyadēśams (divine abodes) is only for the sake of his followers. āḻvār reveals the subtle meaning in this pāsuram that the supreme being, emperumān, desires āḻvār’s divine mind over his divyadēśams. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**pāṟkadal vēngadam** – the divine milky ocean and tiruvēngadamalai (tirumalai)

**vaṇḍu vaḷam kiḷarum neeḷ sōlai vaṇ pūm kadigai** – beautiful, sweet divine kadigai hills, which have orchards swarming with beetles

**iḷam kumaran than viṇṇagar** – the divine abode tiruviṇṇagaram where emperumān, who is forever young, has taken residence.

All these divine abodes…..

**paṇḍellām kōyil pōl** – it appears that these were emperumān’s temples before he recognised āḻvār. It is implicit here that after emperumān accepted him, his heart has become emperumān’s temple.

**paṇḍellām kōyil pōl** – before emperumān accepted āḻvār (as his servitor) these abodes were his temples. āḻvār says that now, after getting rid of āḻvār’s sins, emperumān does not know of anything else. He implies that emperumān has left śrīvaikuṇṭam, tiruppāṟkadal and other divyadēśams and entered āḻvār’s divine mind. ōr we can construe the meaning for this as – for emperumān who earlier used to reside in abodes such as vēngadam, pāṟkadal, vaikundam, kadigai, viṇṇagar etc, āḻvār’s heart has now become the temple. Hasn’t  [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) mercifully stated in tiruvāimoḻi 10-7-8 “*tirumāḷ vaikundamē taṇ tiruvēngadamē*” by which he has brought in parity for tiruvēngadam \[tirumalai\] with paramapadam and tiruppāṟkadal! We can also construe the meaning for this as – for emperumān who was residing in the beautiful sweet kadigai hills and viṇṇagar, abodes such as vēngadam, pāṟkadal, vaikundam etc were probably temples earlier.

We will move on to pāsuram 62 next.

## 62. Vinnagaram vehka

*viṇṇagaram vehkā viri tirai nīr vēngadam maṇṇagaram māmāda vēḷukkai maṇṇagatta tenkudandai tēnār tiruvarangam tenkōtti tankudangai nīrēṟṟān tāḻvu*

**Пословный перевод:**

viṇṇagaram – tiruviṇṇagaram (a divine abode in kumbakōṇam); vehkā – tiruvehkā (a divine abode in kāncīpuram); viri tirai nīr vēngadam – tirumalai where there is plenty of water resource with splashing waves; maṇṇagaram – only this is a city on earth; mā mādam vēḷukkai – tiruvēḷukkai (a divine abode in kāncīpuram) which has huge mansions; maṇ agatta then kudandai – the beautiful tirukkudandai (kumbakōṇam) which is at the centre of earth; tēn Ar tiruvarangam – the divine tiruvarangam town which has flood of honey (inside the surrounding gardens); then kōtti – the divine tirukkōttiyūr on the southern side; than kudangai – in his palm; nīr ēṟṟān – emperumān who took water (from mahābali as symbolic of accepting alms); tāḻvu – are the places of residence where emperumān stays with modesty

**Перевод:**

**viṇṇagaram vehkā viri tirai nīr vēngadam – tiruviṇṇagaram, tiruvehkā and, tirumalai which has lot of water resources. Just as it is mentioned in nācciyār tirumoḻi 8.3 “kuḷiraruvi vēngadam”, it is tiruvēngadam which has lot of streams. āḻvār will not mention tirumalai without adding a special adjective for it!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that emperumān very much desires divyadēśams (divine abodes) as being solitary places from where he can protect followers. Is it that he has merely taken residence in this world? He says that emperumān’s stay at paramapadam is also devoid of anything; isn’t it only at divyadēśams that he is together with witnesses! Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**maṇṇagaram mā māda vēḷukkai** – even though it is located in the materialistic realm, the town of vēḷukkai with its tall mansions will equal paramapadam.

**mā māda** – this signifies the tall mansions.

**maṇ agatta then kudandai** – the divyadēśam tirukkudandai which is on earth is considered to be a piece of paramapadam.

**tēnār tiruvarangam** – the huge temple of tiruvarangam (śrīrangam) which has orchards which have unlimited sweetness.

**tenkōtti** – tirukkōttiyūr, which is on the southern side \[of bhāratam\].

**than kudangai nīrēṟṟān tāḻvu** – the divine places where [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who received water in his small palms (from mahābali) took residence with simplicity in his divine mind. Just as he came down to this world as a mendicant in order to retrieve the worlds which are his possession, he took residence as a mendicant in these divine abodes in order to retrieve souls which are his possessions.

**tāḻvu** – these are the places where he has taken residence will total happiness that he is fortunate to help his followers all the time instead of coming down once in a while through his incarnations and helping his followers once and disappearing. These are the divine abodes where he remains as a humble entity always, instead of having to incarnate every time when his followers request him.

## 63. Tal sadaiyum

*tāḻ sadaiyum nīṇmudiyum oṇmaḻuvum cakkaramum sūḻaravum ponnāṇum tōnṟumāl sūḻum tiraṇḍaruvi pāyum tirumalaimēl endaikku iraṇḍuruvum onṟāy isaindu*

**Пословный перевод:**

tāḻ sadaiyum – matted locks tied in a lowly way; nīḷ mudiyum – long divine crown; oṇ maḻuvum – the beautiful weapon maḻu (axe); cakkaramum – divine disc; sūḻ aravum – snakes coiling around; pon nāṇum – having golden waist cord; iraṇḍu uruvum – these two forms (of Sankara and nārāyaṇa); sūḻum tiraṇḍu aruvi pāyum tirumalai mēl endaikku – for my father (emperumān) who is atop tirumalai hills which have streams on all sides; onṟu āy isaindu tōnṟum – merge into one; Al – how amazing is this!

**Перевод:**

**tāḻ sadaiyum – emperumān took as his śarīram (divine physical form) the physical form of Siva with his matted hair tied in a lowly way, exhibiting clearly that he \[Siva\] has assumed this form \[with matted hair\] in order to accomplish something \[by carrying out penance\]. āḻvār says that without feeling disgusted that he had to take as part of his physical form, the form of one with matted hair, emperumān undertook that too.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that emperumān who is residing in these divyadēśams is one with simplicity. The quality of saulabhyam (simplicity) is spoken of here as the boundary for modesty. āḻvār is amazed at the ability of emperumān to don the act of one who has to accomplish something (the form of Siva) and the act of one who is readily available (the form of viṣṇu) at the same time! How amazing is this, he wonders! Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nīḷ mudiyum** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s crown which gives out to everyone that he is the leader of all the worlds. What simplicity is this that he is equating such divine crown with a lowly matted hair!

**oḷ maḻuvum** – the weapon axe which is used to trouble others.

**cakkaramum** – just as it is mentioned in tiruviruttam 33 “*aruḷār tiruccakkaram*”, the divine disc which is used to protect

**sūḻ aravum** – snake which is wound around the waist to create fear

**pon nāṇum** – the golden waist cord which is wound around the waist to display wealth

**tōnṟumāl** – these two forms appear to be one. īsn’t Siva too a part of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s *śarīram* (divine physical form)!

**sūḻum tiraṇḍaruvi pāyum tirumalai mēl endaikku** – to my father who has taken residence in the divine hills of tirumalai, surrounded everywhere with streams

**iraṇḍu uruvum onṟāy isaindu** – these two forms complemented each other and merged into one! Even though the two forms are incongruent, it appears that they suited him \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] as if they were meant for him that way!

**isaindu** – even [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) had thought that the two forms will not complement each other. One form (of Siva) is meant to accomplish something \[through penance\] and the other (of viṣṇu) is full of beauty. Despite such contrast, the two forms appeared as one and fitted [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) well! āḻvār wonders at this simplicity of emperumān! Hasn’t [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) too mercifully mentioned in periya tirumoḻi 7.10.7 “*kaṇṇudal kūdiya aruttanai*” saying that emperumān has pervaded Siva as his antaryāmi (indvelling soul) and has taken him as his śarīram!

We will take up the 64<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 64. Isainda aravamum

*isainda aravamum veṟpum kadalum pasaindangu amudu padaippa asaindu kadainda varuttamō kacci vehkāvil kidandirundu ninṟaduvum angu*

**Пословный перевод:**

isainda – being fit to be coiled around like a rope; aravamum – the snake vāsuki; (isainda) – being fit to be used as an agitator; veṟpum – the mountain mantara; (isainda) – being fit to be used as the container; kadalum – the ocean; pasaindu – interconnecting these three objects; angu – in that ocean; amudu – nectar; padaippa – making it to be formed; asaindu – undergoing difficulties; kadainda varuttamō – is it due to the tiredness of having had to churn; kacci – in kāncīpuram; vehkāvil – at tiruvehkā (a divine abode); kidandu – in reclining posture; angu – in that kāncīpuram (at tiruppādagam); irundu – in sitting posture; ninṟu – in standing posture

**Перевод:**

**isainda aravamum veṟpum kadalum – if one were to plunge a mountain mantara as the agitator in the ocean and a snake vāsuki as the rope around it and start churning, will these be compatible? They will very well be compatible since mantara is fully fit to be used as the agitator for churning, vāsuki is fully fit to be used as the rope and the ocean is fully fit to be used as the container for the churning process.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār reminisces about the ways by which emperumān transforms himself in order to carry out the tasks of dēvas (celestial entities) who desire other benefits (than emperumān himself) and feels affectionate towards him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**pasaindu angu amudu padaippa** – taking these three together, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) churned using his strength, making the ocean water as the refuse and getting nectar out of it.

**asaindu kadainda varuttamō** – Is emperumān feeling troubled since he had to use his divine body in order to churn the ocean? The term *asaindu* refers to the effort put in by emperumān to move his body during churning.

**kacci vehkāvil kidandu irundu ninṟaduvum angu** – is it due to the tiredness of having had to churn that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has taken a reclining posture at [tiruvehkā](http://thiruvekka.koyil.org/) in kāncīpuram, has taken a sitting posture in the divine abode tiruppādagam in kāncīpuram, and has taken a standing posture in the divine abode tiru ūragam?

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) feels fearful due to his affection for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) since he is present in all three poses (of standing, sitting and lying down) in these divine abodes.

We will take up the 65<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 65. Angarkidarinri

*angaṟkidarinṟi andippoḻudattu manga iraṇiyadāgattai pongi ariyuruvamāyppiḷanda ammānavanē kariyuruvam kombosittān kāyndu*

**Пословный перевод:**

angaṟku – for prahlāda, who is the son; idar inṟi – without any trouble coming his way; andi poḻudattu – during evening time; iraṇiyadu – hiraṇya kaśyap’s; āgattai – chest; manga – to destroy; ari uruvam āy pongi – rising up in the form of narasimha (part man and part lion); kiḷarndu – tearing apart; ammānavanē – that emperumān (supreme being); kāyndu – becoming furious; kari uruvam – the elephant kuvalayāpīdam which had black complexion; kombu – tusks; osittān – broke

**Перевод:**

**angaṟkidarinṟi – ensuring that no trouble came in the way of prahlāda, the son. ḍidn’t emperumān kill hiraṇya kaśyap such that no harm came in the way of prahlāda! If the text is angaṟkidar tīra we can construe the meaning as – ensuring that the harm which came prahlāda’s way was removed.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Apart from the tiredness that emperumān might have had due to churning for nectar, āḻvār lists other activities of emperumān in other incarnations which created tiredness. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**andippoḻudattu** – in the evening time when the strength of dēvas (celestial entities) diminishes and that of demons flourishes.

**manga iraṇiyadu āgattai** – at a time when the strength of iraṇiyan flourishes, he destroyed iraṇiyan’s body.

**iraṇiyadu āgam** – vasn’t iraṇiyan’s body strengthened by all the boons that he had been granted \[by other dēvatās\]! [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) ensured that such a heart was destroyed.

**pongi** – rising furiously, as mentioned in narasimha mantram “*mahāviṣṇum*”

**ari uruvamāy** – having the fiery form of narasimha, as mentioned in narasimha mantram “*jvalantam*” (radiant).

**piḷanda ammān** – the supreme being who tore it! [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) has also mercifully mentioned in his periya tirumoḻi 2.5.8 “*piṟai eyiṟṟanṟadalariyāyp peruginānai*” (one who grew in the form of narasimha with teeth like crescent of moon).

**avanē kari uruvam kombu osittān** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) mentions about how [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) removed the hurdles of all favourable people, apart from removing the hurdles of one prahlāda. During his incarnation as kriṣṇa, emperumān went furiously at the elephant kuvalayāpīdam which was set off by kamsa on him and broke its tusks. It is implied here that by protecting himself from the elephant, he removed the troubles of all those who were very affectionate towards him.

**kāyndu** – was it not emperumān who does not become furious, made himself furious! Since āḻvār has used the phrase *avanē kariyuruvam kombosittān*, we can construe that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) benefitted prahlāda twice (once by killing iraṇiyan and the second time, by killing the elephant). [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) probably considers killing the elephant as a benefit done for prahlāda!

We will take up the 66<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 66. Kayndu irulai marri

*kāyndiruḷai māṟṟik kadir ilagu māmaṇigaḷ ēynda paṇakkadir mēl vevvuyirppa vāynda madu kadaibarum vayiṟurugi māṇḍār adu kēdavarkkiṟudi āngu*

**Пословный перевод:**

kāyndu – with a fury; iruḷai māṟṟi – removing darkness; kadir ilagu – being very radiant; mā maṇigal – great gems; ēynda – fitting well; paṇam – the (spread) hoods \[of tiruvanantāḻvān, ādiśēśan); kadir mēl – over the radiance; vevvuyirppa – as he sighed heavily; vāynda – those who came there; madukaidabarum – demons madu and kaidaba; vayiṟu urugi māṇḍār – got killed by having their bowels rotting; avarkku – for those demons; āngu – in that place; adu kēdu iṟudi – reaching there itself became cause for their lowliness and destruction

**Перевод:**

**kāyndu iruḷai māṟṟi kadi ilagu mā maṇigaḷ – when the gemstones present on the hoods of ādiśēśa emit radiance, the rays will remove darkness with a fury. In other words, they will manifest their anger. It is not that emitting radiance is their nature, but it appears that even as they think that they should remove darkness, they carry out the act immediately.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār asks whether it is a matter of pride for emperumān that he had to assume a form and come down to earth to destroy an enemy (iraṇiyan). He says “Did he not destroy enemies (two demons madu and kaitabha) when he was reclining in his place half asleep? ”. Is it a matter of pride for him to kill an enemy when he is fully awake? He says “īsn’t he capable of destroying his enemy even when he is not attentive and half asleep!”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ēynda paṇakkadir** – the radiance emitted by the rays coming from the appropriate gems in the spread hoods of tiruvantāḻvān (ādiśēśan) will remove darkness with a rage.

**kadir mēl** – on top of that radiance

**vevvuyirppa** – even as he breathed heavily. tiruvanantāḻvān was very happy since he was together with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and as an outlet of that happiness he was inhaling and exhaling deeply.

**vāynda madu kaidabarum vayiṟu urugi māṇḍār** – the demons madu and kaitabha who came there at that time got killed even as their bowels started rotting.

**adu kēdu avarkku iṟudi āngu** – that (act of coming there) itself became ruinous for them and they got destroyed. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) says “Oh my! It is good that it happened to them”. [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) too, in the manner of yaśōdā, has mercifully mentioned in his periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 3.3.7 “*enṟum en piḷḷaikkut tīmai seyvārgaḷ anganam āvargaḷē*” (those who carry out misdeeds towards my son will end up with the same fate).

**āngu** – in that place. The same place, where [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has taken residence, which was responsible for [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) to live and sustain himself, became the reason for madu and kaitabha, who were having wrong ideas, to get destroyed. While being with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is the reason for people of the world to realise their natural characteristics, in the case of the demons madu and kaitabha it became the cause for their death. Just as it is mentioned in cāndōjña upaniśat 8.12.2 “*paranjōtirupasampadya svēna rūpēna abhiniśpadyatē*” (he \[a cētana, sentient entity\] approaches the greatly radiant nārāyaṇa and attains his svarūpa (natural characteristics) blossoming), while the favourable entities attain their svarūpam, for the enemies madu and kaitabha, their evil thoughts became the reason for their destruction.

We will move on to the 67<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 67. Angu malarum

*āngu malarum kuviyum māl undi vāy ōngu kamalattin oṇpōdu āngait tigirisudar enṟum veṇsangam vānil pagarumadi enṟum pārttu*

**Пословный перевод:**

māl – tirumāl’s (emperumān’s); undivāy – in the divine navel; ōngu – rising tall; oṇ kamalattin pōdu – beautiful lotus flower; kai – in emperumān’s right hand; Am – being present; tigiri – divine disc; sudar enṟum – thinking it to be sun; (kai Am) – in his left hand; veṇ sangam – white divine conch; vānil pagarum madi enṟum – thinking it as moon which is emitting light in the sky; pārttu – thinking this way; āngu – simultaneously; malarum kuviyum – it \[the lotus\] will blossom and shrivel

**Перевод:**

**āngu malarum kuviyum Al – in the same position, lotus flower will blossom as well as shrivel**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār enjoys the experience of the divine lotus-like navel which, even as emperumān is reclining in a semi-sleepy condition, looks at the togetherness of emperumān’s tiruccakkarattāḻvān (divine disc) and tiruccangāḻvān (divine conch) and keeps blossoming and śrivelling. He enjoys the half-asleep condition of emperumān after he had destroyed the demons hiraṇya kaśyap, madu and kaitabha. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**āngu** – at that time.  In other words, simultaneously.

**undivāy ōngu kamalattin oṇ pōdu** – the beautiful lotus flower which is rising tall from [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine navel

**āngait tigiri sudar enṟum** – it \[lotus flower\] will think that the divine disc on his right hand is sun

**veṇ sangam vānil pagarum madi enṟum** – looking at the white divine conch *pāncajanyam* which is on the other divine hand, it will think that it is the moon which is travelling in the sky

**ōngu kamalattin oṇ pōdu āngu malarum kuviyum Al** – it carries out the activity simultaneously which sun and moon carry out in two different states. Since sun and moon are seen at the same time, the lotus will carry out the activities simultaneously. In other words, it will blossom after looking at the divine disc, thinking it to be sun. It will shrivel, after looking at the divine conch, thinking it to be moon. Divine disc will not let the lotus to shrivel and divine conch will not let the lotus to blossom. They will make it to remain in a neutral way. While referring to emperumān, [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) has mercifully mentioned in periya tirumoḻi 7-10-1 as “*arumbinai alarai*” ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is between the stages of a flower bud and the flower blossoming; i.e. between the states of adolescence and youthfulness).

We will take up the 68<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 68. Partta kaduvan

*pārtta kaduvan sunai nīr niḻal kaṇḍu pērttōr kaduvan enap pērndu kārtta kaḷanganikkuk kai nīttum vēngadamē mēnāḷ viḷanganikkuk kanṟerindān veṟpu*

**Пословный перевод:**

sunai nīr – in the waters of reservoir at tirumalai; pārtta – looking down; kaduvan – male monkey; niḻal kaṇḍu – looking at its shadow reflected in the water; pērttu ōr kaduvan ena – confusing it for another (inimical) monkey; pērndu – starting to leave that place out of fear; kārtta kalanganikkuk kai nīttum – stretching out its hand in order to get a kaḷā (Carissa) fruit; vēngadamē – the tirumalai hills; mēl nāḷ – once upon a time; viḷanganikku – in order to obtain wood apple fruit (inside which a demon had pervaded); kanṟu erindān – emperumān who threw a calf, as a throwing stick, in order to fell the wood apple; veṟpu – divine hill

**Перевод:**

**pārtta kaduvan sunai nīr niḻal kaṇḍu – the male monkey, looking at its shadow in the waters of the reservoir, not realising that it is its shadow**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that one need not go to tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean) to enjoy such emperumān. One can enjoy him at tirumalai itself. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**pērttu ōr kaduvan enap pērndu** – mistakes it for another monkey. Not knowing that it is its own shadow, the monkey thinks that it is an inimical monkey. Thinking that it is another monkey and another carissa fruit.

**kārtta kaḷanganikku kai nīttum** – it will stretch out its hand for the black coloured Carissa fruit. A monkey is sitting atop a Carissa tree on the banks of the reservoir. It looks at the water and sees a monkey and a Carissa tree. Mistaking these for real ones, out of desire, it stretches its hand for getting the Carissa fruit.

**kārtta kaḷangani** – this implies that the black Carissa fruit is contrasting with the reddish hand of the monkey.

**vēngadamē** – such a divine hill of tiruvēngadam!

**mēnāḷ viḷankanikkuk kanṟu eṟindān veṟpu** – it is the mountain of the one who, once upon the time, threw a calf as a throwing stick, for getting wood apple fruit. Wasn’t his ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s) ignorance similar to that of the monkey! The monkey, without realising that it is a shadow, desired for Carissa fruit which it saw in the waters. ḍidn’t kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) too try to obtain a wood apple fruit by throwing something (a calf) not realising that a demon had pervaded it. īsn’t the activity of monkey which thought of obtaining a non-existing Carissa fruit very much similar to that of kaṇṇan who thought of obtaining a non-existing wood apple fruit!

We will take up the 69<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.
