## 69. Verpenru vengadam

*veṟpenṟu vēngadam pādum viyan tuḻāy kaṟpenṟu sūdum karunguḻal mēl maṟponṟa nīṇḍa tōḷ māḷ kidanda nīḷ kadal nīrāduvān pūṇḍa nāḷellām pugum*

**Пословный перевод:**

veṟpu enṟu – if there is any discussion about any mountain (my daughter); vēngadam – regarding tirumalai; pādum – will sing about; kaṟpu enṟu – being apt to be dependent on the lord in total chaste; viyan tuḻāy – the amazing tuḷasi; karu kuḻal mēl – on (her) dark hair; sūdum – she dons it; mal – wrestlers; ponṟa – to be destroyed; nīṇḍa tōḷ – having long divine shoulders; māl – supreme being; kidanda – reclining; nīḷ kadal – in the expansive milky ocean; nīrāduvān – in order to take a bath; pūṇḍa nāḷ ellam – at the time of every dawn; pugum – she leaves out for

**Перевод:**

**veṟpu enṟu vēngadam pādum – for this girl (āḻvār in the form of a girl), the worldly activities of singing, donning flowers and taking bath would be related to emperumān. If she has to talk about a mountain, she will sing about tiruvēngadamalai (tirumalai hill). She does not know of any mountain other than tirumalai.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*It is said that among all the pāsurams in the tiruvandādis, this pāsuram alone has been composed in anyāpadēsam – āḻvār assuming the posture of nāyaki (feminine form). The meanings have been given such that āḻvār discards his original nature and assumes the state of pirātti (consort) who is deeply involved with emperumān and her mother gives out the words and activities of pirātti. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**viyan tuḻāy kaṟpu enṟu sūdum karunguḻal mēl** – if she has to decorate herself with a flower, she will don only tuḷasi on her hair.

**viyan tuḻāy** – the amazing tuḷasi

**kaṟpu enṟu sūdum** – we can construe that whatever adds glory to the clan is related to chastity. *kaṟpu* is taken as the boundary for the clan \[none will cross it\].

**sūdum karunguḻal mēl** – she does not know of wearing any other sweet smelling flower. Just as [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) had mercifully mentioned in [tiruppallāṇḍu 9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/11/thiruppallandu-9-uduththuk-kalaintha/) “*todutta tuḻāy malar sūdik kaḷaindana sūdum it toṇḍargaḷōm*” (all these servitors will don only the tuḷasi which had decorated [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) earlier and which has now been removed) she will not don any flower other than tuḷasi in her locks.

**maṟponṟa nīṇḍa tōḷ māl kidanda nīḷ kadal nīrāduvān pūṇḍa nāḷ ellām pugum** – if she wants to take a bath in any place, she will choose during every dawn, only tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean ) on which emperumān,  who has divine shoulders which will crush the wrestlers’ clan into smithereens, reclines.

**maṟponṟa nīṇḍa tōḷ** – the divine shoulders which have grown only for decimating enemies.

**māl kidanda** – *māl* –one who is affectionate. Hence, the place where the affectionate [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) reclines.

**nīḷ kadal nīrāduvān** – the mother says that she ([āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/)) will think that unless she has a bath in tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean) her desire will not be fulfilled.

Alternatively, considering that the pāsurams before and the pāsurams after this pāsuram do not have such *anyāpadēsam* (āḻvār taking the stance of a pirātti), taking into account whether this pāsuram alone be considered to be in this way, the meanings for this pāsuram would also be given in a way which will be similar to giving instructions to others. In that case, the words *pādum*, *sūdum*, *pugum* would be taken to instruct others to sing, to don and to enter respectively. Instead of talking about “that mountain” or “this mountain”, sing only about tiruvēngadam hill. If you think of wearing a flower, wear only tuḷasi which is very dear to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). If you want to take bath in a waterway, try to take a bath in the sea where emperumān is reclining. Here, reference to tiruppāṟkadal is to be taken as referring to all waterways which are connected with emperumān \[thus the pond or lake or river associated with any divine abode can be considered as the ocean in which emperumān is reclining\]. **maṟponṟa nīṇḍa tōḷ** – the divine shoulders which have grown, killing enemies. **pūṇḍa nāḷ ellām** – every dawn which passes on to a day. Thus, words such as *pādum, sūdum* and *pugum* should be considered as instructing others to sing, to don and to enter respectively.

We will proceed to the 70<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 70. Pugu madattal

*pugumadattāl vāy pūsik kīḻ tāḻndu aruvi ugumadattāl kāl kaḻuvik kaiyāl migumadattēn viṇḍa malar koṇḍu viṟal vēngadavanaiyē kaṇḍu vaṇangum kaḷiṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

kaḷiṟu – the elephant (in tirumalai); pugumadattāl vāy pūsi – gargling its mouth with the exulting liquid which is coming from its forehead and cheeks and carrying out purification process for its mouth; kīḻ tāḻndu aruvi ugumadattāl – with the exulting liquid which is flowing like a river from its head towards the ground; kāl kaḻivi – washing its feet; kaiyāl – with its trunk; migu madam tēn – having honey which creates exultation; viṇḍa – blossomed; malar koṇḍu – with flower; viṟal vēngadavanaiyē – the lord at tirumalai who is extremely strong; kaṇḍu vaṇangum – will worship him

**Перевод:**

**pugu madattāl vāy pūsi – with the exulting fluid which is coming out of its forehead, neck, cheeks etc**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār, to the query whether we should create some qualification for ourselves to attain him, says that these are not necessary. He says, isn’t he the one who can be attained even by animals! Moreover, there are no rules and regulations for attaining him. It should be considered that whatever is done to attain him has been done following the rule. āḻvār says that emperumān considers whatever has been done by those who are favourable to him, as activities which are done in order to attain him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vāy pūsi** – normally the exulting fluid which is in excess will go into the mouth of the elephant. This becomes the *ācamanam* (a purification process) which the elephant follows.

**kīḻttāḻndu aruvi ugumadattāl kāl kaḻuvi** – cleaning its feet with the exultation fluid which flows down from its forehead

**kaiyāl migu madattēn viṇḍa malar koṇḍu** – plucking flowers from ponds with vanity. This is the implement for [*tiruvārādanam*](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/07/srivaishnava-thiruvaaraadhanam.html) [\[divine method of worship\]](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/07/srivaishnava-thiruvaaraadhanam.html) which the elephant does. Using flowers which have honey which can create exultation. Since another meaning for *madam* is sweet fragrance, we can construe the meaning for this verse as – taking flowers which have sweet fragrance and honey.

**viṟal vēngadavanaiyē** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) says that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is so powerful that he grants knowledge to even animals to worship him. Merely residing at tirumalai hills will enable one to attain emperumān, an activity which is the result of knowledge.

**vēngadavanaiyē** – is the elephant vorśipping śrīvaikuṇṭanātan? Is it not vorśipping tiruvēngavan!

**kaṇḍu vaṇangum kaliṟu** – isn’t this elephant too attaining [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) just like another elephant (gajēndran) which attained him!

**kaḷiṟu** – this implies that for those attaining emperumān, being born in a great lineage, activities, natural characteristics etc are not of material value.

We will take up the 71<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 71. Kaliru mugil kutta

*kaḷiṟu mugil kuttak kaiyeduttōdi oḷiṟu marupposigaiyāḷi piḷiṟi viḻak konṟu ninṟadirum vēngadamē mēnāḷ kuḻak kanṟu koṇḍeṟindān kunṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

kaḷiṟu – elephant; kai eduttu ōdi – running fast, lifting its trunk; mugil – clouds (which appeared like elephants in exultation); kutta – to pierce them (thinking that they are inimical elephants); yāḷi – seeing this, the animal yāḷi (a type of animal with lion face and having tusks and trunk like elephant); oḷiṟu maruppu – the radiant rusks; osi – snapping; kai – having trunk; piḷiri viḻa konṟu – killing (that elephant) so that it will fall down crying out; ninṟu adirum – standing there itself and trumpeting; vēngadamē – tiruvēngadam itself; mēl nāḷ – once upon a time; kuḻa kanṟu koṇḍu – using a calf (as a throwing stick); eṟindān – one who threw it (at a wood apple fruit); kunṟu – tirumalai (divine hill)

**Перевод:**

**kaḷiṟu mugil kuttak kai eduttōdi – in tirumalai, an elephant, looking at the cloud and mistaking it for another elephant, is running towards it with its raised trunk in order to attack it**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār reminisces about the events happening at tiruvēngadam where emperumān, who is so simple that even animals can attain him, resides. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**oḷiṟu marupposi kai yāḷi** – looking at the elephant running like this, yāḷi (an animal having lion’s face and elephant’s trunk and tusks) also starts running towards the elephant raising its trunk in order to snap its tusks (*maruppu*– tusk; *osi* – snapping; *kai* – yāḷi with trunk). Alternatively we can rearrange the words such that they become *marupposigai yāḷi* which will mean a yāḷi for which breaking the tusks (of elephants) is a natural quality.

**piḷiṟi viḻak konṟu ninṟu adirum** – even after killing the elephant such that the elephant falls down crying out, the yāḷi will stand there and roar in anger so that the smaller animals will shiver in fear. It is roaring since it has defeated the elephant.

**mēnāḷ kuḻak kanṟu koṇḍu eṟindān kunṟu** – it (tiruvēngadam) is the hill of that supreme being who threw a calf at a wood apple fruit. It is the place where [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who is happy that he has destroyed those for whom enmity is a nature, resides. īsn’t his ignorance much similar to that of the elephant (just as the elephant mistook cloud for an elephant, emperumān too mistook a demon for wood apple fruit)! Since it has been mentioned that yāḷi killed an elephant in tirumalai, would all those animals living there trouble one another? As per the tenets of the ritual *agniśōmīyam*, the goat which is sacrificed in that ritual attains the high place of *svargam*. Just as what had been done to the goat will not come under the classification of torture, due to the greatness of tirumalai hills, the elephant killed (by the yāḷi) would attain śrīvaikuṇṭam and hence this too will not be a torture.

We will take up the 72<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 72. Kunru onrinaya

*kunṟonṟināya kuṟa magaḷir kōl vaḷaikkai senṟu viḷaiyādum tīngaḻai pōy venṟu viḷangumadi kōḷ vidukkum vēngadamē mēlai iḷangumarar kōmānidam*

**Пословный перевод:**

kunṟu – tirumalai; onṟināya – not knowing any other; kuṟa magaḷir – hilly womenfolk; senṟu viḷaiyādum – when playing; kōl vaḷai kai – hands decorated with beautiful bangles; tīm kaḻai pōy – beautiful bamboo shoots rise (up to the lunar region); venṟu viḷangu madi kōḷ vidukkum – defeating rāhu (a planet) and releasing the rays of moon; vēngadamē – tiruvēngadam only; mēlai iḷam kumarar kōmān idam – is the place belonging to the head of eternally youthful nityasūris who are in śrīvaikuṇṭam

**Перевод:**

**kunṟu onṟināya kuṟamagaḷir – hanuman, due to his devotion towards śrī rāma, did not wish to leave this world and go to paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) \[since śrī rāma walked around only on this earth and he wanted to keep seeing these places\]. nityasūris \[permanent dwellers of paramapadam) who do not have any connection with this world, reside permanently at paramapadam. However, the hilly womenfolk who live in tirumalai, will leave out both of these and reside permanently in tirumalai.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says “īsn’t it at tirumalai that one can have darśan of and experience emperumān, the head of indefatigable nityasūris!”  (he says, hasn’t emperumān taken residence at tirumalai so that we can look at the great emperumān and worship him there!). Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kunṟu onṟināya kuṟamagaḷir** – the hilly women who have only this (tirumalai) hill. They will consider even going down the hill as a slander on their clan.

**senṟu viḷaiyādum kōḷ vaḷai kai** – when these hilly women go out and play, their hands with beautiful bangles

**tīngaḻai venṟu** – their hands will be victorious over bamboo shoots in their roundness and tenderness (since their hands are very beautiful, they will be superior to bamboos).

**pōy viḷangu madi kōḷ vidukkum** – going beyond that, their hands will prevent rāhu (one of the nine plantes) from catching the radiant moon and remove the hurdle that befell moon. It is implied that the beautiful hands of hilly women will easily defeat bamboo and will also remove moon’s difficulties. Alternatively we can say that the bamboos, which are in the beautiful hands of the hilly women with bangles (bamboos are bent by the hilly women in order to make cradles and play in them), will reach up to sky and remove the difficulty of moon. Another explanation – the radiance from the bangles worn by these women will remove the darkness from bamboo plants where even moon’s rays cannot enter \[the bamboo plants are such dense\] and remove the blot of moon too. In the meaning given first, it is implied that since moon is within hands’ reach of the hilly women, they will wipe off the blot from the face of moon (the tirumalai hills are so tall that they will reach up to the moon).

**vēngadamē** – isn’t such tirumalai hill

**mēlai iḷam kumarar kōman idam** – it is the place of the head of those who are residing far above \[in śrīvaikuṇṭam\] and who are always young because they constantly enjoy [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). āren’t those who are in śrīvaikuṇṭam always 25 years of age, just as emperumān himself! tirumalai is the dwelling place of such emperumān.

We will take up the 73<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 73. Idam valam el

*idam valamēḻ pūṇḍa iravaittērōtti vadamuga vēngadattu mannum kudanayanda kūttanāy ninṟān kuraikaḻalē kūṟuvadē nāttannāl uḷḷa nalam*

**Пословный перевод:**

idam valam – on the left side and right side; ēḻ pūṇḍa – drawn by seven horses; iravi tēr – chariot of sūryan; ōtti – one who conducts (as the indvelling soul); vada muga vēngadattu mannum – one who resides permanently in trivēngadam located on the northern direction; kudam nayanda kūttanāy ninṟān – emperumān who incarnated desirously as kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) who was fond of dancing with pots; kurai kaḻal kūṟuvadē – praising the divine feet decorated with resounding ornaments; nā tannāl uḷḷa nalam – the benefit of having mouth

**Перевод:**

**idam valam ēḻ pūṇḍa iravait tērōtti – conducting the chariot of sūryan which travels on the left and right side of mēru mountain (a celestial mountain) and which is clasped with seven horses having seven names. Alternatively, one who conducts the chariot of sūryan which are drawn on the left side and right side by seven horses. Has it not been mentioned in taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli “bīśōdēti sūrya:” (sun rises due to fear of emperumān)! This signifies that emperumān conducts the chariot of sūrya, by remaining as his indvelling soul. Alternatively, we can construe this as referring to conducting the chariot āditya ratam for arjuna during the war of mahābhāratā, on the right side and left side, and destroying enemies. ōr else, we can construe this to mean that emperumān conducts sūrya who is like a radiant chariot. If the first meaning mentioned here, that he is conducting the chariot of sūrya, is taken into consideration, it would mean that he controls all the worlds.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that the purpose of being provided with a mouth is only to keep praising the divine feet of emperumān who has taken residence in tiruvēngada malai (hill). Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vadamuga vēngadattu mannum** – one who resides permanently in tirumalai which is located in the northern direction

**kudam nayanda kūttanāy ninṟān** – being born as kaṇṇan and desiring the game of dancing with pots. Giving off his beauty to the assemblage by dancing like this, with pots.

**kurai kaḻalē kūṟuvadē nāt tannāl uḷḷa nalam** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) says that the benefit of being provided with a mouth is only to sing praises on the divine feet which have been provided with resounding ornaments.

**nāttannāl uḷḷa nalam** – the wealth provided by mouth. It appears that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) decided to come to tirumalai due to the fatigue that he had after dancing with pots.

We will move on to the 74<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

## 74. Nalame validu kol

*nalamē validu kol nanju ūttu van pēy nilamē puraṇḍu pōy vīḻa salamēdān vengongai uṇḍānai mīttāycci ūttuvān tangongai vāy vaittāḷ sārndu*

**Пословный перевод:**

salamē – with deceitfulness; nanju – poison; ūttu – having come to feed; van pēy – the cruel demon pūtanā; nilamē – on earth; puraṇḍu pōy vīḻa – to fall down (as a corpse); tān salamē – he \[emperumān\] also, with deceit; vem kongai – the bosom which was cruel; uṇḍānai – kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) who suckled it; mīttu – drawing him away (from that pūtanā); ūttuvān – in order to feed him (her milk); sārndu – approaching him; than kongai – her bosom; vāy vaittāḷ – kept it in his mouth for him to feed; nalamē validu kol – it appears that this affection is very strong

**Перевод:**

**nalamē validu kol – īsn’t affection stronger than knowledge! For yaśōdā, affection towards kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) was stronger than fear which is natural for a woman.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that among those who are affectionate towards emperumān there is none who is as affectionate as yaśōdāp pirātti. For us, involvement with emperumān is something that we have to create in ourselves with effort. For āḻvār, due to the knowledge that he had been showered with (by emperumān) and the deep affection on emperumān, these become the reasons for bewilderment. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**salamē tān nanju ūttu van pēy nilamē puraṇḍu pōy vīḻa** – making pūtaṇa, who has a strong heart and who came in a deceitful form of a mother to give poisoned milk to kaṇṇa, to fall on the earth

**vengongai uṇḍānai** – one who suckled the cruel bosom (of pūtanā)

**mīttu āycci ūttuvān** – to redeem kaṇṇan from pūtanā and as a remedy for that, to make him drink milk from her bosom

**ūttuvān** – in order to feed him

**sārndu than kongai vāy vaittāḷ** – as a remedy for the poisoned milk which he drank, yaśōdā pirātti offered him her nectar-like milk. As soon as she heard that he has taken milk from pūtanā’s bosom, instead of falling down unconsciously due to fear, she walked all the way to where pūtanā lay on the ground \[with kriṣṇa on her bosom\] and offered her bosom to him (If yaśōdā’s knowledge had gained primacy, she would have swooned out of fear. However, since affection towards kriṣṇa was in the forefront, instead of swooning, she offered her bosom to kriṣṇa). Hence, affection is probably stronger than knowledge!

We will take up the 75<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 75. Sarndu agadu teyppa

*sarndagadu tēyppat tadāviya kōttucci vāy ūrndiyangum veṇmadiyin oṇmuyalai sērndu sinavēngai pārkkum tirumalaiyē āyan punavēngai nāṟum poruppu*

**Пословный перевод:**

tadāviya – expansive; kōdu – peaks; ucci vāy – on top; agadu tēyppa – rubbing the lower part of the stomach; sārndu – approaching; ūrndu iyangum – moving slowly; veṇ madiyin – on the white coloured moon’s; oṇ muyalai – beautiful rabbit; sinam vēngai – an angry tiger; sērndu – approaching; pārkkum – will keep looking at the rabbit (without catching it or leaving it); tirumalaiyē – only tirumalai; āyan – kaṇṇapirān’s (kriṣṇa’s); punam vēngai nāṟum – with the sweet fragrance of vēngai trees (a type of tropical tree) which grow well on its land; veṟpu – mountain

**Перевод:**

**sārndu agadu tēyppat tadāviya kōttucci vāy – approaching the top of the expansive peaks and rubbing its lower stomach**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār enjoys an event that takes place in tirumalai where kaṇṇapirān (emperumān) who has the characteristic of annihilating enemies, dwells. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ūrndu iyangum veṇ madiyin** – moon which is slowly moving. Since it is rubbing against the peak, due to the sharpness gained, the light emitted by the moon is bright (this implies that the peaks of tiruvēngadam rise up to the moon)

**ūrndu iyangum** – the white coloured moon is looking for a resting place to rid of its tiredness and is moving slowly.

**oṇ muyalai** – the beautiful rabbit which is on moon (the craters on the moon appear like rabbit and hence it used to be said that there are rabbits on moon).

**sērndu sina vēngai pārkkum** – an angry tiger, looking at the rabbit will approach the moon in order to eat the rabbit, and will keep looking at it constantly. Since rabbit is a natural prey for tiger, it will keep looking at it. Since it is not real rabbit and since it cannot approach it easily, the tiger cannot catch it. It cannot leave it either, since it has a great desire for the meat. Hence it will keep looking at it.

**āyan puna vēngai nāṟum poruppu** – it is the place which kaṇṇapirān, the covherd, determines to be his and where the tropical vēngai trees will emit a sweet fragrance since they are growing on their lands.

**āyan** – he becomes very happy if he sees forests and mountains. For the covherds who live in forests, the sweet fragrance of trees in the forest will be very dear. Hence, will not the sweet fragrance emitted by the trees in tirumalai be liked by the one who has taken residence there!

We will take up the 76<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 76. Poruppidaiye ninrum

*poruppidaiyē ninṟum punal kuḷittum aindu neruppidaiyē niṟkavum nīr vēṇḍā viruppudaiya vehkāvē sērndānai meymmalar tūyk kai toḻudāl ahkāvē tī vinaigaḷ āyndu*

**Пословный перевод:**

nīr – you all (Oh people of the world!); poruppu idaiyē – amidst the mountains; ninṟum – standing; punal – in the waterways; kuḷittum – immersing in them; aindu neruppidaiyē – amidst pancāgni (five fires); niṟkavum – doing penance, standing; vēṇḍā – there is no need; viruppu udaiya – being desired (by all); vehkā – at tiruvehkā (a divine abode in present day kāncīpuram); sērndānai – emperumān who has come there and is reclining; mey – without expecting any benefit; malar tūy kai toḻudāl – if one offers flowers and worships; tī vinaigaḷ – bad deeds (sins, results of such bad deeds); āyndu – analysing (that there is no place for us here) and knowing; ahkāvē – won’t they shrink? (implies that they will run away)

**Перевод:**

**poruppidaiyē ninṟum punal kuḷittum – standing amidst mountains during snowing winter time and taking bath in holy rivers during winter months**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*After emperumān has taken residence at various divine abodes, one can go to such divine abodes and get salvation. People need not torture their physical forms in order to attain what they desire. āḻvār tells the people of the world that emperumān has taken residence in these places without bothering about his well being and is carrying out tasks for us and hence you do not have to carry out penance by harming your physical forms. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**aindu neruppidaiyē niṟkavum nīr vēṇḍā** – lighting fires on all four sides and standing amidst them, with the sun in the sky burning down, during hot summer months (thus standing amidst five fires); there is no need to carry out any penance like this and suffer. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) is asking whether we have to get rid of our sins by suffering like this. Instead, he tells us to follow the simple method given below and get rid of our sorrows.

**viruppudaiya vehkāvē sērndānai** – going to tiruvehkā, which [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and others like, where he has taken residence.

**mey malar tūy kai toḻudāl** – instead of mixing up with the falsity of desiring other benefits, if we truly consider the benefit of desiring only emperumān, offer flowers  and worship him, and attain him

**ahkāvē tīvinaigaḷ āyndu** – bad deeds, analysing that this is not the correct place for us, will run away. Will they stay there a moment longer? Sins will analyse that this is not the correct place for us and will flee the place. Do sins have knowledge to analyse? An old saying goes like this “*vruśti pratīkśāścalāya:*” (paddy field is expecting rain). In the same way we should consider this too. [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) too has divinely mentioned in his periyāḻvār tirumoḻi  5.4.3 “*summenādē kaivittōdit tūṟugaḷ pāyndanavē*” ((the sins) without even breathing, ran and hid themselves in the bushes).

**vehkāvē sērndānai** – while he has come to tiruvehkā as if he is carrying out a deed in order to attain you all, do you also have to perform a deed in order to attain him?  Is there need for two people to undertake two tasks for attaining the same benefit?

**meymmalar tūvi āyndu** – we can construe the meaning for this as – if we analyse his auspicious qualities, meditate on those and worship him with our hands, sins will not come anywhere near us. If the text is taken as *men malar tūvi*, the meaning will become – vorśipping him with soft flowers.

We will take up the 77<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 77. Aynda aru maraiyon

*āyndavarumaṟaiyōn nānmugattōn nanguṟangil vāynda kuḻaviyāy vāḷarakkan ēynda mudippōdu mūnṟēḻenṟeṇṇinān ārnda adippōdu nangatkaraṇ*

**Пословный перевод:**

āyndu – analysing well and learning; arumaṟaiyōn – having the great vēdas (sacred texts); nānmugattōn – brahmā, who has four faces; his; nanguṟangil – beautiful lap; vāynda – being seen; kuḻaviyāy – being an infant; vāl arakkan – armoured rāvaṇa’s; ēynda – fit (to be severed); pōdu mudi – garlanded heads; mūnru ēḻ enṟu eṇṇinān – one who showed by counting with his divine feet that three plus seven equals ten; adi pōdu – lotus like divine feet; nangatku – for us; ārnda araṇ – faultless means

**Перевод:**

**āynda arumaṟaiyōn nānmugattōn – the four faced brahmā, who analysed the vēdas (sacred texts)  taught to him by emperumān and is capable of understanding the meanings of vēdas and vēdāntas (upaniśats)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the earlier pāsuram, āḻvār had said that our sins will disappear once we attain him. In this pāsuram he says that not only the ordinary cētanas (sentient persons), even when very knowledgeable ones, not knowing what is going to happen in the days to come, try to self-destroy themselves, it is he who does them good and protects them. In the earlier pāsuram, āḻvār said that we do not know what is good for us, apart from him. In this pāsuram he says that not only we, even brahmā et al do not know. It is only emperumān who knows what is good for them, āḻvār says in this pāsuram. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nan kuṟangil** – on his beautiful lap

**vāynda kuḻaviyāy** – as an infant, who was seen; as a beautiful child. rāvaṇa approached brahmā for getting boons; at that time, in order to protect his identity, he hid his ten heads and bowed down to brahmā. brahmā, lacking in natural leadership qualities, became vain on seeing someone bowing to him, wanted to grant the boons asked for by rāvaṇa, without thinking of the difficulties that they (celestial entities) would face. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), knowing that these people will come to him for succour when troubled by rāvaṇa, came as an infant on the lap of brahmā and conveyed the meaning “” if you give him excessive boons, your country will be ruined; you will lose your dwelling; understand that the one who has come is the cruel rāvaṇa” by scratching the ten heads of rāvaṇa with his divine foot and disappeared.

**vāḷ arakkan** – rāvaṇa who was armed with sword

**ēynda mudippōdu** – just as animals such as goat etc are garlanded before being decapitated, rāvaṇa too had decorated his head fit to be severed, with garland.

**mūnṟu ēḻ enṟu eṇṇinān** – just as the act of a child, he counted the heads of rāvaṇa with his divine foot.

**mūnṟu ēḻu**– instead of counting as seven plus three, he counted as three plus seven, as an ignorant child.

**ārnda adippōdu nangatku araṇ** – his divine feet, with unlimited sweetness, is our protection. The divine feet, which help in fulfilling what [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) thinks of, will be helpful like a fort for people like us who have nowhere else to go and who do not have any ulterior benefit to seek.

**ārnda adippōdu** – the flower like divine feet of emperumān which are full of sweetness.

We will take up the 78<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 78. Aranam namakku enrum

*araṇām namakkenṟum āḻi valavan muraṇāḷ valam suḻinda moymban saraṇāmēl ēdu gadi ēdu nilai ēdu piṟappu ennādē ōdu gadi māyanaiyē ōrttu*

**Пословный перевод:**

āḻi – the divine disc; muran – the demon muran; nāḷ – caused by the lengthy life; valam – strength; suḻinda – one who removed; moymban – emperumān who is strong; saraṇ Am ēl – if he becomes the protector; gadi ēdu nilai ēdu piṟappu ēdu ennādē – instead of despising “What is our knowledge? What is our status? What is our birth?” (Instead of looking at the lowliness of these); namakku enṟum araṇ Am – one who is our protector at all times; māyanaiyē – (Oh heart!) that emperumān alone, who has amazing auspicious qualities and activities; gadi – as means; ōrttu – being steadfast; ōdu – recite his divine names

**Перевод:**

**araṇām namaku enṟum āḻi valavan – emperumān who has the divine disc on his divine right hand is always our protector. During times when we face difficulties when we are looking for protection, it is only he who removes our difficulties and protects us. He alone, who is omnipotent, is the protector. īsn’t the one who has disc on his divine hand, the protector! Did he not say in śrī bhagavat gītā 18.66 “aham tvā sarva pāpēbhyō mōkśayiśyāmi” (I will redeem you from all sins) by pointing to himself! āḻvār says that his (the sentient entity’s) ego causes troubles and emperumān’s ego removes those difficulties.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār tell his heart that emperumān alone, who has the characteristic of annihilating enemies, is our protector. Hence think of him as the refuge. Even though he is the protector for everyone, should we not think of our lowly characteristics such as our knowledge, birth, activities etc? āḻvār says that there is no need; he will shower his grace on lowly persons too. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**muran nāḷ valam suḻinda moymban** – his ability to destroy enemies is mentioned here. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has the strength to negate the strength of the demon *muran* who obtained it as a result of his long life.

**saraṇām mēl** – if he becomes the protector

**āḻivalavan** – he will protect with his divine disc. periya tiruvandādi 87 says “*eppōdum kai kaḻalā nēmiyān*” ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) always has the disc which will not go out of his hand)

**ēdu gadi ēdu nilai ēdu piṟappu ennādē araṇām** – emperumān protects irrespective of the knowledge, the status, the birth etc of the cētana, by being born in any type of birth and by carrying out any type of activity. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) too has mercifully mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-3-2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruvaimozhi-1-3-2-elivarum-iyalvinan/) “*nilai varambila pala piṟappāy oḷivaru muḻunalam*” ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) incarnates without looking at the status, boundary etc). We can also construe the meaning for this as [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) saying – after knowing that he removes our enemies, you try and think about him instead of looking at your birth, activities, knowledge etc. Has it not been mentioned in [śrī bhagavat gītā 9-29](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/9-29/) “*samōham sarvabhūtēśu na mē dvēśyōsti na priya:*” (I am equanimous to all creatures; there is none who I like nor anyone who I do not like) and “*yēpi syu: pāpayōnaya:*” (even those who commit sins attain mōkśam). [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) too has mercifully mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 3-7-9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/10/thiruvaimozhi-3-7-9-kulam-thangu/) “*ettanai nalandānillāda caṇḍāḷa caṇḍāḷargaḷāgilum*” (even if they are lowliest among the lowliest…).

**ōdu gadi māyanaiyē ōrttu** – praise him after analysing that he, who has amazing auspicious qualities and activities, is fit to be attained. Think of his divine feet as the refuge. If the text is *ōdu gadi mādavanai ōrttu* we should construe the meaning as – recite his divine names considering [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is the consort of [mahālakśmi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) as the means.

We will move on to the 79<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 79. Ortta manattinaray

*ōrtta manattarāy aindadakki ārāyndu pērttāl piṟappēḻum pērkkalām kārtta viraiyār naṟunduḻāy vīngōdamēni niraiyāra mārvanaiyē ninṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

kārtta – having verdant colour; virai Ar – being fragrant; naṟum tuḻāy – having garland of fresh tuḷasi; vīngu ōda mēni – having the complexion of a full ocean; nirai āram mārvanaiyē – only emperumān who has donned ornaments in an orderly way; ninṟu – standing firmly; ōrtta manattarāy – having a meditating mind; aindu adakki – winning over the five senses; ārāyndu – analysing the lowliness of samsāram (materialistic realm); pērttāl – if one were to turn his face away \[from samsāram\]; piṟappu ēḻum – the long line of births which keep coming one after another; pērkkalām – one can get rid of

**Перевод:**

**ōrtta manattarāy – having a mind which keeps constantly meditating on the auspicious qualities of emperumān**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In order to get rid of the sins that we have accumulated from time immemorial, don’t we need a long time? āḻvār says that it is not needed. He says that if we meditate on emperumān and develop hatred towards worldly pursuits, the sins will disappear at once. He says that if the people of the world carry out what he had said, they can rid themselves of their births. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**aindadakki** – controlling the five senses so that they do not engage with worldly matters

**ārāyndu** – then thinking about the shortcomings in worldly pursuits

**pērttāl piṟappu ēḻum pērkkalām** – if one could redeem the heart \[from worldly pursuits\] one could escape from the chain of births. If one could turn one’s mind away from worldly matters, one could easily get rid of the series of births. Reference to seven births implies the full chain of births. In other words, one can get rid of all the births. One can get rid of the series of births.

**kārtta viraiyār naṟunduḻāy vīngōda mēni** – What is to be done to get rid of the series of births? One should attain [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) says that he is not recommending such bitter water of margosa bark for ridding oneself of all births. Should one not attain emperumān who has verdant complexioned, fresh tuḷasi garland and a beautiful form with the colour of a full ocean! īsn’t this the difficult-to-perform task!

**nirai Ara mārvanaiyē ninṟu** – if one meditates constantly on [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who has ornaments donned in an orderly way. These words describe the natural beauty and the beauty which came from decorations. This emphasises the beauty which he has and which will enable one to attain him, leaving aside the worldly pursuits.

We will move on to the 80<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 80. Ninru ediraya

*ninṟedirāya niraimaṇittēr vāṇan tōḷ onṟiya īrainjñūṟudan tuṇiya venṟilangum ārpaduvān nēmi aravaṇaiyān sēvadikkē nērpaduvān  tān muyalum nenju*

**Пословный перевод:**

ninṟu – standing in front (without any shame); edir āya – one who fought as an enemy; nirai maṇi tēr – one who came riding on a chariot which had gemstones studded properly; vāṇan onṟiya īraijñjñūṟu tōḷ – the thousand shoulders of the demon bāṇa; udan – simultaneously; tuṇiya – ensuring that they \[1000 shoulders\] got severed; venṟu – becoming victorious; ilangum – (on account of that victory) being radiant; ārpadu – having sharpness; vān – being strong; nēmi – having the divine disc; aravu aṇaiyān – emperumān who reclines on ādiśēśan; sē adikkē – at the reddish divine feet; nērpaduvāṇ – to approach; nenju – (my) heart; tān – on its own; muyalum – will attempt

**Перевод:**

**ninṟu edirāya – one who came as an enemy and stood. Since the demon bāṇāsuran had attained the tāmasa (ignorant) deity Siva as his protector, not knowing that kaṇṇan is the supreme being, he came to fight him.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār mercifully mentions as to how his heart got involved with emperumān ahead of him. He says that his heart, without waiting for instructions, meditating on the quality of emperumān by which he annihilates enemies, won over his \[āḻvār’s\] five senses, got rid of attachment towards worldly matters and tries to meditate on emperumān. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nirai maṇit tēr vāṇan** – the demon came on a chariot which was studded with rows of gemstones.

**tōḷ onṟiya īrainjñūṟu udan tuṇiya** – ensuring that all the 1000 shoulders got severed simultaneously. It is implied here that the 1000 shoulders were not equal to even one shoulder. The 1000 shoulders appeared to be similar to a tree which has one root and many branches.

**udan tuṇiya** – severing them such that all 1000 shoulders fell at the same time. This implies that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) alone, who did like this, is fit to be attained and other deities (such as Siva et al who came to protect vāṇa) are unfit to be attained.

**venṟu ilangum ārpaduvān nēmi** – emperumān who has the divine disc which, being victorious, had the resultant radiance and was also sharp (the term *ārpadugai* (which is the root for *ārpaduvān*) would mean sharpness or having spokes).

**aravaṇaiyān sēvadikkē** – at the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is reclining on ādiśēśan

**nērpaduvān tān muyalum nenju** – the heart, on its own, is joyously trying to attain.

We will move on to the 81<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 81. Nenjal ninaippariyanelum

*nenjāl ninaippariyanēlum nilaippeṟṟen nenjamē pēsāy ninaikkungāl nenjattup pērādu niṟkum perumānai enkolō ōrādu niṟpaduṇarvu*

**Пословный перевод:**

en nenjame – Oh my heart who is under my control!; nenjāl ninaippu ariyan ēlum – even though (emperumān) has greatness beyond the thinking ability of heart; nilaip peṟṟu – standing firmly; pēsāy – try to talk about him; ninaikkungāl – if we think of him even once; nenjattu – in the heart; pērādu niṟkum – standing firmly without leaving it; perumānai – about emperumān; uṇarvu – our heart; ōrādu niṟpadu en kolō – why is keeping quiet without thinking?

**Перевод:**

**nenjāl ninaippariyanēlum – Oh heart! Even though you are not able to think, is it possible to measure the ocean with the help of a small vessel! In the same way, is it not impossible to think of the boundless emperumān with the lowly mind! Even though emperumān is difficult to think of …**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār celebrated his heart which, unlike those of the worldly people, was involved in matters relating to emperumān. But, looking at the greatness of emperumān, it did not appear to āḻvār that he had even taken the first step. While emperumān remains unknown, having told his heart to meditate on him, he is wondering as to what is the reason for his heart not meditating on emperumān. As a result, he says that based on his involvement with emperumān, the efforts put in by his heart are not adequate. When we look at emperumān it would appear that we do not have any knowledge while when we look at worldly matters it would appear that we have lot of knowledge. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nilaippeṟṟu en nenjamē pēsāy** – Oh my heart! Instead of stepping back on looking at [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s greatness, try to enjoy. Instead of thinking that it is a flood and stepping back, try to stand firmly and talk about him. Despite [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) saying this, his heart was a bit hesitant. Immediately, āḻvār thinks within himself “Despite my telling this, instead of falling head over heels and attaining him, what is the reason for you to hesitate?”

**ninaikkungāl nenjattup pērādu niṟkum perumānai** – isn’t [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) one who would remain without leaving if he (the cētana) thinks of him once! varāha carama ślōkam is quoted here: “*stitē manasi susvastē śarīrē sati yō nara: dātu sāmyē stitē smartā viśvarūpam ca māmajam tatas tam mriyamāṇantu kāśtāpāśāṇa sannibham aham smarāmi matbhaktam nayāmai paramāmgatim* ” (when the mind is not agitated, when the body is not affected by any disease, when the dātus (constituents) – kapa, pitta and vāda (phlegm, bile and wind respectively) are in balance, whoever thinks of me who has the world as his body and who is without birth, in later years, I think of him, my devotee, when he is like a log of wood or a stone and is on the throes of death, and I take him to exalted state). In the same way, if we think of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) once, emperumān stays in the heart for ever. Even though he may doubt if the cētana has a tongue or despite having a tongue, if he is unable to speak, emperumān does not know how to leave the heart. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) too has mercifully stated in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-10-2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/10/thiruvaimozhi-1-10-2-kannulle-nirkum/) “*kaṇṇuḷḷē niṟkum kādanmaiyāl toḻil eṇṇilum varum*” (if one were to count 1,2,3 etc and say 26, emperumān, thinking that the cētana is thinking of emperumān who is the 26<sup>th</sup> *tatvam* (entity) and enter his mind). Just as emperumān himself had mentioned in mahābhāratam uttara parvam 47-22 “*gōvindēti yadāgrandat kriṣṇā mām dūravāsinam ruṇam pravruddamiva mē hrudayānnapasarppati*” (the crying out of draupadi when I was very far from her is still remaining in my heart, like interest on interest taken on a loan), even if we call him once he will never forget it.

**enkolō ōrādu niṟpadu uṇarvu** – whenever we think of, should we think of other deities who do not think of us?

**uṇarvu ōrādiruppadu enkolō** – even when we can think, what is the reason that we are not thinking of him? Should we attain other deities who are difficult to worship, instead of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)? Instead of thinking of emperumān should we get immersed in sand? How is it that one can remain without thinking of emperumān? [poygai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) too has mercifully mentioned in [mudal tiruvandādi 67](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/08/mudhal-thiruvandhadhi-67/) “*oṇḍāmaraiyāḷ kēḷvan oruvanaiyē nōkkum uṇarvu*” (knowledge will be devoted only to the consort of [śrī mahālakśmi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) ). Leaving aside emperumān can our thoughts go to lowly matters such as *śabdam* etc (the five senses)?

We will move on to the 82<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 82. Unaril unarvariyan

*uṇaril uṇarvariyan uḷḷam pugundu puṇarilum kāṇbariyan uṇmai iṇaraṇaiyak kongaṇaindu vaṇḍaṟaiyum taṇḍuḻāykkōmānai engaṇaindu kāṇḍum ini*

**Пословный перевод:**

uṇaril – if we try to know (about emperumān, on our own); uṇarvariyan – he cannot be known; uḷḷam pugundu puṇarilum – comes into the heart and embraces; uṇmai kānbu ariyan – difficult to know ’as he is’; ini – when things are like this; iṇan aṇaiya – such that bunches of flowers to be lowered; kongu – in honey; vaṇḍu – beetles; aṇaindu – approaching and entering (and drinking honey); aṟaiyum – humming; taṇ – cool; tuḻāy – one who is donning the tuḷasi garland; kōmānai – that supreme being; ini – in this state; engu – in which place; aṇaindu – approaching; kāṇḍum – will be able to see?

**Перевод:**

**uṇaril uṇarvariyan – in the first place it is better not to enter in matters related to emperumān. In other words, instead of getting in to know about him and then stepping aside realising that he cannot be known, it is better not to attempt to know about him. emperumān will remain very difficult to know for those who attempt to know about him on their own. He cannot know about himself. nammāḻvār too has mercifully stated in tiruvāimoḻi 8-4-6 “tanakkum than tanmai aṟivariyānai ” (he cannot know about his qualities himself)! How can we know about such emperumān?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār asks if there is any advantage by getting angry with the heart. He asks if there is any way by which we can approach and know about emperumān who cannot be known by anyone and who has unlimited sweetness. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**uḷḷam pugundu puṇarilum kāṇbariyan uṇmai** – in order to put an end to this eminence, if he mingles with our heart on his own so that there will be no shortcoming in knowing about him, even then he cannot be known “as he is” and cannot be estimated. His true qualities cannot be known.

**kāṇbariyan uṇmai** – one can wonder at his eminence, but he cannot be estimated.

**iṇar ….** – this says that even if he cannot be known, he cannot be left out. The cētana, even if he wants to know, cannot know about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). Even if emperumān comes on his own and shows himself up, he cannot be known. In that case should one lose him and forget everything? His sweetness and his being the lord of all will not allow that (losing him) to happen.

**iṇar aṇaiyak kongaṇaindu vaṇḍaṟaiyum taṇ tuḻāyk kōmānai** – emperumān who dons tuḷasi garland which will get lowered when beetles approach the flowers and drink in honey, humming with the resultant happiness ……

**engaṇaindu kāṇḍum ini** – where will I approach him and see him? He cannot be seen by trying to know about him in the method of the worldly people by making attempts on their own; he cannot be seen by following the method of those who believe in śāstras in which he comes on his own and shows himself up. In such a case, is there anything called seeing him? It is not possible not to approach him and it is not possible to approach him either.

Let us take up the 83<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 83. Iniyavan mayan

*iniyavan māyan ena uraipparēlum iniyavan kāṇbariyanēlum iniyavan kaḷḷattāl maṇ koṇḍu viṇ kadanda paingaḻalān uḷḷattinuḷḷē uḷan*

**Пословный перевод:**

ini – now; avan – that supreme being; māyan – amazing entity who is difficult to be known; ena – like this; uraippar ēlum – even if they (people) say; ini – now; avan – that emperumān; kāṇbu ariyan ēlum – difficult to see and enjoy; kaḷḷattāl – with deceit; maṇ koṇḍu – obtaining earth as a gift; viṇ kadanda pai kaḻalān avan – that emperumān who has expansive divine feet with which he occupied sky and all the outer worlds; ini – now; uḷḷattin uḷḷē uḷan – is dwelling firmly inside my heart (not knowing other places) (this great benefit is enough for me)

**Перевод:**

**ini avan māyan ena uraipparēlum – emperumān is easy to attain, say authentic texts. Even if the worldly people say that he is difficult to attain… Even if they say that he is an amazing entity…. Just as he has mentioned in śrī bhagavat gītā 7-13 “mama māyā duratyayā” (it is difficult to crossover my primordial matter), even if he is difficult to be seen….**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that even though emperumān is one who cannot be seen or thought of by anyone, now he has entered my heart first and is dwelling there. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**iniyavan kāṇbariyanēlum** – even if he, who has unlimited sweetness, is difficult to be approached and seen… If we break the first word as *ini avan*, the meaning will change to – now if he is difficult to be seen or if he is difficult to be seen at the end of the birth…

**iniyavan kaḷḷattāl maṇkoṇḍu** – coming to earth in the form of vāmana (dwarf), hiding his original form so that none will know that he is the supreme being, and seizing the earth. One who obtained the worlds which are his possessions, from mahābali, with a sweet form and measured them….

**viṇ kadanda paingaḻalān** – one who has expansive divine feet with which he brought all the worlds even beyond the sky, under him (alternatively, for the term *paingaḻalān* we can construe the meaning as one who has beautiful divine feet).

**ini uḷḷattin uḷḷe uḷan** – he has now taken residence in my heart. This body will remain until the ordained life span for the birth taken already remains. After that, let him ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) do whatever he wants. Can whatever I have got now (his staying inside my heart) be negated? Even though he came on his own, that he cannot be estimated has become established.

Let us take up the 84<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 85. Kaviyinar kai punaindu

*kaviyinār kai punaindu kaṇṇār kaḻal pōy seviyinār kēḷviyarāyc cērndār puviyinār pōṟṟiyuraikkap poliyumē pinnaikkā ēṟṟuyirai attan eḻil*

**Пословный перевод:**

kaviyinār – those who worship with hymns in praise; kai punaindu – joining the plams together in anjali pose; kaṇ – their eyes; Ar kaḻal pōy – getting to (his) fulsome divine feet; seviyinār kēlviyarāy – being as people with lot of knowledge through listening \[to jñānis, wise people\]; sērndār – those who join together; puviyinār – people of this world; pōṟṟi uraikka – even if they sing in praise (together); pinnaikkāy ēṟu uyirai attān eḻil poliyumē – will it mean that the beauty of emperumān, who killed seven bulls for winning the hand of nappinnaip pirātti, has been greatly described? (no, it will not)

**Перевод:**

**kaviyinār kai punaindu – reciting great hymns through mouth and praising, with folded hands. Being without ego, vorśipping emperumān through singing hymns with their mouths and carrying out anjali (folding palms together).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*If emperumān cannot be known by anyone, just as mentioned by nammāḻvār in his tiruvāimoḻi 3-1-7 “vāḻttuvār palarāga”, if all the people in the world join together, is it possible to know him? āḻvār responds, saying, even if all the people in the world join together, and with the help of their senses and power, if they praise him, he will be beyond the level in which they are. īsn’t that how his matter is! Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kaṇ Ar kaḻal pōy** – eyes reaching the divine beautiful feet of emperumān. Approaching the divine feet which have unlimited sweetness.

**seviyinār kēḷviyarāyc cērndār puviyinār** – All the people of the world, listening to his auspicious qualities which are sweet to hear.

**pōṟṟi uraikkap poliyumē** – Even if all the people join together and with auspicious verses praise him, is it possible to see [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)? Even if every person in the world, using the knowledge and strength that each person has been given by emperumān, praises him in a single voice, vouldn’t emperumān’s greatness go beyond that!

**poliyumē** – we can construe the meaning for this as – will it create a greatness which vasn’t there in front of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)! mahābhāratam karṇa parvam ślokam says “*vaśāyudair yasya guṇā na śakyā vaktum samētairapi sarvalōkai:*” (whose auspicious qualities cannot be spoken of even if all the people of the world join together for ten thousand years). Is it that all of his qualities cannot be spoken of this way \[by all the people speaking together for long periods\]? [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) says that it is not that way; even one of his qualities cannot be spoken of, in this way.

**pinnaikkā ēṟṟuyirai attān eḻil** – the fulsome beauty of one who killed seven bulls which were a hurdle for him in uniting with nappinnaip pirātti.

**puviyinār pōṟṟi uraikkap poliyumē** – is it possible to speak of the beautiful marriage that he had with nappinnai and of his single activity of killing the bulls for her sake?

We will move on to the 86<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 86. Elil konda

*ēḻil koṇḍa minnuk kodiyeduttu vēgat toḻil koṇḍu tān muḻangit tōnṟum eḻil koṇḍa nīr megamanna nedumāl niṟam pōlak kār vānam kāttum adu*

**Пословный перевод:**

eḻil koṇḍa min kodi eduttu – holding the beautiful lightning as flag; vēgam toḻil koṇḍu – keeping as its job, the act of moving fast; muḻangit tōnṟum – appearing as roaring; kār vānam – the sky during monsoon; eḻil koṇḍa – having beauty; nīrmēgam anna – appearing like the rainy cloud; nedumāl niṟam pōla kalandu kāttum – it will aptly display the divine complexion of emperumān

**Перевод:**

**eḻil koṇḍu minnuk kodi eduttu – having as his flag, the beautiful lightning.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that it is not possible to estimate emperumān’s characteristics, his divine form, his qualities etc. With the help of similes, we can compare to an extent. Even though his divine beauty cannot be experienced, it would be such that it cannot be given up. For someone who wishes to see the divine form of emperumān, clouds will show his form. There is a benefit of living too, in this world. āḻvār says that one can look at the clouds which are a simile to emperumān’s divine form. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vēgat toḻil koṇḍu tān muḻangit tōnṟum** – one which has its job the action of moving rapidly and which makes a loud noise

**eḻil koṇḍa nīr mēgamanna nedumāl niṟam pōla** – having as complexion the colour of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who appears to have drunk the monsoon clouds up to his neck \[such dark\]. Just as it is mentioned in nārāyaṇa sūktam “*neelatōyada madyastā vidyullēkēva bhāsvarā*” (shining like lightning which has drunk the blue coloured clouds).

**kārvānam kāttum kalandu** – the cloud, during monsoon time, will manifest the complexion of emperumān. Sky, which has the cloud which moves rapidly and which makes a roaring noise, will display [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s complexion.

**kalandu kāttum** – both the sky and cloud will show together. It is not that only the sky showed or that only the cloud showed. Both sky and cloud, which are blended together, will show the divine complexion of emperumān. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) says that if the sky has clouds which appear to have been released from a bow and which roar loudly, it will appear like the divine complexion of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).  In that the case, the term *eḻil koṇḍa* would be an adjective for lightning. If the text is *eḻil koṇḍu* *minnuk kodi eduttu* the meaning will change as – the monsoon clouds which are beautiful will have lightning as flag. Then, *eḻil koṇḍu* will be an adjective for monsoon clouds.

We will take up the 87<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 87. Kalandu mani

*kalandu maṇiyimaikkum kaṇṇā ninmēni malarndu maradagamē kāttum nalandigaḻum kondin vāy vaṇḍaṟaiyum taṇḍuyāyk kōmānai andivān kāttum adu*

**Пословный перевод:**

kaṇṇā – Oh kaṇṇapirān! (kriṣṇa); maṇi kalandu – being with kaustuba gemstone \[a type of gem worn by emperumān being representative of all ātmās\]; imaikkum – displaying; ninmēni – your divine form; maradagam – emerald gemstone; malarndu – having widespread radiance; kāttum – it shows; nalam tigaḻum – appearing beautiful; kondin vāy – in bunches; vaṇḍu aṟaiyum – with beetles humming; taṇ tuḻāy – donning tuḷasi garland; kōmānai – the supreme being, your divine form; andi vān adu kāttum – sky, in evening time, shows

**Перевод:**

**kalandu maṇi imaikkum kaṇṇā nin mēni malarndu maradagamē kāttum – Oh kaṇṇā! The radiance from the śrīkaustubamaṇi which is on your divine chest and the black light from śrīvatsam, a mole on your chest, combine and once they spread on your divine form, the emerald light from śrīkaustubam clearly shows your dark form (the radiance from the emerald gemstone will show the radiance from your divine form.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār once again gets down to experiencing the beauty of emperumān through a simile. The natural beauty of emperumān will be shown by a gemstone and the beauty which came about through decking up will be shown by the cloud in evening time sky. Let us go through the  pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nalam tigaḻum kondin vāy vaṇḍu aṟaiyum taṇ tuḻāyk kōmānai** – emperumān, who is donning the tuḷasi garland, in bunches of which honey is dripping, which is drunk by beetles and in the resultant happiness, the beetles will be humming.

**andi vānam kāttum adu** – the sky which has the reddish colour of evening time, will show the complexion of emperumān (evening sky, which has reddish colour, will show the colour of emperumān who is donning tuḷasi garland). We can construe the meaning as – Emerald stone will show the divine form of emperumān and the evening sky will show emperumān. Alternatively, we can say that both the emerald gemstone and evening sky will be seen similar to emperumān’s divine form.

We will move on to the 88<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 88. Adu nanru

*adu nanṟu idu tīdenṟu aiyappadādē madu ninṟa taṇ tuḻāy mārvan podu ninṟa ponnangaḻalē toḻumin muḻuvinaigaḷ munnangaḻalum mudindu*

**Пословный перевод:**

adu nanṟu idu tīdu enṟu aiyappadādē – instead of having doubts as to whether that is good or this is bad; madu ninṟa taṇ tuḻāy mārvan – emperumān who has on his divine chest, the cool tuḷasi garland full of nectar; such emperumān’s; podu ninṟa – common to all (which is attaining his divine feet); pon am kaḻalē – the desirable divine feet; tozumin – worship; munnam – ahead of doing that; muḻu vinaigal – all the sins; mudindu – losing their strength; kaḻalum – will leave you

**Перевод:**

**adu nanṟu idu tīdu enṟu aiyappadādē – Do not have doubts such as “being involved with emperumān’s matter is very good; getting involved with worldly matters is very bad” Just as matters other than emperumān make one to doubt the bad things, they will make one to doubt the good things too. If one were to analyse the good and bad among those things, only doubt will remain at the end \[without deciding one way or the other\]. In other words, even though the other matters are indeed bad, it would appear that they are good. It would even end with remaining that way.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that instead of analysing the lower limit and upper limit of worldly matters, attain the divine feet of emperumān who is like this. All your sorrows will disappear. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**madu ninṟa taṇ tuḻāy mārvan** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is the only entity about whom there can be no doubt. He remains with the tuḷasi garland, full of honey, announcing to the world that he is the leader.

**podu ninṟa ponnangaḻalē toḻumin** – he has such divine feet that everyone, without any distinction, will worship them. Hasn’t it been said in jitantē ślōkam 1-2 “*dēvānām dānavānām ca sāmānyam adidaivatam*” (emperumān is common to both celestial entities and to demons)! Did not vibhīśaṇa say as in śrī rāmāyaṇa yudda kāṇḍam 17-15 “*sarvalōka śaraṇyāya rāghavāya*” (to śrī rāma who is he refuge for the entire world)! Did not visbhīṣṇa tell the monkey warriors “śrī rāma’s nature is such that even rāvaṇa, the reason for which you people are not accepting me, could come and surrender to him!”? In the same way, did not śrī rāma, when he told sugrīva to bring vibhīśaṇa to him, tell him “*ānayainam ….. yadi vā rāvaṇam svayam*” (even if he is rāvaṇa, bring him)! Thus [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) says, try and worship the divine feet which have a common connection with everyone and which are beautiful.

**muḻu vinagaḷ munnam kaḻalum mudindu** – the sins, which are the hurdle for vorśipping [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and which have been accumulated from time immemorial, would disappear even before you think of vorśipping him.

**munnam kaḻalum** – even before vorśipping. They will disappear because of the firm thought in the mind that you should worship emperumān.

**mudindu** – instead of going to another place the sins will get destroyed there itself.

An alternative meaning for *adu nanṟu idu tīdu enṟy aiyappadādē* has been given at the end – instead of analysing whether the matter that has been experienced is good and the matter that is being experienced now is bad; instead of analysing the upper limit and lower limit of other matters.

We will take up the 89<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 89. Mudinda poludil

*mudinda poḻudil kuṟavāṇar ēnam padinduḻusāl paindinaigaḷ vitta taditteḻunda vēyngaḻaipōy viṇṭhiṟakkum vēngadamē mēlorunāḷ tīnguḻal vāyvaittān silambu*

**Пословный перевод:**

mudinda poḻudil kuṟavāṇar – the chieftains of hilly people, who are at the throes of death due to old age; ēnam padindu uḻu sāl – on the lands where wild boars (due to their arrogance) plough deeply (such that bamboos will get uprooted); pai tinigaḷ vitta – sowing new seeds \[on those lands\]; tadindu – even after they have been cut; eḻunda – rising aloft (due to the fertility of the soil); vēyngaḻai – bamboo sticks; pōy – rising up; viṇ tiṟakkum – reaching to the skies; vēngadam – tiruvēngadam; tīm kuḻal – the sweet flute; vāy vaittān – kaṇṇapirān (kriṣṇa) who kept \[that flute\] on his divine lips; silambu – his divine hill

**Перевод:**

**mudinda pōdil kuṟavāṇar – the chieftains among hilly people who have reached their last stage. The old hilly people, of whom others will say “he will die now; he will die tomorrow” etc.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār wonders whether it isn’t at tirumalai that one can see and experience emperumān who is attainable by everyone like this! This pāsuram says that everything about tirumalai – the hilly people there, the bamboos, tiruvēngadamudaiyān who has taken residence – is desirable for him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ēnam padindu uḻusāl paindinaigaḷ vitta** – since the old hilly people cannot plough the land, they will plough the land with the help of wild boars and sow beautiful millet seeds on the lands thus ploughed. Even if they are not capable of ploughing the lands, they cannot remain without carrying out their hereditary work.

**tadindu eḻunda vēyngaḻai pōy viṇ tiṟakkum vēngadamē** – while they have sown the seeds the previous day at dawn, they will go to the forest next day dawn to cut the bamboos. Those bamboos would have grown rapidly and risen tall, piercing the sky.

**vēyngaḻai pōy viṇ tiṟakkum** – while they would have sown something (millet seeds) something else would have grown (bamboo).

Which is this place?

**mēl oru nāḷ tīnguḻal vāy vaittān silambu** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who kept the beautiful bamboo flute on  his divine lips and played on it to captivate all the living creatures in tiruvāyppādi (śrī gōkulam) so that they all become his. tirumalai is the residence of that tirumāḷ in order to captivate all the people of the world.

We could rearrange the verse in this way – *mudinda poḻudil tīnguḻal vāy vaittān* – the meaning for this will be: isn’t it at dusk, when the day time comes to an end, that kaṇṇan plays on the flute? tirumalai is the apt hill for such [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

We will take up the 90<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 90. Silambum serikalalum

*silambum seṟikaḻalum senṟisaippa viṇṇāṟ alambiya sēvadipōy aṇḍam pulambiya tōḷ eṇḍhisaiyum sūḻ idam pōdādu enkolō vaṇḍuḻāy māl aḷanda maṇ*

**Пословный перевод:**

silambum – decorative anklets (which have been applied on the divine feet); seṟi kaḻalum – hollow anklets which have been applied closely; senṟu isaippa – making noise everywhere; viṇ āṟu – ākāśa gangai (river gangā in the ethereal layer); alambiya – cleaned; sē adi – the divine feet; aṇḍam pōy – when they extended until the walls of the universe; pulambiya – fit to be vorśipped (by everyone); tōḷ – divine shoulders; eṇ disaiyum sūḻ – to permeate all eight directions; idam pōdādu – there was no place; vaṇ tuḻāy māl – emperumān who is donning the beautiful tuḷasi garland; aḷanda – the one that he measured; maṇ – the earth; en kol – how big is it! \[it appears that it is only small\]

**Перевод:**

**silambum seṟi kaḻalum senṟu isaippa – in such a way that the divine anklets and the closely worn hollow anklets make noise**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*When the bamboo growing at tirumalai itself has become desirable, is it necessary to say that emperumān who is residing there, is desirable? āḻvār mentions the way by which emperumān protects his followers. He says “Won’t you realise that there is no space for him to measure?”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**viṇ āru alambiya sēvadi pōy** – the divine feet which have been cleaned by ākāśa gangā, reach out to. We can also construe the meaning as – the divine feet which have been touched by ākāśa gangā, reach out to.

**sēvadi pōy aṇḍam senṟisaippa** – reaching until the walls of the universe

**pulambiya tōḷ eṇḍhisaiyum sūḻa** – the divine shoulders which are praised by all the worlds “How beautiful these shoulders are”, permeating in the eight directions. Just as mentioned in viṣṇu darmam – māngalya stōtram “*sangais surāṇām divi bhūtalastais tadā manuśyair gaganē ca kēcarai: stuta: kramān ya: pracakāra sarvadā mamāstu māngaḷyavruddayē hari:*” (let that hari who is vorśipped by the dēvas (celestial entities) in svarga (heaven), by the manuśyas (humans) on the earth and those who wander around in the skies and who graced with his divine feet, bless me for my growth), he is vorśipped by celestial entities in large gatherings.

**vaṇḍuḻāy māl aḷanda maṇ idam pōdādu enkolō** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is decorated with tuḷasi garland. The emperumān whose tuḷasi garland gave out his wealth. Since there was no further space to measure, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) is wondering as to how he measured. He is amazed that while there was no space to stand and measure, how did emperumān measure. One should have arranged for the space to be measured. Without doing it, how did he measure it, he is wondering.

We will move on to the 91<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 91. Mannundum

*maṇṇuṇḍum pēycci mulaiuṇḍum āṟṟādāy veṇṇey viḻunga veguṇḍu āycci kaṇṇik kayiṟṟināl kattat tān kattuṇḍirundān vayiṟṟinōdāṟṟā magan*

**Пословный перевод:**

vayiṟṟinōdāṟṟā magan – the child (kaṇṇan; kriṣṇa) who cannot keep quiet with his stomach; maṇ uṇḍu – swallowing all the worlds; pēycci – the demon pūtanā’s; mulai – (poisoned) bosom; uṇḍum – drinking that; āṟṟādāy – not being satisfied; veṇṇey – butter; viḻunga – as he swallowed; āycci – yaśōdāp pirātti; veguṇḍu – getting angry; kaṇṇi kayiṟṟināl – with a rope having many knots; katta – tying securely; tān – he, the omnipotent supreme being; kattuṇḍu irundān – remained tied (instead of trying a way to get out of it)

**Перевод:**

**maṇ uṇḍum pēycci mulai uṇḍum – eating generally, the world and lives of enemies. He did not get satisfied until he ate things which were touched by his favourable ones.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In this pāsuram, āḻvār mercifully mentions the characteristic of emperumān by which he is devoted to his followers. He says that just as one cannot see a limit to his characteristics, one cannot see the limit for his narratives too. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**maṇ uṇḍum pēycci mulai uṇḍum āṟṟādāy** – when deluge came to take away everything, he kept all the worlds inside his stomach to protect them. On top of that, as if he were going to drink milk from the demon pūtanā’s bosom, he drank her life too along with milk. Not being satisfied with eating these,

**venney viḻunga** – in order to get rid of all the faults that he had, he stole butter and ate it.

**veguṇḍu āycci** – Once the butter was stolen, yaśōdāp pirātti, looking at the fear in kaṇṇan’s face, concluded that only he had eaten the butter and was furious (she was furious because if he ate so much of butter it would do harm to his physical form). She had as much anger towards kaṇṇan as she had affection for him. yaśōdā had as much enthusiasm in protecting kaṇṇan as he had in stealing butter.

**veṇṇey viḻunga** – his acts of eating the worlds etc were to remove the concern of those entities while his eating butter was to remove his concern (he could not sustain himself without eating butter). Just as [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s stomach is for the worlds, so is emperumān’s stomach for butter.

**kaṇṇik kayiṟṟināl kattat tān kattuṇḍirundān** – while yaśōdā had tied him with a rope which had many knots, he remained tied as if he could not liberate himself from that.

**kattuṇḍu** – one who controls everyone, one who is the reason for binding everyone to samsāram (materialistic realm),  stabilising them in samsāram and releasing them, was tied by a woman and stood being unable to untie himself and leave. One who binds everyone through his *sankalpa* (vow / will) stood bound. Other [āḻvārs](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) too have enjoyed this narrative, as in periya tirumoḻi 8-3-5 “*tāyedutta siṟukōlukku uḷaindōdi*”, [tiruvāimoḻi 5-10-3](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/09/thiruvaimozhi-5-10-3-peyyum-punguzhal/) “*neyyuṇ vārttaiyuḷ annai kōl koḷḷa nīyun tāmaraikkaṇgaḷ nīr malgap paiyavē nilaiyum*”, perumāḷ tirumoḻi 7-8 “*muḻudum veṇṇey aḷaindu tottuṇṇum mugiḻiḷam siṟuttāmaraik kaiyum …*”.

**vayiṟṟinōdu āṟṟā magan** – he could not keep quiet with his stomach. He could tolerate hunger only if he grew beyond adolescence. Since he was a little child, and could not tolerate his hunger, he stole butter and ate. [piḷḷai uṟangā villi dāsar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/pillai-urangavilli-dhasar/) (one of the disciples of [bhagavat śrī rāmānujar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/emperumanar/)), on hearing this pāsuram, said “could she not tear off his stomach and give it to the vaśerman?”. He had perhaps mercifully stated this since his stomach could not get satisfied even after eating everything just as the vaśerman’s vessel would not get filled up even after throwing all the clothes into it!

We will move on to the 92<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 92. Magan oruvarkku

*magan oruvarkkallāda māmēni māyan maganām avan magan tan kādal maganai siṟai seyda vāṇan tōḷ seṟṟān kaḻalē niṟai seydu en nenjē ninai*

**Пословный перевод:**

en nenjē – Oh my mind!; oruvarkku magan allāda – not having to be born as a son to anyone (on the basis of his karma, deeds); māmēni – having a great divine form; māyan – kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) with amazing activities; magan Am – he was born as a son (to vasudēvar and dēvaki); avan magan than – his son pradyumna’s; kādal maganai – favourite son aniruddāḻvān (anirudda); siṟai seyda – one who imprisoned \[anirudda\]; vāṇan – bāṇāsura’s; tōḷ – thousand shoulders; seṟṟān – one who severed; kaḻalē – the divine feet alone; niṟai seydu – being complete; ninai – think of

**Перевод:**

**magan oruvarkku allāda mā mēni māyan – one who manifests in his own divine form that he is not born as a son to anyone and one who has amazing power.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār tells his heart to think fully of emperumān who is capable of destroying enemies of followers. Instead of thinking of him as one who has been imprisoned, think of him as one who is capable of severing our imprisonment. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**magan oruvarkku allāda** – one who is totally independent. He is “*avyaya: pitā*” (indestructible father). He is a father who will never die.

**mā mēni** – he has a divine form fit to be vorśipped.

**māyan** – he has amazing qualities such as knowledge, strength etc.

**maganām** – How amazing! One, who is a father to everyone, incarnates as a son. Has it not been mentioned in taittirīya upaniśat yajur 3-12-9 “*pitā putrēṇa pitrumān yōni yōnau*” (he is a father to all the world. He selects one as his father and incarnates in each birth, as that person’s son)! Since he does not have the necessity for being a son, this is something which he gets that he would otherwise not have got. For anyone, desire is created in something which he does not have. In the same way, for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), desire is created in being a son. He, who is father to everyone, in order to save what he has created, assumes the role of a son, and begets a son. There too, in whichever birth they become his sons, he also becomes a son in those births. If emperumān, who does not have a birth, voluntarily takes up a birth, will he not carry out deeds till the limits of the birth! bāṇāsura (a demon) imprisoned anirudda, who is the son of pradyuna, who in turn is kriṣṇa’s son. Wouldn’t affection be more towards grandson than son! Such bāṇāsura, who imprisoned the grandson anirudda . . . . .

**vāṇan tōḷ seṟṟān** – he destroyed the forest of bāṇāsura’s shoulders \[the demon had a thousand shoulders and hence they looked like a forest of shoulders\].

**seṟṟān kaḻalē** – the divine feet of one who severed the shoulders of bāṇāsura so that he could never imprison anyone.

**niṟai seydu in nenjē ninai** – Oh my heart! Try to think of him fully. Even as he removes your enemies, you enjoy.

We will take up the 93<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 93. Ninaittulagilar

*ninaittulagilār teḷivār nīṇḍa tirumāḷ anaittulagum uḷḷodukki ālmēl kanaittulavu veḷḷattōr piḷḷaiyāy mellat tuyinṟānai uḷḷattē vai nenjē uyttu*

**Пословный перевод:**

nenjē – Oh heart!; nīṇḍa – one who grew (to measure the worlds); tirumāḷ – consort of śrī mahālakśmi; kanaittu ulavu veḷḷattu – in the deluge when spread all over, making huge noise; ōr piḷḷaiyāy – as a unique infant; ananittu ulagum – all the worlds; uḷ odukki – keeping them inside his stomach; Al mēl – on top of a tender banyan leaf; meḷḷat tuyinṟānai – emperumān who reclined slowly on it; ulagil – in this world; ninaittu – meditating on him (as the refuge); Ar teḷivār – Is there anyone who gets clarity? (there is none); uyttu – bringing him (at least you) \[āḻvār tells his heart\]; uḷḷattu vai – keep him inside

**Перевод:**

**ninaittu ulagil Ar teḷivār –  there is no one in the world who thinks that emperumān will be a refuge for us and remains without any bewilderment. Is there anyone who will attain his true state \[being emperumān’s servitor\] after thinking about him?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that there is none in the world who would attain such an emperumān who has amazing activities, knowing him truly. He tells his heart to keep him,  within itself. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nīṇḍa tirumāḷ** – one who is immeasurable. Being the consort of [śrī mahālakśmi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/), one who has everyone, other than himself, as his servitors. āḻvār, in order to mention about his immeasurable nature, talks about all his greatness and says that he is like this.

**anaittulagum uḷḷodukki Al mēl kanaittu ulavu veḷḷattu ōr piḷḷaiyāy meḷḷat tuyinṟānai** – one who reclined slowly on a just sprouted banyan leaf, after swallowing all the worlds and keeping them in a corner of his stomach, as a unique child, without disturbing anyone of those whom he swallowed, when water was flowing without any hurdle, during the time of deluge.

**uḷḷaittē vai nenjē uyttu** – bring him inside, oh heart, and keep such an entity who has all these wealth! Bringing him inside is nothing but not stopping him when he enters the mind.

We will move on to the 94<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.
