## 94. Uyttunarvennum

*uyttuṇarvennum oḷi koḷ viḷakkēṟṟi vaittavanai nādi valaippaduttēn mettenavē ninṟān irundān kidandān en nenjattup ponṟamai māyan pugundu*

**Пословный перевод:**

uṇarvu ennum – knowledge which is readily available; oḷi koḷ viḷakku – radiant lamp; ēṟṟi – lighting it; avanai uyttu vaittu – searching for emperumān; nādi – analysing and meditating; valaippaduttēn – I made him my own; māyan – that emperumān; ponṟāmai – without any break; en nenjattu – inside my heart; pugundu – entered; mettena – slowly; ninṟān – (initially) stood; irundān – (later) sat; kidandān – (after that) reclined and graced me

**Перевод:**

**uyttu – bringing in**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār reminisces in this pāsuram about all the benefits that he obtained starting from the first pāsuram vaiyam tagaḷiyā. In the previous pāsuram he had asked his heart to retain emperumān. In this pāsuram he mercifully says that as soon as he told his heart to think \[about emperumān\], emperumān, with affection, entered his heart and how he became very affectionate towards him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**uṇarvennum oḷi viḷakku ēṟṟi** – instead of the regular lamps which have faults such as oil, wick etc, lighting the lamp of knowledge.

**vaittu avainai nādi valaippaduttēn** – bringing him inside, analysing his characteristics, meditating on those qualities, I enticed him inside the net of being a favourable entity to him. Can being favourable to him be termed as a net? Has it not been said “*bhaktikrīta:*” (he is bought for the price of bhakti)! The activity carried out by [cētana](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) is the net which ensnares him.

**mettenavē ninṟān irundān kidandān en nenjattu** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) too was willing to be ensnared by the net. He entered my heart, slowly stabilised himself by standing on a leg, and then carried out further activities of standing firmly, sitting and reclining in my heart.

**ponṟāmal** – this could mean any of (1) such that I was not destroyed (2) standing, sitting and reclining in my heart without any gap (3) continuously.

**māyan pugundu** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is an amazing entity.

The sequence in which the pāsuram should be considered is: *pugundu uyttu vaittu uṇarvennum oḷi koḷ viḷakkēṟṟi avanai nādi valaippaduttēn; māyan pugundu ponṟāmal mettenavē ninṟān irundān kidandānen nenjattu*.

We will move on to the 95<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 95. Pugundu ilangum

*pugundu ilangum andip poḻudattu ariyāy igaḻnda iraṇiayadāgam sugirndengum sindappiḷanda tirumāḷ tiruvadiyē vandittu en nenjamē vāḻttu*

**Пословный перевод:**

andi pugundu – with dusk setting; ilangum poḻudattu – at the time when it manifests; ari āy – incarnating in the form of narasimha (lion head and human body); igaḻnda iraṇiyadu āgam – the body of demon iraṇiyan (hiraṇya kaśyap) who was constantly abusing emperumān; engum sinda – dropping it all over the place; piḷanda – one who tore; tirumāl – emperumān’s; tiruvadiyē – only divine feet; en nenjamē – Oh my heart!; vandittu vāyāra vāḻttu – bow the head and praise him fully

**Перевод:**

**pugundu ilangum andip poḻudattu – āḻvār mentions about the setting in of dusk. Since sun sets at that time, it will be reddish in colour.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Thinking of the simplicity that emperumān displayed towards him, āḻvār mercifully mentions in this pāsuram as to how emperumān displayed that quality towards prahlāda. He tells his heart to praise and worship at the divine feet of such emperumān who carries out favours like this. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**andip poḻudattu** – during evening time, the strength of demons will increase many fold. iraṇiyan had taken a boon that he should not be killed during day time or night time. He did not take a boon for not being killed at dusk. Hence [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) chose that time to kill him.

**ariyāy** – iraṇiyan had taken a boon that he should not be killed by *dēvas* (celestial entities) or *manuśyas* (humans) . Hence emperumān took the form of *narasimha*, a form that iraṇiyan did not think had the capability to kill him and hence did not ask a boon for. If we rearrange the words of the verse to get *andippoḻudattu pugundu ilangum ariyāy* the meaning would change to – entering during dusk in the form of a radiant narasimha.

**igaḻnda iraṇiyadu āgam** – the body of iraṇiyan, who constantly was abusing [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) as well as prahlāda,  which had grown out of the strength from boons granted by celestial entities.

**sugirndu engum sindap piḷanda** – apart from killing iraṇiyan who is the enemy of follower prahlāda, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) also mentions the fury that emperumān had when he killed iraṇiyan.

**sugirndu** – cutting into many pieces

**engum sindap piḷanda** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) tore iraṇiyan’s body and strew them all over the place.

**tirumāl** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is together with [pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) who is the reason for his carrying out beneficial activities for his followers. The reason for his having partiality towards his followers is pirātti. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) says this so that we praise her along with praising emperumān.

**tiruvadiyē** – the divine feet of emperumān who is the consort of pirātti.

**vandittu** – bowing down to him

**en nenjamē** – Oh my heart which feels happy when thinking of him!

**vāḻttu** – praise him. Bowing down to and praising are to be carried out to him who is together with *tirumagaḷ* ([pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/)). [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) says that the enemies of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s followers who desire the union of the divine couple, will suffer the fate of irāniyan.

We will take up the 96<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 96. Valttiya vayaray

*vāḻttiya vāyarāy vānōr maṇimagudam tāḻtti vaṇangat taḻumbāmē kēḻtta adittāmarai malarmēl mangai maṇāḷan adittāmariyām alar*

**Пословный перевод:**

kēḻtta adi – having huge stem; mangai – periya pirātti’s; maṇāḷan – consort emperumān’s; adi Am tāmarai alar – divine feet which are like lotus flowers; vānōr – nityasūris \[permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam\]; vāḻttiyvāyarāy – praising with their mouths; maṇi magudam – crowns studded with gemstones; tāḻtti – lowering; vaṇanga – since they worship; taḻumbu āmē – (those divine feet) have got scarred

**Перевод:**

**vāḻttiya vāyarāy – nityasūris who have as their nature, praising emperumān constantly. They have mouths which constantly keep singing hymns. Their mouths would have got scarred due to constant praising.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says in this pāsuram that emperumān has such a soft nature that he cannot tolerate the conduct \[vorśipping constantly\] of nityasūris too. Alternatively, it can be construed that āḻvār says that even people with high levels of knowledge such as brahmā et al worship at his divine feet and attain what they desire. tirumaḻisai āḻvār too, in his nānmugan tiruvandādi 11 has mercifully stated “māmalarān vārsadaiyān vallarē allarē vāḻttu” (brahmā, born in a lotus flower, and Siva, who has matted hair, are not capable of praising emperumān). Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vānōr maṇimagudam tāḻtti vaṇagat taḻumbāmē** – since [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine feet are very soft, they cannot tolerate the constant bowing down of nityasūris on themselves, and would get scarred. *dēvatās* (celestial deities) such as brahmā et al, bow down to emperumān’s divine feet with their opulent crowns, due to which the divine feet would get scarred.

**kēḻtta adittāmarai mēl** – lotus flower which has rounded, huge stalk. This implies that even if periya pirātti sits atop the flower, it will bear her. We can construe this as lotus flower having a scent and beauty.

**tāmari malar mēl mangai** – periya pirātti who dwells on such lotus flower and who is always in a youthful state.

**maṇāḷan** – one who enjoys her beauty.

**māṇāḷan adittāmarai** – the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) are so soft that even if periya pirātti wishes to touch them with her very soft hands , she will hesitate to do so. This implies that it is the union of  pirātti and emperumān that nityasūris as well as entities such as brahmā et al  consider fit to be vorśipped.

**adittāmaraiyāmalar** – the divine lotus like feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) would acquire a scar.

We will take up the 97<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 97. Alar edutta undiyan

*alaredutta undiyān āngeḻilāya malaredutta māmēni māyan alaredutta vaṇṇattān māmalrān vārsadaiyān enṟivargatku eṇṇattānāmō imai*

**Пословный перевод:**

alar edutta undiyān – one who has a divine navel which has a highly rising lotus flower; eḻilāya malar edutta – like a beautiful kāyāmpū (a dark blue coloured) flower; māmēni – having a dark divine form; māyan – emperumān who has amazing activities; alar edutta vaṇṇattān – indra (head of celestial entities), who has the complexion of kānci flower; mā malarān – brahmā, who was born on a lotus; vār sadaiyān – Siva, who has lowly matter hair; enṟu ivargatku – for all these celestial entities; imai – even a little bit; eṇṇattān āmō – can he be thought of?

**Перевод:**

**alar edutta undiyān – one who has a divine navel which displays a flower. One who has the lotus flower which is the cause for the worlds, on his navel. īsn’t that lotus the reason for the creation of worlds?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Unless emperumān, who is the consort of periya pirātti and head of the indefatigable nityasūris, with his mercy grants it, even for entities such as brahmā et al, it is impossible to attain him with their own efforts. āḻvār says that emperumān, who is vorśipped by nityasūris, is beyond the reach of brahmā et al.- Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**āngu** – with him

**eḻilāya malar edutta māmēni māyan** – he is one with amazing activities, whose divine form displays a beautiful flower. He is one with amazing activities, who has a dark complexion like that of kāyāmpū (a type of flower). He has a divine form which has the complexion and softness of a flower.

With that amazing entity…..

**alar edutta vaṇṇattān** – indra who has the complexion of a beautiful flower \[kānci\]

**māmalarān** – nānmugan (brahmā) who was born in the divine lotus

**vār sadaiyān** – Siva who has a form with lowly matted hair, as if he wants to accomplish something

**enṟu ivargatku eṇṇattān āmō** – is it possible for these entities to even think of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)? [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) says that these people \[indra, brahmā, Siva et al\] cannot even think of the characteristics of emperumān which have been mentioned earlier.

**imai** – would mean enquire or a little bit.

We will take up the 98<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 98. Imam sul malaiyum

*imam sūḻ malaiyum iru visumbum kāṟṟum amam sūḻndaṟa viḷangittōnṟum namam sūḻ naragattu nammai naṇugāmal kāppān turagattai vāy piḷandān tottu*

**Пословный перевод:**

imam sūḻ malaiyum – the himālaya mountain which is covered with snow; iru visumbum – the expansive sky; kāṟṟum – vāyu (wind); amam sūḻndu – (due to robust build, height and speed) lowering and pervading; aṟa viḷangit tōnṟum – coming in front, clearly; turagattai – the demon kēśi who came in the form of a horse; tottu – touching him (with his divine hands); vāy piḷandān – emperumān who tore (his) mouth; nammai – us; naman sūḻ naragattu naṇugāmal kāppān – he will protect us from entering hell which has yama as its Lord

**Перевод:**

**imam sūḻ malaiyum – in the (himālaya) mountain covered with snow**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that emperumān gets rid of the enemies of those who attain him. He says that he removes our hurdles and gets rid of samsāram (life in this materialistic world). Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**iru visumbum** – in the expansive sky

**kāṟṟum** – in the wind

**amam sūḻndu** – permeating in a widespread way

**aṟa viḷangit tōnṟum** – permeating all these, due to his incarnations, the radiant [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) too has mercifully stated in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-3-2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruvaimozhi-1-3-2-elivarum-iyalvinan/) “*pala piṟappāy oḷi varu muḻu nalam*” that emperumān gains radiance due to his several incarnations. By mentioning mountain, sky, wind etc, emperumān’s *jagatākārata* (having the entire world as his physical form) has been indicated. Alternatively, if these words are taken as adjectives for the horse kēśi, a form taken by a demon, we can construe the meanings as – the horse which came with these characteristics

**vāy piḷandāṇ tottu** – since kēśi is an enemy for his followers, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) due to that rage, with his own hands, caught him, tore his mouth and killed him.

**naman sūḻ naragattu** – in the naragam (hell) which is surrounded by yama. We can consider the meaning for this as – the hell that we have surrounded ourselves with. ōr, we can consider this as – this world (materialistic realm itself is hell) which is under the control of yama.

**nammai naṇugāmal kāppān** – he will protect us from approaching hell. He will prevent us from entering hell. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/peyazhwar/) says that the sufferings which the horse (kēśi) underwent at the hands of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) are similar to the sufferings in the hell.

We will take up the 99<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 99. Totta padai ettum

*totta padai ettum tōlāda venṟiyān attapuyakarattān annānṟu kuttattuk kōḷ mudalai tunja kuṟitteṟinda cakkarttān tāḷ mudalē nangatkuc cārvu*

**Пословный перевод:**

totta – carrying in the hand; padai ettum – the eight divine weapons; tōlāda – without losing; venṟiyān – one who always wins; attabuyakarattān – one who dwells in the divine abode attabuyakaram in kāncīpuram; annānṟu – once upon a time; kuttattu – in the water reservoir; kōḷ mudalai tunja – the powerful crocodile to be killed; kuṟittu eṟinda cakkarattān – one who aimed the divine discus without losing the target, his; tāḷ mudalē nangatkuc cārvu – divine feet alone are our refuge

**Перевод:**

**totta padai ettum tōlāda venṟiyān – once he holds the divine weapons in his hand, he has the ability to win at every place, without losing anywhere. āḻvār says that he is the real great warrior who, holding his eight weapons (arrows, bow, mace, conch, sword, disc, shield and knob) is always victorious. āḻvār says that if we have to get rid of our fears, should we not attain him?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that it is only the divine feet of emperumān, who removes the enemies of followers, which are to be attained for us. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**attabuyakarattān** – one who resides in the divine abode attabuyakaram, in kāncīpuram. He holds the eight divine weapons on his eight divine shoulders and has taken residence there.

**kuttattuk kōḷ mudalai tunja kuṟitteṟinda** – one who demonstrated by his action that he will get rid of our enemies and protect us.

**annānṟu** – during that period when gajēndrāḻvān (the elephant gajēndran) was in danger

**kuttattuk kōḷ mudalai tunja** – destroying the crocodile which was waiting in the pond, to kill gajēndran

**kōḷ mudalai** – the powerful crocodile which pulled gajēndran such that only its \[elephant’s\] mouth was above the level of water.

**kuṟitteṟinda** – the disc was thrown (by [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) at the target such that it would tear apart the crocodile without hurting the divine feet of gajēṇḍhran inside the crocodile’s mouth. Instead of getting bewildered in the anxiety to protect gajēndran, thrown at the target correctly.

**cakkarattān** – one who has the divine disc.

**tāḷ mudalē nangatkuc cārvu** – his divine feet alone are our refuge. Just as āḷavandār had mercifully mentioned in [stōtra ratnam 22](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/01/sthothra-rathnam-22/) “*tvat pāda mūlam śaraṇam prapadyē*” (I surrender at your divine feet), only the divine feet of one who helped śrī gajēndrāḻvān are auspicious and fit to be attained.

**mudalē** – our assistance is not needed for us. Our assistance is something which will not only be of no benefit to us, they will also become inimical to us. If we are able to extricate ourselves from the role of protecting ourselves, that alone will become the reason for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) to shower his grace on us. The destruction of one (our activity) will become the reason for another (emperumān’s grace) to be born. Independence and subservience cannot coexist in the same person. Could one kingdom have two kings?

**nangatku** – for those who do not have strength on their own, emperumān alone is the refuge.

We will move on to the last pāsuram of this tiruvandādi, next.

## 100. Sarvu namakkenrum

*sārvu namakkenṟum cakkarattān tanduḻāyt tārvāḻ varaimārvan tān muyangum kārārnda vānamaruminnimaikkum vaṇḍāmarai nedungaṇ tēnamarum pūmēl tiru*

**Пословный перевод:**

cakkarattān – one who has sudarśanam \[divine disc\] in his divine hand; taṇ tuḻāy tār vāḻ – one who has the cool tuḷasi garland; varai mārvan tān – by emperumān, who has mountain-like chest; muyangum – one who is together with him; kār ārnda vān – in the sky full of dark clouds; amarum – one which is established well; min – like lightning; imaikkum – being resplendent; vaṇ tāmarai – like a beautiful lotus; nedu – wide; kaṇ – having divine eyes; tēn amarum pū mēl – one who lives atop a lotus which is full of honey; tiru – periya pirāttiyār (śrī mahālakśmi); namakku enṟum sārvu – is our refuge for all times

**Перевод:**

**sārvu namakku enṟum – when we are in danger, our refuge is emperumān. Whether we are in danger or not, the refuge always is periya pirāttiyār. Just as it is mentioned in śrī bhagavat gītā 6-5 “ātmaivaripur ātmana:” (for ātmā, mind is enemy), when we cannot be refuge to ourselves, emperumān remains as our refuge. When he too, out of his total independence, is angry towards us, only periya pirāttiyār is our refuge. The difference between emperumān and periya pirātti is the same as the difference between us and emperumān. We only keep looking for whatever is advantageous to us. emperumān is one who gets very angry with us, looking at our sins. Has it not been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 27 – 45 “rāgavāddi bhayam gōram” (due to the severe fear towards śrī rāma)! But, if he is angry, it is pirātti who lowers it. Since she is his consort, he cannot but do what she likes.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that emperumān will be the refuge only for those who attain periya pirāttiyār’s divine feet as the refuge. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**cakkarattān** – the supreme being who has cakkaram (divine disc). He has the divine disc as a symbol of protection. nammāḻvār too has mercifully said in periya tiruvandādi 87 “*eppoḻudum kai kaḻalā nēmiyān nam mēl vinai kadivān*” (one from whose hand the divine disc will never disappear, will remove our sins).

**tanduḻāyt tārvāḻ varai mārban** – one who has the divine tuḷasi garland on his divine shoulders.

**tanduḻāyt tārvāḻ varai marban tān** – donning the divine tuḷasi garland as the symbol of being the head of all wealth. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) himself, who has mountain-like chest.

**tān muyangum** – one who is donning the divine tuḷasi garland as the symbol of sweetness, will himself embrace.  m*uyangudal* – embracing. In other words, he will be happy with pirātti. She will offer herself to him. emperumān who is like this, will make her \[say\] “*agalagillēn*” (inseparable) from him. He will say that he feels *agalagillēn* from her. The reason for a mother being very affectionate towards her husband is the cause of good things happening to the child. The simile for this is given ahead.

**kārārnda vānamarum minnimaikkum** – just as lightning flashes across the sky with dark clouds, \[śrī mahālakśmi is\] shining with a complexion contrary to the dark colour \[of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\].

**vaṇḍāmarai nedungaṇ** – one who has wide lotus-like eyes which look at followers with affection and magnanimity. śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-9-105 says “*paśyatām sarvadēvānām yayau vakśastalam harē:* ” (even as the celestial entities were watching, tirumagaḷ (śrī mahālakśmi) attained the divine chest of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)). That she did not bother about the celestial entities watching is indicated here by the 6<sup>th</sup> case. Just as mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-9-106 “*tayā vilōkitā dēvā: harivakśastalastayā lakśmyā maitrēya sahasā parām nirvrutimāgatā:*” (the celestial entities who were graced by lakshmi dēvi from the divine chest of hari (emperumān) were very joyous), residing in the divine chest of emperumān, pirātti graces the cētanas who travel far and wide in search of wealth so that the swelling in their soles would disappear.

**tēnamarum pūmēl tiru** – [periya pirāttiyār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/), who is sitting atop a lotus which is full of honey, is our refuge at all times.

**tēnamarum pūmēl tiru** – periya pirāttiyār who has unlimited sweetness, is our refuge.

This completes the translation of mūnṟām tiruvandādi.
